DONE (local, not yet pushed): paper16 strengthened again. It now proves thm:singlearc by Kac--Rice (sharp Gaussian t/sqrt(m) single-arc small ball away from z=±1), lem:kr-regression-lower (explicit Schur-complement normal-velocity regression lower bound away from the parity points), prop:away-firstmoment (matching away-from-endpoint first moment for Gaussian level crossings), prop:bulk-kr-constant (fixed-bulk down-crossing density \(t\sqrt N/\sqrt{12\pi}\) per unit arclength), cor:bulk-continuum-intensity (at \(t=\tau/\sqrt N\), fixed-bulk down-crossings converge to \((2\pi-4\delta)\tau/\sqrt{12\pi}\), with all-crossings doubled), cor:bulk-prob-upper (fixed-bulk continuum-scale first-moment probability upper bound with doubled all-crossing constant), prop:endpoint-singlearc by endpoint Kac--Rice, prop:endpoint-lower showing the endpoint arc scale is sharp, cor:endpoint-two-point giving the exact two-endpoint Gaussian formula \(2p_N(t)-q_N(t)\) with covariance \(\rho_N=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(-1)^j\in\{0,1\}\), hence the sharp second-order expansion P{min(|G_m(1)|,|G_m(-1)|)<=t}=2 sqrt(2/pi) t/sqrt(N)-(2/pi)t^2/sqrt(N^2-rho_N^2)+O(t^3/N^{3/2}), cor:endpoint-continuum-negligible showing that at \(t=\tau/\sqrt N\) the two endpoint probability is \(2\sqrt{2/\pi}\tau/N+O_\tau(N^{-2})\), cor:endpoint-neighborhood-continuum showing that every fixed natural endpoint neighborhood has probability \(O_{C_0}(\tau/N)\) at the same scale, cor:endpoint-global-floor making the global endpoint floor explicit, and thm:uniform giving the sharp Gaussian uniform boundary law P{min_T |G_m|<=t} <= C sqrt(m) t. prop:uniform-polylog is now a corollary with no log loss. It also proves bounded-density fixed-point/deterministic-grid anti-concentration, speed-conditional bounded-density local arc/endpoint bounds, cor:bd-uniform-speed (uniform bounded-density boundary bound under a deterministic speed cutoff), cor:bd-speedtail-to-boundary (polynomial speed tail at sqrt(log m) scale implies hyp:boundary-law, with honest law/speed-tail constant dependence), cor:bd-subexp-boundary (subexponential bounded-density laws verify the speed-tail input, hence subgaussian laws as a special case), and prop:rademacher-endpoint (translated endpoint lattice anti-concentration for Rademacher coefficients). hyp:clearance is only the circle-clearance estimate for the coefficient law; prop:fs-uniform gives a deterministic threshold N_0(m,eta) under a clearance floor and the event intersects {nu=r} at additive o(1) cost. Therefore Gaussian balanced conditioning is unconditional on the zero-winding event by KMS clearance, while every bounded-density variance-one law satisfying the same circle-clearance estimate gets the balanced-conditioning conclusion with no tail assumption. Remaining paper16 targets: sharp constants/global two-sided boundary minimum law beyond the fixed-bulk first-moment constants, upper bound, and endpoint floor; anti-clustering/decorrelation for a matching global lower-tail law; continuum non-Gaussian bounded-density universality at full generality without exponential-tail/speed-cutoff assumptions; and discrete analogues beyond the endpoint lattice scale.

CURRENT paper16 global upper/sandwich addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-prob-upper combines the fixed-bulk down-crossing probability upper bound with a cover of the fixed-width endpoint neighborhoods outside the natural O(1/m) endpoint arcs. The single-arc theorem gives O(delta tau) for the fixed-width endpoint remainder, cor:endpoint-neighborhood-continuum gives o(1) for the natural endpoint arcs, and letting delta->0 yields the sharp whole-circle continuum upper bound limsup_N P{min_T |G_m| <= tau/sqrt(N)} <= sqrt(pi/3) tau, uniformly for tau in compact sets. The remaining Gaussian lower-tail gap is now exactly the uniform anti-clustering/decorrelation estimate feeding that factorial-moment hypothesis, plus the stronger exponential minimum-modulus law.

CURRENT paper16 finite-confidence concentration addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:variance-speed-confidence, cor:variance-boundary-confidence, and cor:variance-grid-confidence turn the variance-only derivative cutoff into direct eta-level concentration certificates. For independent centered unit-variance coefficients, P{sup_T |G_m'|>R}<=C min{1,m^4/R^2}, hence sup_T |G_m'|<=C m^2 eta^{-1/2} with probability at least 1-eta. This gives continuum excursion detection from any mesh h<=c tau sqrt(eta)/(m^2 sqrt N), including uniform, stratified, and randomly shifted uniform grids, with miss probability at most eta. With bounded densities, the same cutoff plus the speed-conditional boundary estimate gives P{min_T |G_m|<=t}<=eta+C_{B,K} m t/sqrt(eta) for m^{-B}<=t<=1, hence a 2eta boundary separation at t<=c_{B,K} eta^{3/2}/m. This is a finite-confidence concentration/anti-concentration package, not a Matrix-Spencer addendum.

CURRENT paper12 quantum-effect finite-confidence addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:quantum-effect-bernstein-finite-confidence specializes the bounded matrix-Bernstein finite-confidence certificate to centered random quantum effects and density matrices. For conditionally independent 0<=E_{h,i}<=I, X_{h,i}=E_{h,i}-E(E_{h,i}|F) has ||X_{h,i}||op<=1 and conditional variance sum E(X_{h,i}^2|F) <= sum E(E_{h,i}|F), so the selected finite-menu Bernstein cost is 2D exp{-u_h^2/(2(v_h+u_h/3))} with v_h>=||sum_i E(E_{h,i}|F)||op. Therefore S_h=max{1,u_h/sqrt n} contraction-normalizes each selected centered quantum-effect stream and the all-plus signing gives disc<=sqrt n with confidence 1-delta. In the homogeneous finite-menu form, S=C max{1,sqrt(vL/n)+L/sqrt n}, L=log(2MD/delta); for conditionally maximally mixed random density matrices v=n/D, so unscaled centered states are already all-plus certified when D dominates L and n dominates L^2.

CURRENT paper16 critical inverse-moment addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-truncated upgrades the weak-L1 inverse-minimum endpoint into the exact critical truncation statement. For Y_N=(sqrt(N) min_T |G_m|)^{-1}, it proves sup_N E[(Y_N wedge R)-1]_+ <= C log R for all R>=1, and for every fixed truncation window limsup_N E[(Y_N wedge R)-1]_+ <= sqrt(pi/3) log R. Thus the endpoint q=1 inverse moment is controlled logarithmically under truncation with the same sharp upper constant, while strong inverse moments remain available precisely below q=1 via cor:global-negative-moments.

CURRENT paper16 inverse-log-moment addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-logmoments upgrades the weak-L1 inverse-minimum endpoint to full logarithmic integrability. For Y_N=(sqrt(N) min_T |G_m|)^{-1} and every p>0, sup_N E[(log_+ Y_N)^p]<infty, and limsup_N E[(log_+ Y_N)^p] <= sqrt(pi/3) Gamma(p+1). The proof is a layer-cake integration of cor:global-inverse-weak, using the sharp finite-window weak constant on bounded inverse scales and the uniform weak-L1 tail for the far inverse tail.

CURRENT paper16 inverse-quantile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-quantiles upgrades the weak-L1 inverse-minimum endpoint to quantile form. For M_N=sqrt(N) min_T |G_m| and Y_N=M_N^{-1}, the upper-tail quantile Q_N(alpha)=inf{u>=1:P(Y_N>u)<=alpha} obeys Q_N(alpha)<=C/alpha uniformly in N and 0<alpha<=1, and for every fixed alpha, limsup_N Q_N(alpha)<=sqrt(pi/3)/alpha. In lower-quantile form, if q_N(p)=inf{s>=0:P(M_N<=s)>=p}, then q_N(p)>=c p uniformly and liminf_N q_N(p)>=p sqrt(3/pi) for every fixed 0<p<1.

CURRENT paper16 finite-family inverse-quantile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-quantiles makes the endpoint quantile bound simultaneous over finite batches. If Y_{N,1},...,Y_{N,L} have the same marginals as Y_N=(sqrt(N) min_T |G_m|)^{-1}, with no independence required, then Q_{N,L}(alpha)=inf{u>=1:P{max_l Y_{N,l}>u}<=alpha} satisfies Q_{N,L}(alpha)<=C L/alpha uniformly in N,L,alpha, and for every fixed L and alpha, limsup_N Q_{N,L}(alpha)<=L sqrt(pi/3)/alpha. Equivalently, the lower quantile of min_l sqrt(N) min_T |G_m^{(l)}| is at least c p/L uniformly and at least p sqrt(3/pi)/L asymptotically for fixed L.

CURRENT paper16 finite-family inverse-moment addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-moments upgrades the finite-batch endpoint package from quantiles to integrability. In the same no-independence, common-marginal setting, the batch maximum Y^*_{N,L} satisfies sup_N E[(Y^*_{N,L} wedge R)-1]_+ <= C L log R and, for fixed L,R, limsup_N E[(Y^*_{N,L} wedge R)-1]_+ <= L sqrt(pi/3) log R. For every p>0, sup_N E[(log_+ Y^*_{N,L})^p] <= C_p L and, for fixed L, limsup_N E[(log_+ Y^*_{N,L})^p] <= L sqrt(pi/3) Gamma(p+1). Thus simultaneous finite testing preserves the endpoint q=1 obstruction with only the linear union-bound factor in the sharp constants.

CURRENT paper16 finite-family subcritical inverse-power addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-subcritical extends the finite-batch endpoint package below the critical exponent. In the same no-independence, common-marginal setting, the batch maximum Y^*_{N,L} satisfies sup_N E[(Y^*_{N,L})^q] = sup_N E[M_{N,L,*}^{-q}] <= C_q L for every 0<q<1. For fixed L and small-clearance window a, limsup_N E[M_{N,L,*}^{-q} 1_{M_{N,L,*}<=a}] <= L sqrt(pi/3) a^{1-q}/(1-q), so finite simultaneous clearance preserves the single-symbol subcritical endpoint exponent and only multiplies the sharp coefficient by L.

CURRENT paper16 finite-family summable-clearance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-borel packages the finite-batch small-clearance bound into a Borel-Cantelli schedule. For arrays with L_N common-marginal inverse minima on one probability space, no independence required within a batch or across N, any deterministic thresholds 0<a_N<=1 with sum_N L_N a_N<infty satisfy P{M_{N,L_N,*}<=a_N infinitely often}=0. In particular, if L_N<=C N^beta then M_{N,L_N,*}>N^{-beta-1-eps} eventually almost surely for every eps>0, equivalently the unscaled simultaneous Gaussian boundary minimum is eventually larger than N^{-beta-3/2-eps}.

CURRENT paper16 finite-family weak-endpoint addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-weak makes the finite-batch weak-L1 endpoint explicit. If Y_{N,1},...,Y_{N,L} have the common marginal law of Y_N=(sqrt(N) min_T |G_m|)^{-1}, with no independence required, then sup_N sup_{u>=1} u P{max_l Y_{N,l}>u} <= C L. For fixed L and inverse window R, limsup_N sup_{1<=u<=R} (u P{max_l Y_{N,l}>u} - L sqrt(pi/3))_+=0, so the sharp finite-window weak constant only pays the linear union-bound factor. The finite-family quantile proof now cites this weak-tail corollary directly.

CURRENT paper16 finite-family bad-clearance count addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-counts records the expected number of bad clearances in a finite batch. In the common-marginal/no-independence setting, Z_{N,L}(s)=sum_l 1_{M_{N,l}<=s} satisfies E Z_{N,L}(s)<=C L s uniformly for all s>=0, and for fixed L,K the sharp window limsup sup_{s<=K}(E Z_{N,L}(s)-L sqrt(pi/3) s)_+=0. Markov gives P{Z_{N,L}(s)>=r}<=C Ls/r and the matching sharp fixed-window upper coefficient after multiplying by r. Thus the finite-batch endpoint package controls not only the first bad symbol but also bad-clearance counts.

CURRENT paper16 finite-family clearance order-statistic addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-order-quantiles turns the bad-count estimate into lower quantiles for the r-th smallest simultaneous clearance. If M_{N,L}^{(r)} is the r-th order statistic of L common-marginal scaled clearances, then q_{N,L,r}(p)>=c p r/L uniformly in N,L,r,p, and for fixed L,r,p the asymptotic lower bound improves to (p r/L) sqrt(3/pi). Equivalently, forcing r bad clearances among L symbols costs the r/L fraction of the one-symbol continuum clearance scale, with no independence assumption.

CURRENT paper16 sparse bad-count schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-count-borel upgrades the growing-batch Borel-Cantelli package from first bad clearance to a permitted bad-count budget. For arrays of L_N common-marginal scaled clearances and deterministic 1<=r_N<=L_N, if sum_N L_N a_N/r_N<infty then P{at least r_N clearances are <=a_N infinitely often}=0, so the r_N-th order statistic is eventually above a_N almost surely. In polynomial form, L_N<=C N^beta and r_N>=c N^gamma imply Z_N(N^{-beta+gamma-1-eps})<r_N eventually for every eps>0, with no independence within or across batches.

CURRENT paper16 total bad-clearance mass addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-count-sum strengthens the growing-batch endpoint package from event-level Borel-Cantelli to total bad-symbol summability. If sum_N L_N a_N<infty, then E sum_N Z_N(a_N)<infty and therefore sum_N Z_N(a_N)<infty almost surely, so only finitely many individual pairs (N,l) have scaled clearance at most a_N. In polynomial form, L_N<=C N^beta gives only finitely many individual symbols with scaled clearance <=N^{-beta-1-eps}, equivalently unscaled Gaussian boundary minimum <=N^{-beta-3/2-eps}, again without independence assumptions.

CURRENT paper16 clearance-deficit mass addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-deficit-sum strengthens the bad-clearance count package from summing indicators to summing shortfall sizes. For D_N(a)=sum_l (a-M_{N,l})_+, the linear small-ball count estimate gives E D_N(a)<=C L_N a^2 and, for fixed L, the sharp finite-window coefficient (L/2) sqrt(pi/3) a^2. Hence sum_N L_N a_N^2<infty implies sum_N D_N(a_N)<infty almost surely, with no independence. In polynomial batches L_N<=C N^beta, the scaled deficits below N^{-(beta+1)/2-eps} are summable almost surely, equivalently the unscaled Gaussian shortfalls below N^{-(beta+2)/2-eps} are summable after the natural sqrt(N) scaling.

CURRENT paper16 normalized deficit-budget addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-deficit-budget turns the clearance-deficit mass estimate into a relative budget theorem. If b_N>0 and sum_N L_N a_N^2/b_N<infty, then E sum_N D_N(a_N)/b_N<infty, hence sum_N D_N(a_N)/b_N<infty almost surely and D_N(a_N)=o(b_N). In polynomial batches L_N<=C N^beta with budget b_N>=c N^{-delta}, the total scaled shortfall below N^{-(beta+delta+1)/2-eps} is o(N^{-delta}) almost surely; equivalently the unscaled Gaussian shortfalls below N^{-(beta+delta+2)/2-eps}, after sqrt(N) scaling, are o(N^{-delta}). No independence is required.

CURRENT paper16 powered clearance-deficit addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-deficit-powers upgrades deficit summability from first powers to every p>0. For D_N^{(p)}(a)=sum_l (a-M_{N,l})_+^p, the endpoint count bound gives E D_N^{(p)}(a)<=C_p L_N a^{p+1}; for fixed L the sharp finite-window coefficient is L sqrt(pi/3) a^{p+1}/(p+1). Consequently sum_N L_N a_N^{p+1}/b_N<infty implies sum_N D_N^{(p)}(a_N)/b_N<infty a.s. and D_N^{(p)}(a_N)=o(b_N). In polynomial batches L_N<=C N^beta and b_N>=c N^{-delta}, powered scaled shortfalls below N^{-(beta+delta+1)/(p+1)-eps} are o(N^{-delta}) a.s.; p=1 recovers the previous normalized deficit-budget theorem.

CURRENT paper16 jump-weighted clearance-deficit addendum (local, not yet pushed): cor:global-inverse-family-deficit-weighted now allows an entrance jump at zero in addition to Stieltjes jumps away from zero and smooth shortfall penalties. For W_N^Phi(a)=sum_l Phi((a-M_{N,l})_+) and H_Phi(a)=a Delta_0 Phi+int_(0,a] (a-u) dmu_Phi(u), the count bound gives E W_N^Phi(a)<=C L_N H_Phi(a) for a<=1; for fixed L the sharp finite-window coefficient is L sqrt(pi/3) H_Phi(a). The triangular-array schedule version allows Phi_N, including hard positive-shortfall indicators, to vary with N: if sum_N L_N H_N/b_N<infty, then the normalized weighted shortfall sum is finite almost surely. Delta_0 Phi=0 recovers the Stieltjes/AC weighted theorem, Phi(u)=u^p recovers powered deficits, and Phi(u)=1_{u>0} recovers the strict bad-clearance count scale.

CURRENT paper16 subcritical inverse-clearance mass addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-subcritical-sum upgrades the endpoint inverse-power package from fixed-batch minimum moments to growing-batch total individual inverse-clearance mass. For 0<q<1 and S_N^{(q)}(a)=sum_l M_{N,l}^{-q} 1{M_{N,l}<=a}, the count bound gives E S_N^{(q)}(a)<=C_q L_N a^{1-q}; for fixed L the sharp coefficient is L sqrt(pi/3) a^{1-q}/(1-q). If sum_N L_N a_N^{1-q}/b_N<infty, then sum_N S_N^{(q)}(a_N)/b_N<infty a.s., hence S_N^{(q)}(a_N)=o(b_N). CURRENT paper16 inverse-clearance gauge addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-gauge-sum upgrades the growing-batch inverse-mass schedule from pure powers to absolutely continuous nonincreasing endpoint gauges. For T_N^psi(a)=sum_l psi(M_{N,l}) 1{M_{N,l}<=a} and B_psi(a)=a psi(a)+int_0^a s(-psi'(s)) ds, the count bound gives E T_N^psi(a)<=C L_N B_psi(a); for fixed L the sharp finite-window coefficient is L sqrt(pi/3) B_psi(a). If sum_N L_N B_{psi_N}(a_N)/b_N<infty, then sum_N T_N^{psi_N}(a_N)/b_N<infty a.s. and T_N^{psi_N}(a_N)=o(b_N). This recovers s^{-q}, 0<q<1, and adds logarithmic refinements such as s^{-q}(log(e/s))^gamma and (log(e/s))^gamma.

CURRENT paper16 Stieltjes inverse-gauge upgrade (local, not yet pushed): cor:global-inverse-family-gauge-sum now allows any nonincreasing right-continuous endpoint gauge psi on (0,a], with Stieltjes measure nu_psi((u,v])=psi(u)-psi(v) and budget B_psi(a)=a psi(a)+int_(0,a] s dnu_psi(s). The AC theorem is the special case dnu_psi=(-psi')ds; jump gauges are now included.

CURRENT paper16 inverse-gauge menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-gauge-menu makes the confidence theorem simultaneous over deterministic finite or countable menus of endpoint gauges. CURRENT paper16 aggregate inverse-gauge menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-inverse-family-gauge-menu-sum controls total mass across a finite or countable deterministic gauge menu. AUDIT fixes folded in locally: paper16 one-point lemma covers the s=2t use and proves covariance nondegeneracy via exact eigenvalues; away-from-endpoint regression lower bound promoted to lem:kr-regression-lower with endpoint-near-pi reduction and bulk Schur complement; endpoint Dirichlet-deficit lemma, endpoint Kac--Rice, endpoint lower sharpness, bounded-density endpoint occupation bound, speed-tail-to-boundary reduction, subexponential speed-tail corollary via Bernstein, Rademacher endpoint lattice proposition, and a speed-cutoff-is-extra warning added; overstrong Videnskii local-Bernstein claim removed; speed-tail constant dependence made explicit. paper12 AF ceiling now requires full compressed span/full-unit-ball coverage, not mere full rank, and explanatory overclaims were narrowed to the full-span finite-lift route; paper12 also adds a spectral-packing entropy floor for finite-stage approximation and cor:nouniversal, making the no-cheap-universal-finite-lift consequence explicit. Lean EntropyAbsorption now machine-checks the c^2 tensor-amplification entropy identity plus direct-sum entropy <=, equality at constant height, <= with finite sup height, monotonicity under blockwise enlargement, zero-rank simplification, disjoint-union splitting, and strict < warnings. paper5/paper14 variance narrative corrected: current proved paper5 status is Var(nu_m(D))=O(m), with Theta(m) expected/evidenced and matching lower bound/constant/CLT/LLT still open; log m applies only to the real-root subpopulation, and anti-concentration needs CLT/LLT or Esseen-type input. paper14 now cross-links paper16: Gaussian one-symbol boundary small-ball is proved and single-symbol balanced conditioning is settled under circle clearance for all bounded-density variance-one laws; paper14's expected-normalized full random Toeplitz/Hankel model is now solved by a fixed-sign high-probability theorem, the expected-normalized Fourier edge profile is proved at the natural n/log d scale for n >= C log d, and a new normalizer-tail criterion shows the same realization-normalized O(sqrt n) conclusion for any sub-Gaussian law whose one-matrix lower tail satisfies P{||T_d|| < a E||T_d||} min(n,log^2(e+d))=o(1). Convex concentration verifies this tail, so standard Gaussian, Rademacher, and bounded real coefficient full Toeplitz/Hankel contractions are settled in the unbounded-aspect regime. The stricter arbitrary sub-Gaussian realization-normalized contraction question is reduced to this low-normalizer tail / empirical edge-variance profile beyond convex-concentrated laws. paper15 status narrowed to: general-position flat law uniform under the stated curvature/angle bands; curved companion fixed-family law uniform only over compact subcones, with curvature-band-uniform lower/slab constants open; /sin(omega) formula documented. master_index updated for 16 numbered papers / 17 PDFs, paper12 full-bandwidth theorem, paper14 expected-normalized full-bandwidth theorem, expected edge-profile benchmark, normalizer-tail criterion, convex-concentrated/Gaussian/Rademacher/bounded realization-normalized full-discrepancy theorem, edge-variance reduction, and residual arbitrary-sub-Gaussian contraction question, and paper16 sharp Gaussian uniform boundary law, bounded-density conditioning theorem under circle clearance, and bounded-density/discrete-endpoint refinements.
CURRENT paper12 strengthening (local, not yet pushed): paper12 now folds in Akbas--Sra arXiv:2606.16005 (June 14, 2026) as the current strongest exact finite-dimensional C*-algebra Matrix Spencer input. New thm:akbassra states the intrinsic-dimension criterion dim_C(A)<=C0 n => disc_op=O_{C0}(sqrt n); cor:group-akbassra upgrades arbitrary nonabelian group convolution when |G|=O(n) with no bounded-irrep hypothesis; new cor:group-akbassra-pert adds the perturbative square group-algebra endpoint, allowing self-adjoint low-Frobenius tails ||L_i||_F^2<=n/log^3(en), in particular operator-norm-one rank <= n/log^3(en) noise, on top of arbitrary self-adjoint convolution contractions. cor:afdim gives the AF approximation version for stages dim_C(A_k)=O(n), separating the older finite-lift entropy Ent(A_k)=h_k R_k from the stronger square-sum/dimension parameter d_k=sum r_{k,alpha}^2. UHF/full-block thresholds are unchanged because d_k=Ent(A_k)=D^2 there; heterogeneous direct sums can improve. New thm:akbassra-pert and cor:afdim-pert add the low-Frobenius perturbative upgrade: in finite-dimensional matrix representations, self-adjoint tails with ||L_i||_op<=1 and ||L_i||_F^2<=n/log^3(en) may be added to a dimension-controlled AF-stage core at only the usual AF approximation cost. New cor:akbassra-lowrank makes the rank-constrained consequence explicit: operator-norm-one self-adjoint tails of rank <= n/log^3(en) are allowed, since ||L_i||_F^2 <= rank(L_i)||L_i||_op^2. The banded multilevel section now also promotes the anisotropic ∏_j(2w_j+1) Toeplitz/Hankel estimate to a formal corollary, upgrades the circulant matching lower bound to the same anisotropic product form already in the self-adjoint scalar/block-circulant subclass, and strengthens the self-adjoint square-root regime to the product-bandwidth condition ∏_j(2w_j+1)≤n, with ambient D still unrestricted.
CURRENT paper12 distribution-free random-matrix gauge addendum (pushed): new cor:distribution-free-all-plus-gauge-random-matrices handles arbitrary random matrices in general. For any self-adjoint or rectangular random matrices X_i, with no independence, centering, moment, tail, or structural assumptions, the pathwise gauge G=max{max_i ||X_i||op, n^{-1/2}||sum_i X_i||op} makes A_i=X_i/G contraction-normalized and the all-plus signing gives disc or disc_op <= sqrt n. The active-menu form says deterministic or environment-measurable scales s_{m,h} are valid whenever the event max_{h<=J_m} G_{m,h}>s_{m,h} is summable, so all later random-matrix tail theorems can be read as ways to certify deterministic gauges rather than as Toeplitz-specific input.

CURRENT paper12 distribution-free gauge moment-schedule addendum (pushed): new cor:distribution-free-gauge-moment-schedules makes the arbitrary-random-matrix gauge checkable from moment or quantile budgets. For the all-plus gauge G_{m,h}=max{max_i||X_{m,h,i}||op,n_m^{-1/2}||sum_i X_{m,h,i}||op}, if deterministic scales s_{m,h} satisfy sum_m sum_{h<=J_m} E(G_{m,h}/s_{m,h})^p<infty for some p>0, then Borel-Cantelli gives eventual contraction normalization and the all-plus disc/disc_op <= sqrt(n_m) certificate for every active self-adjoint or rectangular tuple. Equivalently, rowwise budgets eta_{m,h} with summable total allow s_{m,h}>=eta_{m,h}^{-1/p}(E G_{m,h}^p)^{1/p}, and a common row scale costs J_m^{1/p} times the maximum L^p gauge. No independence across matrices, menus, or dimensions is used.

CURRENT paper12 closed-form distribution-free gauge schedules: new cor:distribution-free-gauge-closed-schedules turns the arbitrary-random-matrix all-plus gauge moment criterion into explicit normalizations. If E G_{m,h}^p<=r_{m,h}^p, arbitrary active-menu growth is handled by s_{m,h}>=r_{m,h}(J_m(e+m)^{1+gamma})^{1/p}. If J_m<=(eD_m)^Gamma and max_h r_{m,h}<=r_m, the common scale s_m=r_m(eD_m)^B is summable on all integer dimensions when pB>Gamma+1 and on sparse stretched-exponential dimension schedules when pB>Gamma. More generally, any common row scale with sum_m (sum_h r_{m,h}^p)/s_m^p<infty works. The proof is only Markov plus the previous moment-schedule corollary, so no independence assumptions enter.

CURRENT paper12 tail-profile distribution-free gauge schedules: new cor:distribution-free-gauge-tail-schedules turns direct all-plus gauge tail bounds into deterministic normalizations for arbitrary random matrices. If P{G_{m,h}>r_{m,h}u}<=Phi_{m,h}(u) and sum_m sum_h Phi_{m,h}(u_{m,h})<infty, then s_{m,h}=r_{m,h}u_{m,h} eventually contraction-normalizes every active tuple and the all-plus signing gives disc/disc_op<=sqrt(n_m). Under a uniform sub-Weibull gauge tail P{G>r u}<=C0 exp(-c0 u^q), arbitrary active-menu growth is handled by u_m=max{1,(max{0,log(C0 J_m)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)}/c0)^{1/q}}. If J_m<=(eD_m)^Gamma and max_h r_{m,h}<=r_m, the common scale r_m A(log(eD_m))^{1/q} is summable on all integer dimensions when c0 A^q>Gamma+1 and on sparse stretched-exponential schedules when c0 A^q>Gamma. No independence assumptions enter.

CURRENT paper12 finite-confidence distribution-free gauge certificates: new cor:distribution-free-gauge-finite-confidence gives one-row finite-menu versions of the arbitrary-random-matrix all-plus gauge. For a finite menu of arbitrary self-adjoint or rectangular tuples with gauges G_h and confidence 1-delta, moment envelopes E G_h^p<=r_h^p are certified by s_h=r_h(J/delta)^{1/p}; direct tail bounds P{G_h>r_h u}<=Phi_h(u) are certified by any upper-tail choices with sum_h Phi_h(u_h)<=delta; and uniform sub-Weibull gauge tails P{G_h>r_h u}<=C0 exp(-c0 u^q) are certified by u=max{1,(max{0,log(C0 J/delta)}/c0)^{1/q}}. With probability at least 1-delta, every scaled tuple is contraction-normalized and has the all-plus disc/disc_op<=sqrt(n_h) certificate. No independence, centering, or identical-distribution assumption enters.

CURRENT paper12 conditional finite-confidence distribution-free gauge certificates: new cor:distribution-free-gauge-conditional-confidence turns the arbitrary-random-matrix all-plus gauge into an adaptive finite-confidence certificate. For a finite menu and side information F, conditional moment envelopes E(G_h^p|F)<=R_h^p give F-measurable scales S_h=R_h(J/delta)^{1/p}; chosen-threshold conditional tail budgets P{G_h>R_h U_h|F}<=eta_h with sum_h eta_h<=delta give S_h=R_h U_h; and conditional sub-Weibull gauge tails give the same u=max{1,(max{0,log(C0 J/delta)}/c0)^{1/q}} multiplier. Conditional Markov and the conditional union bound give simultaneous contraction normalization and all-plus disc/disc_op<=sqrt(n_h) with probability at least 1-delta. No conditional independence, centering, or identical-distribution assumption enters.

CURRENT paper12 conditional distribution-free gauge schedule addendum: new cor:distribution-free-gauge-conditional-schedules promotes the conditional finite-confidence all-plus gauge certificate to triangular arrays. For arbitrary random self-adjoint or rectangular matrix menus with side information F_m, conditional moment envelopes E(G_{m,h}^p|F_m)<=R_{m,h}^p and summable deterministic budgets eta_{m,h} give random scales S_{m,h}=R_{m,h} eta_{m,h}^{-1/p}; chosen-threshold conditional tail budgets P{G_{m,h}>R_{m,h}U_{m,h}|F_m}<=eta_{m,h} work whenever sum E eta_{m,h}<infty; conditional sub-Weibull gauge tails give the adaptive row multiplier u_m=max{1,(max{0,log(C0 J_m)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)}/c0)^{1/q}}. Conditional Markov/union bounds plus Borel-Cantelli give eventual simultaneous contraction normalization and all-plus disc/disc_op<=sqrt(n_m), with no independence, conditional independence, centering, or identical-distribution assumption.

CURRENT paper12 random active-menu distribution-free gauge schedule addendum: new cor:distribution-free-gauge-random-active-schedules allows the active random-matrix catalogue itself to be environment-selected. For finite F_m-measurable active sets H_m of arbitrary self-adjoint or rectangular matrix tuples, conditional moment envelopes E(G_{m,h}^p|F_m)<=R_{m,h}^p and positive F_m-measurable budgets eta_{m,h} are valid when sum_m E sum_{h in H_m} eta_{m,h}<infty, with scales S_{m,h}=R_{m,h} eta_{m,h}^{-1/p}. Chosen-threshold conditional tail budgets work under the same selected-budget summability. Conditional sub-Weibull gauge tails work with any random size bound M_m>=|H_m| whose selected tail cost sum_m E[M_m C0 exp(-c0 u_m^q)] is finite, including the deterministic-size multiplier u_m=max{1,(max{0,log(C0 M_m)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)}/c0)^{1/q}}. Thus random active subcatalogues pay only the selected conditional budget, with no independence, conditional independence, centering, or identical-distribution assumption.

CURRENT paper12 random active finite-confidence distribution-free gauge addendum: new cor:distribution-free-gauge-random-active-confidence gives one-row finite-confidence certificates when the active random-matrix catalogue is itself F-measurable and environment-selected. For an a.s. finite active set H of arbitrary self-adjoint or rectangular random matrix tuples, conditional moment envelopes E(G_h^p|F)<=R_h^p and positive F-measurable budgets eta_h give scales S_h=R_h eta_h^{-1/p} whenever E sum_{h in H} eta_h<=delta; chosen-threshold conditional tail budgets P{G_h>R_h U_h|F}<=eta_h work under the same selected-budget condition. Conditional sub-Weibull gauge tails work with any F-measurable size bound M>=|H| and multiplier u satisfying E[M C0 exp(-c0 u^q)]<=delta, in particular the random multiplier u=max{1,(max{0,log(C0 M/delta)}/c0)^{1/q}}. Thus finite-confidence requests for arbitrary random matrices pay only for the active selected menu, not a deterministic ambient library, with no independence, conditional independence, centering, or identical-distribution assumption.

CURRENT paper12 random active full operator-tail addendum: new cor:random-active-full-operator-tail-random-matrices extends the Gaussian/operator-tail average-case route to environment-selected random active libraries. For F_m-measurable finite active sets A_m of full self-adjoint random matrix streams satisfying the conditional scalar-combination operator tail with F_m-measurable K_{m,h}, the normalized streams Y_{m,h,i}/(K_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m)+tau_{m,h})) are eventually contraction-normalized and all-plus Matrix-Spencer certified whenever sum_m E sum_{h in A_m} 2(n_m+1) exp(-tau_{m,h}^2)<infty. A common tau_m works under sum_m E[2 M_m(n_m+1) exp(-tau_m^2)]<infty for any F_m-measurable size bound M_m>=|A_m|; deterministic M_m is handled by tau_m^2>=log(2M_m(n_m+1))+(1+gamma)log(e+m), and the polynomial expected-size envelope E[M_m](n_m+1)<=(eD_m)^Gamma gives the usual all-integer threshold R>Gamma+1 and sparse stretched-exponential threshold R>Gamma for tau_m^2=R log(eD_m). Thus full GOE/GUE/Wigner-type or correlated operator-tail libraries pay only the selected expected tail count, not a deterministic ambient library size, with no coordinatewise sign symmetry.

CURRENT paper12 random active rectangular full operator-tail addendum: new cor:random-active-rectangular-full-operator-tail-random-matrices extends the selected-cost operator-tail route to rectangular/non-self-adjoint streams. For F_m-measurable finite active sets A_m of rectangular streams satisfying the conditional scalar-combination operator tail at the left-plus-right dimension D_m^rec=d_{m,1}+d_{m,2}, the normalized matrices X_{m,h,i}/(K_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau_{m,h})) are eventually contraction-normalized and all-plus rectangular Matrix-Spencer certified whenever sum_m E sum_{h in A_m} 2(n_m+1) exp(-tau_{m,h}^2)<infty. A common tau_m works under sum_m E[2 M_m(n_m+1) exp(-tau_m^2)]<infty for any F_m-measurable size bound M_m>=|A_m|, and the polynomial expected-size envelope E[M_m](n_m+1)<=(eD_m^rec)^Gamma gives the all-integer threshold R>Gamma+1 and sparse stretched-exponential threshold R>Gamma for tau_m^2=R log(eD_m^rec). Thus rectangular Gaussian/sub-Gaussian, correlated rectangular matrix-series, feature-block, measurement-matrix, and other non-self-adjoint operator-tail selected libraries pay only the selected expected tail count, with no sign symmetry, entry independence, or self-adjointness beyond the displayed tail.

CURRENT paper12 random active bilinear-form rectangular addendum: new cor:random-active-bilinear-form-rectangular-random-matrices makes the selected-cost rectangular operator-tail route checkable from scalar bilinear tails. For F_m-measurable finite active sets A_m, if every fixed bilinear form <u,sum_i a_i X_{m,h,i}v> has conditional sub-Gaussian proxy q_{m,h}||a||_2 and q_{m,h},tau_{m,h} are F_m-measurable, then the normalized matrices X_{m,h,i}/(5q_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau_{m,h})) are eventually contraction-normalized and all-plus rectangular Matrix-Spencer certified whenever sum_m E sum_{h in A_m} 2(n_m+1) exp(-tau_{m,h}^2)<infty. The common-tau, deterministic-size, all-integer, and sparse schedules are inherited from the random-active rectangular operator-tail theorem. CURRENT paper12 random active Bernstein bilinear rectangular addendum: new cor:random-active-bernstein-bilinear-rectangular gives the selected-cost random-active analogue of the Bernstein bilinear rectangular theorem. For F_m-measurable active sets A_m, if fixed bilinear forms <u,sum_i a_i X_{m,h,i}v> have conditional Bernstein tails with variance proxy q_{m,h}||a||_2 and jump proxy b_{m,h}||a||_infty, then normalization by C_Bb(q_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau_{m,h})+b_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau_{m,h})^2) is eventually contraction-normalized and all-plus Matrix-Spencer certified whenever sum_m E sum_{h in A_m}2(n_m+1)exp(-tau_{m,h}^2)<infty. CURRENT paper12 random active independent-entry rectangular Bernstein addendum: new cor:random-active-independent-entry-bernstein-rectangular makes the selected-cost Bernstein bilinear theorem entrywise checkable for environment-selected rectangular arrays. Conditional complex scalar Bernstein estimates for all linear combinations of entries, with F_m-measurable variance proxies sigma_{m,h,i,r,s} and jump proxies L_{m,h,i,r,s}, produce Q^{rB}_{m,h} and B^{rB}_{m,h}; normalizing by C(q scale)=C(Q^{rB}_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau_{m,h})+B^{rB}_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau_{m,h})^2) gives eventual contraction-normalized all-plus Matrix-Spencer certificates whenever the selected Bernstein tail count sum_m E sum_{h in A_m}2(n_m+1)exp(-tau_{m,h}^2) is finite. Homogeneous proxies reduce the scale to sigma_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau)+L_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau)^2, covering selected bounded-entry, Poissonized, exponential-entry, gamma-entry, and other light-tailed independent-entry rectangular libraries.

CURRENT paper12 conditionally independent entrywise Bernstein addendum: new cor:random-active-entrywise-bernstein-rectangular derives the selected independent-entry Bernstein proxy theorem from ordinary conditional independence of the entries. Given F_m, every selected stream has independent centered entries whose real rotations obey Bernstein mgf bounds with variance proxies sigma_{m,h,i,r,s} and jump proxies L_{m,h,i,r,s}; applying scalar Bernstein to each complex linear combination gives the exact V_c and M_c proxy hypothesis needed by cor:random-active-independent-entry-bernstein-rectangular. Thus selected bounded, Poissonized, centered gamma, centered exponential, and other entrywise Bernstein rectangular libraries inherit the same contraction-normalized all-plus Matrix-Spencer schedules under the selected expected tail count sum_m E sum_{h in A_m}2(n_m+1)exp(-tau_{m,h}^2)<infty.

CURRENT paper12 bounded-entry finite-confidence addendum: new cor:bounded-entry-bernstein-finite-confidence specializes the entrywise Bernstein finite-confidence theorem to ordinary conditionally independent bounded rectangular entries. Given side information F and an F-measurable active set A, conditional second-moment proxies E(|x_{h,i,r,s}|^2|F)<=nu_{h,i,r,s}^2 and uniform caps |x_{h,i,r,s}|<=R_{h,i,r,s} imply the rotated Bernstein mgf hypothesis with variance proxy nu and jump proxy R. Thus if E sum_{h in A}2(n+1)exp(-tau_h^2)<=delta, the normalized streams with scale C[Q_h^{bd}(sqrt(D^rec)+tau_h)+R_h^{bd}(sqrt(D^rec)+tau_h)^2] are simultaneously contraction-normalized and all-plus Matrix-Spencer certified with probability at least 1-delta. If |A|<=M, tau^2>=log(2M(n+1)/delta) gives the explicit homogeneous scale C[nu_h(sqrt(D^rec)+tau)+R_h(sqrt(D^rec)+tau)^2].

CURRENT paper12 bounded matrix-Bernstein finite-confidence addendum: new cor:bounded-matrix-bernstein-finite-confidence gives one-row selected-library Matrix-Spencer certificates directly from matrix Bernstein data. For an F-measurable active set A of conditionally independent centered self-adjoint streams with ||X_{h,i}||op<=s_h and ||sum_i E(X_{h,i}^2|F)||op<=v_h, if E sum_{h in A}2D exp(-u_h^2/(2(v_h+s_h u_h/3)))<=delta, then S_h=max{s_h,u_h/sqrt n} contraction-normalizes every selected stream and the all-plus signing gives disc<=sqrt n with probability at least 1-delta. The same statement holds for rectangular matrices with D^rec=d_1+d_2 after replacing the variance budget by the row/column budgets and using self-adjoint dilation. If |A|<=M, L=log(2M Delta/delta) and u=C(sqrt(vL)+sL) give the explicit homogeneous scale C max{s,sqrt(vL/n)+sL/sqrt n}.

CURRENT paper12 bounded matrix-Freedman finite-confidence addendum: new cor:bounded-matrix-freedman-finite-confidence gives the same one-row selected-library Matrix-Spencer certificate for adapted matrix martingale-difference streams. For a G-measurable finite active set A, self-adjoint streams adapted to filtrations F_{h,i}, norm caps ||X_{h,i}||op<=s_h, and predictable quadratic variation ||sum_i E(X_{h,i}^2|F_{h,i-1})||op<=v_h, the condition E sum_{h in A}2D exp(-u_h^2/(2(v_h+s_hu_h/3)))<=delta implies that S_h=max{s_h,u_h/sqrt n} contraction-normalizes every selected stream and the all-plus signing gives disc<=sqrt n with probability at least 1-delta. Rectangular martingale-difference streams follow by self-adjoint dilation with row and column predictable variance budgets and D^rec=d_1+d_2. If |A|<=M, L=log(2M Delta/delta), and u=C(sqrt(vL)+sL), the homogeneous finite-menu scale is C max{s,sqrt(vL/n)+sL/sqrt n}.

CURRENT paper12 tail-capped finite-confidence addendum: new cor:tail-capped-finite-confidence gives one-row selected-library Matrix-Spencer certificates for unbounded independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular matrix streams. For a G-measurable active set A, cap b_h, capped variables T_{h,i}=X_{h,i}1_{||X_{h,i}||<=b_h}, centered capped variables Y_{h,i}=T_{h,i}-E(T_{h,i}|P_{h,i}), drift r_h, and variance budget v_h, if E sum_{h in A}[P(F_h^cap|G)+2 Delta exp(-u_h^2/(2(v_h+2b_h u_h/3)))]<=delta, then S_h=max{b_h,(u_h+r_h)/sqrt n} contraction-normalizes every selected original stream and the all-plus signing gives disc<=sqrt n with probability at least 1-delta. The same statement covers adapted martingale-difference streams by matrix Freedman and rectangular streams by dilation. If |A|<=M and selected cap failures cost <=delta/2, L=log(4M Delta/delta) and u=C(sqrt(vL)+bL) give the homogeneous scale C max{b,(r+sqrt(vL)+bL)/sqrt n}.

CURRENT paper12 sub-Weibull finite-confidence addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:subweibull-finite-confidence makes the tail-capped finite-confidence certificate checkable from conditional operator-norm sub-Weibull tails. For each selected independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular stream, conditional tails P{||X_{h,i}||op>s_h L_h^{1/alpha}|P_{h,i}}<=A_*e^{-L_h}, truncated drift r_h, and uncentered capped row/column variance v_h imply the tail-capped hypotheses because centering subtracts a positive square and the cap failure costs at most A_* n e^{-L_h}. Thus if E sum_{h in A}[A_* n e^{-L_h}+2 Delta exp(-u_h^2/(2(v_h+2s_hL_h^{1/alpha}u_h/3)))]<=delta, the selected original streams are contraction-normalized and all-plus Matrix-Spencer certified with probability at least 1-delta. In the homogeneous finite-menu form, |A|<=M, L_cap=max{1,log(2A_*Mn/delta)}, L_mat=log(4M Delta/delta), and b=s L_cap^{1/alpha} give scale C max{b,(r+sqrt(vL_mat)+bL_mat)/sqrt n}.

CURRENT paper12 finite-moment finite-confidence addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:moment-finite-confidence is the polynomial-tail one-row companion to cor:subweibull-finite-confidence. For each selected independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular stream, conditional moment envelopes E(||X_{h,i}||op^p|P_{h,i})<=A_*s_h^p, cap U_h, b_h=s_hU_h^{1/p}, truncated drift r_h, and uncentered capped variance v_h imply the tail-capped hypotheses with selected cap cost A_*nU_h^{-1}. Hence if E sum_{h in A}[A_*nU_h^{-1}+2 Delta exp(-u_h^2/(2(v_h+2b_hu_h/3)))]<=delta, then the selected original streams are contraction-normalized and all-plus Matrix-Spencer certified with probability at least 1-delta. The capped variance proxy is automatic with v_h=A_*s_h^2 n U_h^{(2-p)_+/p}; for conditionally centered independent streams or adapted martingale differences and p>=1, the drift proxy is automatic with r_h=A_*ns_hU_h^{(1-p)/p}. In the homogeneous finite-menu form, U=max{1,2A_*Mn/delta}, L=log(4M Delta/delta), b=sU^{1/p}, and scale C max{b,(r+sqrt(vL)+bL)/sqrt n} give the one-row certificate.

CURRENT paper12 heterogeneous cap-profile finite-confidence addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:heterogeneous-cap-profile-finite-confidence removes the common cap from the one-row tail-capped Matrix-Spencer certificate. For selected independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular streams, each increment has its own cap b_{h,i} and conditional cap budget eta_{h,i}; with b_h^*=max_i b_{h,i}, truncated drift r_h, and uncentered capped row/column variance v_h, the condition E sum_{h in A}[sum_i eta_{h,i}+2 Delta exp(-u_h^2/(2(v_h+2b_h^*u_h/3)))]<=delta gives contraction normalization by S_h=max{b_h^*,(u_h+r_h)/sqrt n} and the all-plus Matrix-Spencer certificate with probability at least 1-delta. The homogeneous finite-menu form needs only selected cap budget <=delta/2 and uses C max{b,(r+sqrt(vL)+bL)/sqrt n}, L=log(4M Delta/delta). This lets a single finite-confidence request mix different tail scales, exponents, moment orders, and increment-specific quantile choices without replacing every increment by a worst common cap.

CURRENT paper12 heterogeneous finite-moment finite-confidence addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:heterogeneous-moment-finite-confidence is the per-increment moment companion to cor:heterogeneous-cap-profile-finite-confidence. In a selected independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular stream, each increment may have its own moment order p_{h,i}, scale s_{h,i}, and Markov cap parameter U_{h,i}; the cap is b_{h,i}=s_{h,i}U_{h,i}^{1/p_{h,i}}, the selected cap budget is A_* sum_i U_{h,i}^{-1}, and the automatic capped variance proxy is V_h=A_*sum_i s_{h,i}^2 U_{h,i}^{(2-p_{h,i})_+/p_{h,i}}. If E sum_{h in A}[A_*sum_i U_{h,i}^{-1}+2 Delta exp(-u_h^2/(2(V_h+2b_h^*u_h/3)))]<=delta and the truncated drift is <=r_h, then S_h=max{b_h^*,(u_h+r_h)/sqrt n} contraction-normalizes every selected original stream and the all-plus Matrix-Spencer certificate holds with probability at least 1-delta. For conditionally centered independent streams or adapted martingale differences with p_{h,i}>=1, the drift proxy is automatic as A_*sum_i s_{h,i}U_{h,i}^{(1-p_{h,i})/p_{h,i}}.

CURRENT paper12 tail-profile finite-confidence addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:tail-profile-finite-confidence is the exact-survival-profile one-row companion to cor:heterogeneous-cap-profile-finite-confidence. For selected independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular streams, each increment has a scale s_{h,i}, cap level lambda_{h,i}, and deterministic conditional survival profile pi_{h,i}; the cap is b_{h,i}=s_{h,i}lambda_{h,i}, the selected cap budget is sum_i pi_{h,i}(lambda_{h,i}), and the automatic capped variance proxy is V_h=2 sum_i s_{h,i}^2 int_0^{lambda_{h,i}} t pi_{h,i}(t) dt. If E sum_{h in A}[sum_i pi_{h,i}(lambda_{h,i})+2 Delta exp(-u_h^2/(2(V_h+2b_h^*u_h/3)))]<=delta and the truncated drift is <=r_h, then S_h=max{b_h^*,(u_h+r_h)/sqrt n} contraction-normalizes every selected original stream and the all-plus Matrix-Spencer certificate holds with probability at least 1-delta. For centered or martingale-difference streams, the drift proxy follows from sum_i s_{h,i}[lambda_{h,i}pi_{h,i}(lambda_{h,i})+int_{lambda_{h,i}}^\infty pi_{h,i}(t)dt].

CURRENT paper12 lift-entropy invariant (local, not yet pushed): new def:liftentropy and prop:liftentropy define L_{K,Lambda}(A_1,...,A_n), prove disc_op <= C Lambda K sqrt(n log(e+L/n)), record L<=D^2 by the identity lift, L>=D^2 for full-span generic tuples, and recover small L for finite C*-algebras, common low-dimensional corners, scalar Toeplitz/Hankel, and banded multilevel symbol classes.
CURRENT paper12 commutative-AF partition trade-off (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-partition makes the AF approximation bound concrete for C(X). A clopen partition with M atoms and oscillation eta for all real-valued contractions f_i gives disc(f_1,...,f_n) <= C sqrt(n log(e+M/n))+2n eta; therefore M=O(n) and eta=O(n^{-1/2}) imply O(sqrt n). Conversely, if one f_i has interval range, the spectral floor forces M=Omega(eta^{-1}) atoms for any finite-stage certificate at approximation scale eta, hence Omega(sqrt n) atoms at the Spencer approximation scale. This is certificate complexity, not a discrepancy lower bound.
CURRENT paper12 metric commutative-AF corollary (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-metric specializes the partition trade-off to compact zero-dimensional metric spaces. If the clopen partition growth M_X(r) is finite and omega(r) is the common modulus of continuity of the tuple, then disc(f_1,...,f_n) <= C sqrt(n log(e+M_X(r)/n))+2n omega(r). Polynomial growth M_X(r)<=A r^{-d} and uniform (L,alpha)-Holder regularity give O_{A,d,L,alpha}(sqrt(n log(e+n^{d/(2alpha)-1}))), hence O(sqrt(n log n)) for fixed d,alpha and O(sqrt n) when d<=2alpha. New lem:clopen-shrinking and cor:comm-af-covering show ordinary ball-covering numbers suffice with no cardinality loss: M_X(2rho)<=N_X(rho), so N_X(rho)<=A rho^{-d} gives the same Holder threshold d<=2alpha and the same logarithmic critical-window estimates with N_X in place of M_X. New def:metric-minkowski and cor:comm-af-metric-minkowski package this as an ordinary upper metric Minkowski theorem: finite ordinary upper metric dimension gives O(sqrt(n log n)), ordinary dimension <2alpha gives Spencer scale, and finite ordinary upper metric 2alpha-content gives the critical endpoint. New def:upper-assouad-growth and cor:comm-af-assouad package the same consequence in local-covering language: upper Assouad growth (A,s) gives the same bound with exponent s, and a D-doubling zero-dimensional model is Spencer-scale for Holder exponent alpha whenever log_2 D <= 2alpha. New cor:comm-af-ordinary-product shows finite max-metric products add ordinary covering exponents, ordinary Minkowski dimensions, critical contents, and Assouad/doubling exponents; hence the Spencer threshold is governed by the sum of the factor exponents. New def:zero-dim-holder-resolution and cor:comm-af-holder-resolution transfer the entropy bound through Holder surjections from zero-dimensional models: an entropy exponent s and Holder exponent beta give threshold s<=2alpha beta. New prop:compact-metric-resolution and cor:comm-af-compact-metric now give a scale-flexible zero-dimensional Lipschitz resolution for every compact metric space: for each 0<theta<1, N_X(s)<=N_Y(theta s) and Lip(pi)<=2/(1-theta), hence disc <= C sqrt(n log(e+N_Y(theta s)/n))+2n omega(4s/(1-theta)). Choosing theta=1/2 and s=2rho gives the direct profile bound disc <= C sqrt(n log(e+N_Y(rho)/n))+2n omega(16rho). Hence arbitrary compact metric targets with N_Y(rho)<=A rho^{-d} satisfy O(sqrt(n log(e+n^{d/(2alpha)-1}))) and are Spencer-scale when d<=2alpha; the logarithmic critical window, finite ordinary upper metric dimension, ordinary dimension <2alpha, and finite ordinary upper metric 2alpha-content endpoint all hold without any zero-dimensional hypothesis. New cor:comm-af-compact-product extends product additivity beyond zero-dimensional targets: finite max-metric products of arbitrary compact metric spaces add ordinary covering exponents, ordinary Minkowski/content data, and Assouad/doubling exponents, so the Spencer threshold is governed by the sum of factor exponents after the compact-metric resolution. New cor:comm-af-euclidean-cube gives [0,1]^D, plus compact subsets for 0<alpha<=1, the Euclidean estimate O(sqrt(n log(e+n^{D/(2alpha)-1}))) with Spencer scale when D<=2alpha. New def:clopen-minkowski, def:clopen-content, cor:comm-af-dim-threshold, cor:comm-af-critical-content, cor:comm-af-log-window, cor:comm-af-ultrametric, and cor:comm-af-product package this intrinsically and concretely: finite upper clopen Minkowski dimension gives O(sqrt(n log n)), upper clopen dimension <2alpha gives the Spencer-scale O(sqrt n) bound for Holder tuples, the critical endpoint dim_cl=2alpha is still Spencer-scale under finite upper clopen 2alpha-Minkowski content, logarithmic critical growth M_X(r)<=A r^{-2alpha}(log(e/r))^beta gives O(sqrt n) for beta<=0 and O(sqrt(n log log n)) for beta>0, compact ultrametric spaces inherit the same bounds from ball-covering growth, the q-symbol Cantor shift with scale theta has Spencer threshold alpha >= (log q)/(2 log(1/theta)), and finite max-metric products add these symbolic dimensions; dimension zero works for every positive Holder exponent.
CURRENT paper14 strengthening (local, not yet pushed): paper14 now carves out three full-bandwidth endpoints. thm:bounded-aspect records paper12's scalar full Toeplitz/Hankel bound disc_op <= C sqrt(n log(e+d/n)); hence bounded aspect ratio d/n=O(1) is pathwise settled. New thm:expected-full proves that in the expected-normalized full random Toeplitz/Hankel model, every fixed sign pattern already gives disc_op=O_{L,K}(sqrt n) with probability at least 1-d^{-L}, by applying a Bernstein/net bound to the signed Toeplitz symbol and dividing by Meckes' N_d ~ sqrt(d log d). Thus the expected-normalized full-random conjecture is now a theorem. New prop:variance-profile gives a deterministic edge-variance criterion for the remaining realization-normalized contraction question: if the normalized Fourier sample vectors satisfy max_omega sum_i |v_i(omega)|^2 <= C n/log d, then independent random signs already give the required O(sqrt n) edge bound with probability 1-d^{-A}. New prop:edge-moment-profile strengthens this deterministic criterion: the same edge-variance profile gives max_j E_eps |sum_i eps_i v_i(j)|^{2k} <= (C k n/log(e+d))^k for every k>=1. New prop:expected-profile verifies this profile in the expected-normalized model itself for n >= C log d: max_omega sum_i |P_i(omega)/N_d|^2 <= C n/log d with probability 1-d^{-A}. New thm:normalizer-tail proves a general transfer theorem: if a sub-Gaussian coefficient law has P{||T_d||_op < a E||T_d||_op} min(n,log^2(e+d))=o(1), then max_omega sum_i |P_i(omega)|^2/||T_i||_op^2 <= C n/log d with high probability, using expected-profile for good normalizers, Markov/union-counting for the bad-normalizer count, a sub-Gaussian root-value bound, and a Paley-Zygmund/Chernoff Hilbert-Schmidt floor; hence disc_op=O(sqrt n) follows by prop:variance-profile and prop:reduction, and the realization-normalized scalar edge-grid moments also obey (C k n/log d)^k on the same matrix event. New cor:normalizer-tail-empirical isolates the deterministic bad-count core: once the realized number of low-normalizer samples is at most R n/log^2(e+d), the edge profile and O(sqrt n) signing certificate hold outside only the standard d^{-A} matrix events, with constants depending on R. New cor:normalizer-tail-quant keeps the constants finite: for n>=C log(e+d), the profile failure is <=C d^{-A}+p_d(a)min(n,log^2(e+d)), and the same event supports the random-sign tail and deterministic signing certificate. New cor:normalizer-tail-rates spells this out: p_d(a)<=C log^{-B}(e+d) with B>2 gives failure O(d^{-A}+log^{2-B}(e+d)), in n=O(log d) windows B>1 suffices, and polynomial lower tails give polynomial profile failure with any smaller exponent. New cor:normalizer-tail-large-sample improves the large-sample regime: if n/log^2(e+d)->infinity and only p_d(a)<=L/log^2(e+d), then Chernoff control of the empirical bad-normalizer count gives profile failure O(d^{-A}+exp(-c_L n/log^2(e+d))) and the same O(sqrt n) signing/discrepancy conclusion. New thm:convex-normalizers verifies the lower-tail hypothesis for every real or complex coefficient law satisfying dimension-free convex concentration on its real-coordinate product space; hence real or complex Gaussian laws, Rademacher laws, and bounded real or complex coefficient laws all have disc_op(A_1,...,A_n)<=C sqrt n with probability tending to one for realization-normalized full Toeplitz/Hankel contractions whenever n<=d<=e^{o(n)} and d/n->infinity. The remaining paper14 open problem is therefore narrowed to proving the same one-matrix lower-normalizer tail for arbitrary fixed sub-Gaussian coefficient laws outside the convex-concentrated classes, equivalently preventing a logarithmic-density population of low-normalizer samples in the unbounded-aspect regime.
CURRENT paper14 empirical-package addendum (local, not yet pushed): cor:normalizer-tail-empirical, cor:normalizer-tail-quant, and cor:normalizer-tail-large-sample now carry the full deterministic profile package. On the empirical bad-count event, the realization-normalized edge profile gives not only the random-sign tail and deterministic signing certificate, but also sign-averaged edge maximum O(sqrt n) and scalar edge-grid sign moments (C k n/log(e+d))^k for every 2k-th moment.
CURRENT paper14 data-dependent normalizer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-datadep sharpens the empirical core before choosing any fixed budget. If M_a is the realized count of samples with ||T_d||_op<a E||T_d||_op, then outside only the standard d^{-A} matrix events the realization-normalized edge profile is O(n/log(e+d)+M_a log(e+d)); independent signs have tail, sign-averaged maximum, and deterministic certificate at scale O(sqrt(n+M_a log^2(e+d))), and scalar edge-grid 2k-moments are bounded by (C k (n/log(e+d)+M_a log(e+d)))^k. The previous empirical criterion is the fixed-budget specialization M_a<=R n/log^2(e+d).
CURRENT paper14 dyadic normalizer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-dyadic refines the low-normalizer empirical core. Instead of charging every sample below aN_d by the worst Hilbert-Schmidt-floor cost, it bins 2^{-(r+1)}aN_d<=||B_i||_op<2^{-r}aN_d and uses the weighted budget W_a=sum_r 4^r M_{a,r}+ell M_{a,infty}, ell=log(e+d). Outside only the standard d^{-A} matrix events the edge profile is O(n/ell+W_a), the sign scale is O(sqrt(n+W_a ell)), and all scalar edge-grid 2k-moments are bounded by (Ck(n/ell+W_a))^k. Thus the full O(sqrt n) profile/signing/moment package follows from the strictly weaker realized condition W_a=O(n/ell), which distinguishes moderately low normalizers from genuinely tiny ones.

CURRENT paper14 probabilistic dyadic-tail addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-dyadic-prob converts the realized dyadic budget W_a into a one-matrix lower-tail criterion. If Y_{d,a} is the dyadic depth of one matrix and mu_{d,a}^{dy}=E Y_{d,a}, then Markov gives profile/signing/moment failure at most C d^{-A}+ell mu_{d,a}^{dy}/R for the O(sqrt n) package. In the large-sample window, the weaker density condition mu_{d,a}^{dy}<=theta/ell yields Bernstein excess cost exp(-c_{R,theta} n/ell^2). Thus the Chernoff-scale transfer no longer needs a single-cutoff bound p_d(a)<=L/ell^2; it can use the actual dyadic depth distribution of the low-normalizer tail.

CURRENT paper14 power-curve dyadic-tail addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-power-curve makes the dyadic expectation criterion checkable from a shaped one-matrix lower-tail curve. If F_{d,a}(t)=P{||T_d||_op<t a N_d} is bounded by L ell^{-gamma} t^beta for all 0<t<=1 with beta>2 and gamma>=1, then mu_{d,a}^{dy}<=C_beta L ell^{-gamma}. Thus Markov gives profile/signing/moment failure C d^{-A}+C_beta L/(R ell^{gamma-1}), and in the critical density gamma=1 every fixed budget R>C_beta L gets the Bernstein excess cost exp(-c_{R,beta,L} n/ell^2). This upgrades the previous single-cutoff p_d(a)<=L/ell^2 requirement: top-scale low-normalizer mass of order 1/log d is acceptable when the lower tail decays quadratically-plus below that scale.

CURRENT paper14 power-curve randomized-certificate addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-power-search pushes the power-curve dyadic-tail input through the random-search and Hamming-stability machinery. Under F_{d,a}(t)<=L ell^{-gamma} t^beta with beta>2, the same matrix exception C d^{-A}+C_beta L/(R ell^{gamma-1}) supports sampled-list edge bounds, d^{-AM} success amplification over M random sign trials, binomial success-count tails, radius c ell Hamming-stable good balls, and sampled robust-center binomial tails. In the critical density gamma=1, every budget R>C_beta L gets the same randomized-certificate package outside C d^{-A}+exp(-c n/ell^2), so the relaxed top-scale lower-normalizer mass still yields actual finite randomized certificates.

CURRENT paper14 almost-sure power-curve search addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-power-borel-search is the Borel-Cantelli version of the power-curve randomized-certificate criterion. Under F_{d_m,a}(t)<=L ell_m^{-gamma}t^beta with beta>2, summability of d_m^{-A}+ell_m^{1-gamma} gives almost-sure eventual sampled-list bounds, d_m^{-AM} success amplification, binomial success-count tails, radius c ell_m Hamming-stable good balls, and sampled robust-center tails. In the critical density gamma=1 and budget R>C_beta L, the Markov summability is replaced by the sharper Bernstein condition sum_m[d_m^{-A}+exp(-c n_m/ell_m^2)]<infty; along all integer dimensions this gives eventual randomized certificates from n(d)>=q log^3(e+d) once q>1/c_{R,beta,L}.

CURRENT paper14 power-curve actual sampled-list Borel addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-power-borel-sampled upgrades the power-curve Borel search transfer from conditional finite-search tails to actual sampled-list success. Under F_{d_m,a}(t)<=L ell_m^{-gamma}t^beta, beta>2, the summability condition gains only sum_m d_m^{-alpha}<infty: then M_m>=ceil(alpha) sampled signs per dimension eventually contain, almost surely over matrices and signs, a center of a radius c ell_m Hamming ball of O(sqrt(n_m))-good signings. In the critical density gamma=1, R>C_beta L, the same conclusion uses the Bernstein summability sum_m[d_m^{-A}+exp(-c n_m/ell_m^2)+d_m^{-alpha}]<infty. Along all integer dimensions, alpha>1 and n(d)>=q log^3(e+d), q>1/c_{R,beta,L}, give actual finite sampled robust certificates eventually almost surely even though the single-cutoff p_d(a)min(n,ell^2) condition may be nonsummable.

CURRENT paper14 sparse power-curve Borel-window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-power-borel-window specializes the almost-sure power-curve search transfer to stretched-exponential dimension schedules d_m>=exp(c m^kappa). In the Markov form, the shaped lower-tail density F_{d_m,a}(t)<=L ell_m^{-gamma}t^beta is summable once gamma>1+1/kappa, giving eventual randomized certificates under only the admissible n_m>=C ell_m sample floor. In the critical density gamma=1 with budget R>C_beta L, the Bernstein form is summable if n_m>=q ell_m^2 log(e+m), q>1/c_{R,beta,L}, and in particular under any superquadratic logarithmic window n_m>=Q ell_m^{2+rho}, rho>0. Thus sparse schedules need less than the all-integer log^3 window for eventual finite randomized certificates.

CURRENT paper14 sparse sampled power-curve Borel-window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-power-borel-window-sampled combines the actual sampled-list Borel theorem with the stretched-exponential dimension-window corollary. CURRENT paper14 polynomial-confidence robust search addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:edge-profile-poly-confidence extracts the constant-size randomized search consequence from the Hamming-stable edge-profile theorem: once max_j sum_i |v_i(j)|^2 <= C n/log(e+d), M>=ceil(B) independent random signs find, with failure at most d^{-B}, a sign that is the center of a radius c log(e+d) Hamming ball of O(sqrt n)-good signings. New cor:normalizer-tail-poly-confidence pushes this through the quantitative normalizer-tail event, giving joint matrix-and-sign failure C d^{-A}+p_d(a)min(n,log^2(e+d))+d^{-B}. Thus arbitrary polynomial confidence in a robust realized-normalized certificate costs only O_B(1) random sign trials after the same matrix event.

CURRENT paper14 log-corrected logarithmic-window endpoint addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-borel-window-log-corrected sharpens the stretched-exponential logarithmic-window Borel transfer to include the integral-test endpoint. CURRENT paper14 log-corrected sampled-count endpoint addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-borel-window-log-corrected-counts carries the log-corrected logarithmic-window endpoint through the non-Chernoff sampled-count Borel theorem. Under p_d(a)<=C log^{-B}(e+d), n_m<=C_1 log^gamma(e+d_m), theta=min{gamma,2}, d_m>=exp(c0 m^kappa log(e+m)^lambda), and kappa(B-theta)>1 (or equality with lambda(B-theta)>1), summable sampled-list and binomial count tails e^{-u_m}+binom(M_m,r_m)d_m^{-A r_m} imply eventual sampled-list edge maximum and at least M_m-r_m+1 good robust centers. In particular, with u_m=s log(e+m) and r_m=1, polynomial-in-d_m batches M_m<=d_m^beta are eventually entirely robust whenever beta<A; log-corrected stretched-exponential batches M_m<=exp(b m^sigma log(e+m)^nu) are allowed when sigma<kappa, or sigma=kappa, nu<lambda, or sigma=kappa, nu=lambda, b<A c0. Taking r_m=ceil(rho M_m) gives eventual 1-rho robust-center fractions for arbitrary batch sizes, with only the list maximum retaining its entropy factor.

CURRENT paper14 iterated-log sampled-count endpoint addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-borel-window-iterlog-counts pushes the logarithmic-window sampled-count endpoint through one more integral-test layer. With theta=min{gamma,2}, q=B-theta, d_m>=exp(c0 m^kappa log(e+m)^lambda log(e+log(e+m))^mu), and p_d(a)<=C log^{-B}(e+d), the same sampled-count Borel conclusion holds whenever kappa q>1, or kappa q=1 and lambda q>1, or kappa q=lambda q=1 and mu q>1. Thus the boundary case left critical by the first log correction is recovered by an extra log-log factor. The corollary also gives eventual all-robust polynomial-in-d_m batches M_m<=d_m^beta for beta<A, lexicographic second-order log-corrected stretched-exponential batches M_m<=exp(b m^sigma log(e+m)^nu log(e+log(e+m))^eta) below the schedule profile (or at equality with b<A c0), and arbitrary-size sampled batches with eventual 1-rho robust-center fractions.

CURRENT paper14 summable logarithmic-tail sampled-count addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-borel-log-summable-counts isolates the parent schedule condition behind the stretched, log-corrected, and iterated-log logarithmic-window count theorems. If p_d(a)<=C log^{-B}(e+d), n_m<=C_1 log^gamma(e+d_m), theta=min{gamma,2}, and sum_m[d_m^{-A}+log(e+d_m)^{theta-B}+e^{-u_m}+binom(M_m,r_m)d_m^{-A r_m}]<infty, then the sampled-list maximum and at least M_m-r_m+1 good robust centers hold eventually almost surely. In particular, polynomial-in-d_m batches M_m<=d_m^beta are eventually entirely robust under sum_m[log(e+d_m)^{theta-B}+d_m^{beta-A}+e^{-u_m}]<infty and bounded u_m/log(e+d_m), while arbitrary-size batches have eventual 1-rho robust-center fractions under sum_m[log(e+d_m)^{theta-B}+d_m^{-(A-tau)}+e^{-u_m}]<infty for any fixed 0<tau<A. This makes the later integral-test schedules explicit examples rather than separate mechanisms.

CURRENT paper14 logarithmic-tail schedule-design addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-borel-log-schedule-counts turns the summable logarithmic-tail count theorem into a dimension-schedule design rule. Then the same sampled-list maximum, good-sign count, and robust-center count conclusions hold whenever the sampled sign tails are summable. CURRENT paper14 vanishing discarded-fraction sampled-count addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-borel-log-vanishing-fractions refines the logarithmic-tail schedule-design theorem for arbitrary sampled batch sizes. Under the same lower-profile hypotheses and sampled-list tail summability, the discarded fraction may vary with m: if rho_m in (0,1], 0<tau<A, rho_m>=e d_m^{-tau} eventually, r_m=ceil(rho_m M_m), and sum_m e^{-u_m}<infty, then eventually almost surely the sampled-list maximum holds and at least a 1-rho_m fraction of sampled signs are logarithmically robust centers, with no upper bound on M_m. In particular rho_m->0 is allowed, so sufficiently sparse logarithmic-tail schedules give robust-center fractions tending to one for arbitrary-size sampled batches, while only the list maximum retains its entropy factor.

CURRENT paper14 Chernoff-density addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-density makes the large-sample normalizer-count step tunable. If p_d(a)<=theta/log^2(e+d) and one allows the empirical bad-count budget R n/log^2(e+d) with R>=1 and R>theta, then the excess-count failure is exp(-I(R,theta)n/log^2(e+d)), I(R,theta)=R log(R/theta)-R+theta, while the full profile/signing/sign-averaged/scalar-moment package holds with constants depending on R. New cor:normalizer-tail-relative-density packages the application form: p_d(a)<=L/log^2(e+d) and budget (1+eta)L n/log^2(e+d) cost exp(-L h(eta)n/log^2(e+d)), h(eta)=(1+eta)log(1+eta)-eta. The previous large-sample corollary is the specialization eta=1.
CURRENT paper14 Chernoff search-stability addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-search pushes the full random-search and robust-center package through the tunable Chernoff bad-normalizer count. If p_d(a)<=theta/log^2(e+d) and the allowed empirical budget is R n/log^2(e+d), R>=1 and R>theta, then outside probability C d^{-A}+exp(-I(R,theta)n/log^2(e+d)) the M-trial entropy bound, d^{-AM} success amplification, successful-certificate binomial tails, and radius c log(e+d) Hamming-stable robust-center package all hold. The relative-excess p_d(a)<=L/log^2(e+d) and eta=1 large-sample forms inherit the same exp(-L h(eta)n/log^2(e+d)) and exp(-c_L n/log^2(e+d)) failures.

CURRENT paper14 finite sampled Chernoff confidence addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-poly-confidence extracts the one-shot actual sampled-list consequence of the tunable Chernoff search theorem. Under p_d(a)<=theta/log^2(e+d), M>=ceil(B) independent sampled signs, and n>=C log(e+d), the joint matrix-and-sign failure that no sampled sign centers a radius c log(e+d) Hamming ball of O(sqrt n)-good signings is at most C d^{-A}+exp(-I(R,theta)n/log^2(e+d))+d^{-B}. In the relative-excess form p_d(a)<=L/log^2(e+d), the Chernoff term becomes exp(-L h(eta)n/log^2(e+d)).

CURRENT paper14 finite Chernoff joint-search addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-joint-search upgrades the finite Chernoff search-stability transfer from a conditional-on-matrices package to explicit joint matrix-and-sign estimates. Under p_d(a)<=theta/log^2(e+d), every M-sample of signs has sampled-list maximum failure bounded by C d^{-A}+exp(-I(R,theta)n/log^2(e+d))+e^{-u}; the counts of sampled O(sqrt n) signings and radius c log(e+d) robust Hamming centers obey the same matrix exception plus binom(M,r)d^{-Br} tails. The relative-excess form again replaces the Chernoff term by exp(-L h(eta)n/log^2(e+d)).

CURRENT paper14 Chernoff sampled-count Borel addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-counts upgrades the finite joint-search tails to an almost-sure triangular-array count theorem. If the Chernoff matrix exception, sampled-list maximum tail e^{-u_m}, and binomial count tail binom(M_m,r_m)d_m^{-B r_m} are summable, then eventually the entire sampled list satisfies the entropy-sensitive maximum bound and at least M_m-r_m+1 sampled signs are both O(sqrt n_m)-good and centers of radius c log(e+d_m) robust Hamming balls. Along all integer dimensions, if M_d<=d^beta, u_d=s log(e+d) with s>1, n(d)>=q log^3(e+d), and B>beta+1, then eventually every sampled sign in the polynomial-size batch is robust.
VERIFY addendum: pdflatex passed after the paper14 Chernoff sampled-count Borel corollary cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-counts and summary/page-count updates (71pp), plus the master-index rebuild (13pp). CURRENT paper14 sparse Chernoff sampled-count window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-window-counts specializes the sampled-count Borel theorem to stretched-exponential dimension schedules d_m>=exp(c0 m^kappa) under the same sparse Chernoff sample windows as cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-window. If sum[e^{-u_m}+binom(M_m,r_m)d_m^{-B r_m}]<infty, then eventually the entire sampled list satisfies the entropy-sensitive maximum bound and at least M_m-r_m+1 sampled signs are robust centers. In particular, with u_m=s log(e+m), r_m=1, polynomial-in-d_m batches M_m<=d_m^beta are eventually entirely robust whenever B>beta; more generally M_m<=exp(b m^sigma) is allowed if sigma<kappa, or sigma=kappa and b<Bc0.

CURRENT paper14 finite Chernoff sampled-list abundance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-abundance packages the joint sampled-search tails into a simultaneous abundance statement. The relative-excess version uses the exp(-L h(eta)n/log^2(e+d)) Chernoff term.

CURRENT paper14 robust-fraction polynomial-confidence addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-robust-fraction-confidence converts the sampled-list abundance binomial tail into a clean constant-size randomized test. For any fraction rho and polynomial confidence exponent S, choosing a count exponent B>=2 log_2(e/rho) and M>=2(S+1)/(B rho) sampled signs gives, outside the Chernoff matrix exception plus e^{-u}+d^{-S}, the entropy-sensitive sampled-list maximum and a 1-rho fraction of radius c log(e+d) robust centers; taking u=S log(e+d) makes the whole sampled list O(sqrt n) with only another d^{-S}-level sign term.

CURRENT paper14 almost-sure robust-fraction Chernoff addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-fractions is the Borel-Cantelli counterpart of the finite robust-fraction test. If B>=2 log_2(e/rho), M>=2(S+1)/(B rho), and sum_m[d_m^{-A}+exp(-I(R,theta)n_m/log^2(e+d_m))+d_m^{-S}]<infty, then almost surely all but finitely many sampled lists have O(sqrt n_m) maximum and a 1-rho fraction of radius c log(e+d_m) robust centers. In the relative-excess form the Chernoff term is exp(-L h(eta)n_m/log^2(e+d_m)); along all integer dimensions the log^3 threshold gives the same eventual robust fraction whenever A>1 and S>1.

CURRENT paper14 sparse robust-fraction Chernoff window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-window-fractions specializes the robust-fraction Borel theorem to stretched-exponential dimension schedules. CURRENT paper14 almost-sure Chernoff search-stability addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-search turns the Chernoff search-stability transfer into a Borel-Cantelli theorem. If p_{d_m}(a)<=theta/log^2(e+d_m) and sum_m[d_m^{-A}+exp(-I(R,theta)n_m/log^2(e+d_m))]<infty, then almost surely all but finitely many matrix arrays carry the full random-search, success-count, Hamming-ball, and robust-center package. In the relative-excess form p_d(a)<=L/log^2(e+d), sum exp(-L h(eta)n_m/log^2(e+d_m)) suffices; along all integer dimensions this gives eventual certificates from the previously nonsummable density hypothesis once n(d)>=q log^3(e+d) with q>1/(L h(eta)).

CURRENT paper14 sampled Chernoff Borel addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-sampled upgrades the Chernoff Borel search transfer from conditional random-search guarantees to actual sampled-list robust certificates. Under p_{d_m}(a)<=theta/log^2(e+d_m), M_m>=ceil(alpha) sampled signs, and sum_m[d_m^{-A}+exp(-I(R,theta)n_m/log^2(e+d_m))+d_m^{-alpha}]<infty, almost surely over matrices and signs all but finitely many dimensions contain a sampled sign that centers a radius c log(e+d_m) Hamming ball of O(sqrt(n_m))-good signings. In the relative-excess form p_d(a)<=L/log^2(e+d), the Chernoff term is exp(-L h(eta)n_m/log^2(e+d_m)); along all integer dimensions the log^3 threshold also gives actual sampled robust certificates once A>1 and alpha>1.

CURRENT paper14 almost-sure Chernoff profile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-density is the profile/signing/moment analogue of the Chernoff search Borel transfer. Under p_{d_m}(a)<=theta/log^2(e+d_m) and sum_m[d_m^{-A}+exp(-I(R,theta)n_m/log^2(e+d_m))]<infty, almost surely all but finitely many arrays satisfy the realization-normalized edge profile O(n_m/log(e+d_m)), hence the independent-random-sign tail, sign-averaged edge maximum, deterministic signing certificate, and scalar edge-grid moment package. The relative-excess form p_d(a)<=L/log^2(e+d) needs sum exp(-L h(eta)n_m/log^2(e+d_m))<infty, and along all integer dimensions gives eventual profile/signing/moment certificates once n(d)>=q log^3(e+d) with q>1/(L h(eta)).

CURRENT paper14 sparse Chernoff Borel-window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-window makes the large-sample bounded-density Borel-Cantelli regime explicit along stretched-exponential dimension schedules. Under p_d(a)<=L/log^2(e+d) and d_m>=exp(c m^kappa), the full eventual profile/signing/scalar-moment package and the full eventual random-search/robust-center package follow if either n_m>=C log^{2+rho}(e+d_m) for any rho>0 or the critical-log condition n_m>=q log^2(e+d_m) log(e+m) with q>1/(L h(eta)) holds. Thus sparse schedules need only a superquadratic logarithmic sample window, compared with the all-integer log^3 threshold.

CURRENT paper14 sparse sampled Chernoff Borel-window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-window-sampled combines the sampled Chernoff Borel theorem with the stretched-exponential dimension-window corollary. This removes the all-integer alpha>1 restriction on sparse Chernoff schedules.

CURRENT paper14 critical-log Chernoff Borel addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-chernoff-borel-critical extracts the general schedule criterion behind the all-dimensions and stretched-exponential consequences. Under p_{d_m}(a)<=L/log^2(e+d_m), summable auxiliary matrix losses sum d_m^{-A}<infty, and n_m>=q log^2(e+d_m) log(e+m) with q>1/(L h(eta)), almost surely all but finitely many arrays satisfy both the profile/signing/scalar-moment package and the random-search/robust-center package. CURRENT paper14 expected-normalized all-sign corollary (local, not yet pushed): new cor:expected-all-signs upgrades thm:expected-full in the logarithmic sample window n<=lambda log d. With probability at least 1-d^{-L}, every sign pattern simultaneously satisfies ||sum_i eps_i A_i||_op <= C_{L,lambda,K} sqrt n in the expected-normalized full Toeplitz/Hankel model. Thus at logarithmic sample size the expected-normalized endpoint is not merely fixed-sign good; the whole signing cube is uniformly harmless.
CURRENT paper14 expected-normalized polynomial-list corollary (local, not yet pushed): new cor:expected-sign-list proves the common generalization behind the all-sign logarithmic window. Any deterministic sign list S with |S|<=d^alpha is uniformly harmless with probability at least 1-d^{-L}: max_{eps in S} ||sum_i eps_i A_i||_op <= C_{L,alpha,K} sqrt n. The same holds for a random sign list independent of the matrices with only E|S|<=d^alpha, by conditioning on the realized list and averaging the fixed-sign union bound; the earlier almost-sure cardinality version is a special case. The all-sign statement is the specialization |S|=2^n<=d^{lambda log 2}.
CURRENT paper14 expected-normalized sign-entropy addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:expected-sign-entropy sharpens the sign-list package from polynomial cardinality to a continuous entropy bound. In the expected-normalized full Toeplitz/Hankel model, any deterministic sign family S with |S|<=M, and any independent random sign family with E|S|<=M, is uniformly controlled with probability at least 1-d^{-L} by C_K sqrt(n(L+1+log(eM)/log(e+d))). Hence the whole signing cube has scale C_K sqrt(n(L+1+n/log(e+d))) for all n, and the previous all-sign O(sqrt n) result is exactly the n=O(log d) specialization.

CURRENT paper14 edge-profile Hamming-stability addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:edge-profile-hamming records robustness of the deterministic edge-profile certificate. If a sign vector eps has edge maximum R and eps' differs in at most r coordinates, then the edge maximum for eps' is <=R+2 sqrt(r) V, where V^2=max_j sum_i |v_i(j)|^2. Under the variance profile V^2<=C n/log(e+d), a random sign vector is, with probability at least 1-d^{-A}, the center of a Hamming ball of radius c_{A,C} log(e+d) on which every signing still has edge maximum O_{A,C}(sqrt n). Thus the realization-normalized empirical profile gives not only an optimized certificate but logarithmically robust families of certificates.
VERIFY addendum: pdflatex passed after the paper14 Hamming-stable edge-profile corollary cor:edge-profile-hamming and summary updates (36pp), plus the master-index rebuild (10pp).
