CURRENT paper14 edge-profile random-search addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:edge-profile-random-search packages the randomized algorithmic consequence of the variance profile. For fixed edge vectors with V^2=max_j sum_i |v_i(j)|^2, M independent random sign vectors obey max_{s<=M,j}|sum_i delta_i^{(s)}v_i(j)| <= C V sqrt(log(e+d)+log(eM)+u) with probability >=1-e^{-u}. Under V^2<=C0 n/log(e+d), this is C sqrt(n(1+(log(eM)+u)/log(e+d))). Also, M independent random trials amplify the O(sqrt n) success failure from d^{-A} to d^{-AM}. Thus the edge-profile event gives not just existential signing but a short randomized search certificate with tunable confidence.
VERIFY addendum: pdflatex passed after the paper14 edge-profile random-search corollary cor:edge-profile-random-search and summary updates (37pp), plus the master-index rebuild (11pp). CURRENT paper14 edge-profile success-count addendum (local, not yet pushed): cor:edge-profile-random-search now also counts how many sampled signs are good. If G_M is the number of the M independent random sign vectors whose edge maximum is <=C_{A,C0}sqrt n under V^2<=C0 n/log(e+d), then for every 1<=r<=M, P{G_M<=M-r}<=binom(M,r)d^{-Ar}. The all-fail d^{-AM} amplification is the r=M case; the stronger form says random search produces a binomial-tail abundance of certificates, not just one certificate with high probability.
VERIFY addendum: pdflatex passed after the paper14 edge-profile success-count extension to cor:edge-profile-random-search and summary updates (37pp), plus the master-index rebuild (11pp). CURRENT paper14 sampled robust-center addendum (local, not yet pushed): cor:edge-profile-hamming now also has a random-search abundance form. Under V^2<=C0 n/log(e+d), if H_M counts the number of M independent random signs that are centers of radius floor(c_{A,C0}log(e+d)) Hamming balls on which every signing has edge maximum O_{A,C0}(sqrt n), then P{H_M<=M-r}<=binom(M,r)d^{-Ar} for every 1<=r<=M. Thus the edge profile supplies many robust local certificate neighborhoods with the same binomial failure-count tail as the non-robust sampled certificate.

CURRENT paper14 fixed-edge moment identity (local, not yet pushed): new prop:fixed-edge-fourth proves the exact Rademacher-sign identity E_eps |sum_i eps_i a_i|^4 = 2(sum_i |a_i|^2)^2 + |sum_i a_i^2|^2 - 2 sum_i |a_i|^4 for complex edge scalars. Applied to a fixed Fourier node, this identifies the variance profile, the phase-square statistic, and the diagonal correction as the fourth-moment data; the remaining moment-method route is therefore an all-edge uniform control problem for the phase-square and higher pairing analogues, not a missing fixed-node calculation.
CURRENT paper14 phase-square cancellation (local, not yet pushed): new prop:expected-phase-square proves that in the expected-normalized full Toeplitz model, away from the real edge nodes, max_{omega^d=1, omega^2!=1} |sum_i v_i(omega)^2| <= C sqrt(n/log(e+d)) with high probability whenever n<=d and n>=C log(e+d). Consequently the fixed-edge fourth moment is <=2 V_omega^4 + C n/log(e+d) at every non-real edge node. This removes the phase-square term as an expected-normalized fourth-moment obstruction; what remains for a moment method is all-edge/all-order control of higher pairing analogues in the realization-normalized model.
CURRENT paper14 fourth-moment profile (local, not yet pushed): new cor:expected-fourth-profile combines expected-profile, fixed-edge-fourth, and expected-phase-square to prove max_{omega^d=1} E_eps |sum_i eps_i v_i(omega)|^4 <= C (n/log(e+d))^2 with high probability in the expected-normalized model, plus the non-real refinement E_eps|...|^4 - 2(sum_i |v_i(omega)|^2)^2 <= C n/log(e+d). Thus the fixed-frequency fourth-moment layer is now packaged uniformly over the edge grid; the unsolved moment route starts at higher pairings and realization normalization.
CURRENT paper14 sixth-moment profile (local, not yet pushed): new prop:fixed-edge-sixth proves the exact Rademacher-sign identity E_eps |sum_i eps_i a_i|^6 = 6A_2^3+9A_2|B_2|^2-18A_2A_4-12 Re(B_{2,1} conjugate(B_2))+16A_6, where A_2=sum |a_i|^2, B_2=sum a_i^2, A_4=sum |a_i|^4, B_{2,1}=sum a_i^2|a_i|^2, and A_6=sum |a_i|^6. It also records the perturbative criterion |E|S|^6-6A_2^3| <= (34 eta+21 eta^2)A_2^3 when |B_2|<=eta A_2 and A_4<=eta A_2^2, and the universal bound E|S|^6<=61A_2^3. New lem:expected-entry proves max_{i,omega^d=1}|v_i(omega)|<=C with high probability in the expected-normalized model. New cor:expected-sixth-profile combines this with expected-profile and phase-square cancellation to prove max_{omega^d=1} E_eps |sum_i eps_i v_i(omega)|^6 <= C (n/log(e+d))^3 with high probability, and away from the real edge nodes refines the leading term to E_eps|sum_i eps_i v_i(omega)|^6=6V_omega^6+O((n/log(e+d))^2), uniformly. Thus the fixed-node sixth layer is no longer a hidden calculation; remaining moment-method work is genuinely all-frequency/all-order and realization-normalized.
CURRENT paper14 scalar even-moment profile (local, not yet pushed): new cor:expected-even-profile uses the deterministic prop:edge-moment-profile plus expected-profile to prove that on the same high-probability expected-normalized edge-profile event, simultaneously for every integer k>=1, max_{omega^d=1} E_eps |sum_i eps_i v_i(omega)|^{2k} <= (C k n/log(e+d))^k. The normalizer-tail theorem now records the identical scalar moment conclusion for realization-normalized samples on its high-probability matrix event. Thus every fixed scalar edge-grid sign moment has the sub-Gaussian size forced by the variance profile; what remains open is the realization-normalized all-frequency/all-order matrix-pairing problem, not a fixed scalar edge moment estimate.
CURRENT paper14 max-edge moment bridge (local, not yet pushed): new cor:edge-max-moment-profile proves E_eps max_j |sum_i eps_i v_i(j)|^{2k} <= d(CkV^2)^k under the deterministic edge-profile notation. Under V^2<=C0 n/log(e+d), choosing k~log d gives an L_{2k} edge-maximum bound O(sqrt n), and Markov recovers the high-probability edge-control criterion. Thus the scalar moment method reaches the finite edge grid once the variance profile is known; the remaining moment route is not a scalar edge-maximum issue.
CURRENT paper14 signing certificate (local, not yet pushed): new cor:edge-profile-certificate proves E_eps max_j |sum_i eps_i v_i(j)| <= C V sqrt(log(e+d)) and hence min_eps max_j |sum_i eps_i v_i(j)| <= C V sqrt(log(e+d)). Under the edge profile V^2<=C0 n/log(e+d), a deterministic sign vector with edge maximum O(sqrt n) exists. The normalizer-tail theorem now records this conditional expectation/existence conclusion on the same high-probability matrix event, in addition to the random-sign tail and all scalar moments.
CURRENT paper14 expected-normalized edge-maximum moment package (local, not yet pushed): new cor:expected-edge-max-moment-package packages the expected-normalized scalar even-moment profile into an all-edge statement on the same high-probability matrix event. It proves E_eps max_{omega^d=1}|sum_i eps_i v_i(omega)|^{2k} <= d(C k n/log(e+d))^k for every k>=1, hence for k>=log(e+d) the L_{2k} edge maximum is <= C sqrt(n k/log(e+d)); in particular the sign-average edge maximum is O(sqrt n), every B>0 has a d^{-B} sign-tail at threshold C_B sqrt n, and a deterministic sign vector with O(sqrt n) edge maximum exists. This closes the expected-normalized scalar moment bridge from fixed-node estimates to finite edge-grid signing; the remaining moment route is genuinely realization-normalized and higher-pairing/all-frequency.
CURRENT paper14 expected-normalized sampled robust-certificate addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:expected-sampled-robust-certificates applies the deterministic edge-profile random-search and Hamming-stability machinery on the same expected-profile matrix event. Conditional on that event, any M independent sampled sign vectors have edge-grid maximum <= C sqrt(n(1+(log(eM)+u)/log(e+d))) except with probability e^{-u}; if M>=ceil(B), at least one sampled sign is a logarithmic-radius Hamming-robust O(sqrt n) center with failure at most d^{-B}; and the number of robust centers in a sampled batch has the binomial tail binom(M,r)d^{-Sr}. Thus the expected-normalized random Toeplitz/Hankel edge theorem now has not only moment/sign-tail/deterministic existence, but also explicit finite randomized certificates and robust-center abundance on the same high-probability matrix event.

CURRENT paper14 inverse-moment normalizer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-invmoment gives a practical sufficient condition for the dyadic low-normalizer budget. The one-matrix dyadic depth mu_{d,a}^{dy} is bounded by an absolute constant times the truncated inverse-square mass E[1_{||T_d||op<aN_d} min{(aN_d/||T_d||op)^2, ell}], ell=log(e+d). Hence the full dyadic edge-profile/signing/sign-averaged maximum/deterministic-certificate/scalar-moment package holds with matrix failure C d^{-A}+C ell nu_{d,a}^{(2)}/R, and if C nu_{d,a}^{(2)}<=theta/ell it has the Chernoff-scale exception exp(-c n/ell^2). More generally inverse p-moments control the same criterion with the explicit factor ell^{(2-p)_+/2}. This gives a new route for realization-normalized random Toeplitz/Hankel Matrix Spencer bounds when negative normalizer moments are available but a pointwise power lower-tail curve is not.

CURRENT paper14 finite inverse-moment certificate addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-invmoment-search pushes the inverse-moment dyadic-tail criterion through the finite randomized-certificate machinery. O_B(1) sampled signs return a robust certificate with only an additional d^{-B} failure. If C_* nu_{d,a}^{(2)}<=theta/ell, the same package uses the Bernstein matrix exception d^{-A}+exp(-c n/ell^2), and inverse p-moment estimates inherit the same explicit ell^{(2-p)_+/2} loss.

CURRENT paper14 inverse-moment abundance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-invmoment-abundance gives the finite abundance/fraction/batch layer for the inverse-moment route. Under the same matrix exception C d^{-A}+C ell nu_{d,a}^{(2)}/R, a sampled list has the entropy-sensitive maximum, at least M-rho M good signs, and at least M-rho M logarithmic Hamming-stable robust centers outside e^{-u}+2 binom(M,rho M)d^{-B rho M}. Fixed O_{rho,S}(1) lists certify a 1-rho robust fraction with added d^{-S}, while M<=d^beta sampled batches are entirely robust with added d^{-sigma}. The Bernstein branch replaces the matrix part by d^{-A}+exp(-c n/ell^2), and inverse p-moment bounds inherit the same ell^{(2-p)_+/2} loss.

CURRENT paper14 finite inverse-moment vanishing-fraction addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-invmoment-vanishing-fractions is the finite-size arbitrary-batch counterpart of the inverse-moment Borel vanishing-fraction theorem. The large-sample Bernstein and inverse p-moment replacements are inherited from the finite inverse-moment abundance theorem.

CURRENT paper14 inverse-moment operator-norm robust-batch addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:edge-to-operator-robust records the deterministic robust form of the circulant reduction, transferring any O(sqrt n) edge-grid Hamming ball to an O(sqrt n) realization-normalized full Toeplitz/Hankel operator-norm robust certificate whenever d/n->infty and log d=o(n). CURRENT paper14 general operator-norm Borel robust-batch transfer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:borel-edge-to-operator-batches packages the robust edge-to-operator reduction for every almost-sure sampled-count branch. Whenever a Borel theorem supplies at least M_m-r_m+1 sampled signs whose Hamming balls have O(sqrt(n_m)) edge-grid maximum, and d_m/n_m->infty with log d_m=o(n_m), the same centers and radii are O(sqrt(n_m)) robust certificates for the realization-normalized full Toeplitz and full Hankel operator-norm discrepancy problems. CURRENT paper14 convex-concentrated sampled operator-certificate addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:convex-normalizers-sampled-operator combines the convex-concentration lower-normalizer tail with the finite random-search/Hamming-stability theorem and the deterministic edge-to-operator transfer. For any triangular sequence n_m<=d_m with d_m/n_m->infty and log d_m=o(n_m), O_B(1) sampled signs contain, with probability tending to one, a logarithmic-radius Hamming ball on which every sign is an O(sqrt(n_m)) realization-normalized full Toeplitz and full Hankel operator-norm certificate. Quantitatively, the pre-transfer edge-certificate failure is C d_m^{-A}+log^2(e+d_m)(e+d_m)^{-beta}+d_m^{-B}. This makes the Gaussian/Rademacher/bounded convex-concentrated full-bandwidth theorem constructive by constant-size randomized search, up to the already deterministic robust reduction.

CURRENT paper14 convex-concentrated operator-certificate abundance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:convex-normalizers-operator-abundance pushes the convex-concentration lower-normalizer tail through the finite robust-fraction and polynomial-batch search theorems, then through the deterministic robust edge-to-operator transfer. CURRENT paper14 strongly log-concave coefficient-law addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:strongly-log-concave-normalizers specializes the convex-concentration normalizer theorem to unbounded strongly log-concave real and complex coefficient laws. If the real density is proportional to exp(-V) with V''>=rho>0, or the complex law on R^2 has Hess V>=rho I and variance normalization, then Bakry-Emery/log-Sobolev concentration supplies the dimension-free convex concentration input. Hence the realization-normalized full Toeplitz/Hankel Matrix Spencer bound O_rho(sqrt n) holds when n<=d<=e^{o(n)} and d/n->infty, and the constant-size sampled operator certificate, robust-fraction/polynomial-batch abundance, and almost-sure sampled-batch schedule corollaries all apply to these strongly log-concave laws.

CURRENT paper14 finite-convolution coefficient-law addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:convex-convolution-normalizers proves a closure theorem for the one-matrix Toeplitz normalizer tail. A fixed finite independent linear combination of centered sub-Gaussian coefficient laws that each satisfy dimension-free convex concentration, after variance normalization, has P{||T_d||op < a E||T_d||op} <= C(1+d)^(-beta). The proof uses a Doob/telescoping conditional-concentration argument for the Toeplitz norm as a separately Lipschitz convex function of each input coefficient block, then Meckes' lower bound. Consequently the realization-normalized full Toeplitz/Hankel Matrix Spencer bound O(sqrt n), constant-size sampled operator certificates, robust-fraction and polynomial-batch abundance, and almost-sure sampled-batch schedules all extend to examples such as Gaussian plus Rademacher noise, Gaussian plus bounded noise, and strongly-log-concave plus bounded noise.

CURRENT paper14 heterogeneous lower-normalizer library addendum (local, not yet pushed): new thm:heterogeneous-normalizer-library removes the identical-coefficient-law restriction from the realization-normalized full Toeplitz/Hankel Matrix Spencer theorem. For independent matrices B_i=T_d(xi^{(i)}) whose lag variables are iid inside each matrix but whose laws mu_i may vary with i,d,n, uniform centered variance-one sub-Gaussian bounds plus a uniform polynomial one-matrix lower-normalizer tail P{||T_d(mu_i)||op < a E||T_d(mu_i)||op} <= L(1+d)^(-beta) imply the same edge profile max_omega sum_i |P_i(omega)|^2/||B_i||op^2 = O(n/log d), hence O(sqrt n) discrepancy when n<=d<=e^{o(n)} and d/n->infty. The proof replaces the expected profile by a non-identical Bernstein argument with scales N_{d,i}=E||T_d(mu_i)||op, uses the uniform lower-tail bound only to control the bad-normalizer count, and keeps the same root-value/Hilbert-Schmidt floor and edge-to-operator transfer. Thus matrices may be sampled heterogeneously from any uniform convex-concentrated, strongly-log-concave, finite-convolution, or other polynomial lower-normalizer library; finite matrix-level mixtures are included by conditioning on the chosen laws.

CURRENT paper14 heterogeneous average-normalizer-budget addendum (local, not yet pushed): new thm:heterogeneous-average-normalizer-budget weakens the heterogeneous lower-normalizer theorem from a uniform per-law polynomial tail to an aggregate lower-tail budget. For independent non-identically distributed Toeplitz matrices with centered variance-one sub-Gaussian coefficient laws mu_i, define p_{d,i}(a)=P{||T_d(mu_i)||op < a E||T_d(mu_i)||op} and Pi_{d,n}(a)=sum_i p_{d,i}(a). If Pi_{d,n}(a) min{1,log^2(e+d)/n}->0, then the realization-normalized edge profile max_omega sum_i |P_i(omega)|^2/||B_i||op^2=O(n/log d) holds with high probability; hence the O(sqrt n) Toeplitz/Hankel Matrix Spencer conclusion follows when d/n->infty. The proof keeps the non-identical expected-normalized Bernstein profile and uses only Markov on the aggregate bad-normalizer count, so sparse bad-law contamination and nonuniform lower-tail quality are allowed. The heterogeneous sampled-certificate conclusions also hold with the matrix failure term replaced by Pi_m(a)min{1,log^2(e+d_m)/n_m}, and the almost-sure sampled-batch schedule uses the corresponding summability condition.

CURRENT paper14 heterogeneous dyadic-normalizer-budget addendum (local, not yet pushed): new thm:heterogeneous-dyadic-normalizer-budget upgrades the average-budget theorem from a single cutoff count to aggregate dyadic depth. For independent non-identically distributed Toeplitz matrices with centered variance-one sub-Gaussian laws mu_i, define Y_{d,i,a}^{dy} by dyadic bins below a E||T_d(mu_i)||op, with weight 4^{r+1} in the r-th bin and weight log(e+d) in the Hilbert-Schmidt-floor bin, and put Delta_{d,n}^{dy}(a)=sum_i E Y_{d,i,a}^{dy}. If log(e+d) Delta_{d,n}^{dy}(a)/n ->0, then the realization-normalized edge profile is O(n/log d) with high probability, hence the O(sqrt n) Toeplitz/Hankel Matrix Spencer conclusion follows when d/n->infty. Quantitatively the matrix failure is C d^{-A}+log(e+d) Delta_{d,n}^{dy}(a)/(R n), and if Delta_{d,n}^{dy}(a)<=theta n/log(e+d) with theta<R it improves to C d^{-A}+exp(-c n/log^2(e+d)). The heterogeneous sampled operator-certificate package inherits the same Markov or Bernstein dyadic-budget replacement. This allows top-scale low-normalizer contamination at density o(1/log d), rather than the o(1/log^2 d) cutoff-count density.

CURRENT paper14 heterogeneous inverse-moment normalizer-budget addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:heterogeneous-invmoment-normalizer-budget makes the heterogeneous dyadic budget checkable from aggregate truncated inverse-normalizer moments. For non-identically distributed coefficient laws mu_i, define nu_{d,i,a}^{(2)}=E[1_{X_{d,i}<aN_{d,i}} min{(aN_{d,i}/X_{d,i})^2,log(e+d)}] with X_{d,i}=||T_d(mu_i)||op and N_{d,i}=E X_{d,i}. If log(e+d) sum_i nu_{d,i,a}^{(2)}/n ->0, then the heterogeneous realization-normalized edge profile is O(n/log d), hence the O(sqrt n) full Toeplitz/Hankel Matrix Spencer conclusion follows when d/n->infty. Quantitatively the matrix failure is C d^{-A}+C log(e+d) sum_i nu_{d,i,a}^{(2)}/(R n), with the Bernstein replacement C d^{-A}+exp(-c n/log^2(e+d)) when C sum_i nu_{d,i,a}^{(2)}<=theta n/log(e+d). Aggregate inverse p-moments also suffice with the explicit loss log(e+d)^{(2-p)_+/2}, giving condition log(e+d)^{1+(2-p)_+/2} sum_i I_{d,i,a}^{(p)}/n ->0. The sampled operator-certificate package inherits the same replacements.

CURRENT paper14 random-environment inverse-p batch-maximal schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-environment-invp-operator-batch-maxima-schedules allows the coefficient laws in the heterogeneous inverse-p sampled-list theorem to be selected by an arbitrary random environment before the matrices and sampled signs are drawn. Thus all-integer alpha_m=O(m^{-B}), B>1; polynomial alpha_m=O(d_m^{-B}), B kappa>1; and stretched-exponential alpha_m=O(ell_m^{-B}), B>1+(2-p)_+/2+1/kappa, give the same arbitrary-batch fixed robust fractions, vanishing discarded fractions, and all-robust polynomial batches with whole-list Toeplitz/Hankel operator maximum O_{theta,q}(sqrt(n_m)). This covers random law selection and random sparse contamination patterns without independence assumptions on the environment.

CURRENT paper14 random-environment inverse-p expectation/catalog criterion addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-environment-invp-expectation-schedules turns the random-environment empirical inverse-p tail hypothesis into expectation tests. With beta_m=E[I_m^{(p)}(a)/n_m] and sigma_p=1+(2-p)_+/2, the all-integer expectation test is sum m^B beta_m<infty for some B>1, in particular beta_m=O(m^{-B_*}) with B_*>2; the polynomial test is sum d_m^B beta_m<infty for some B kappa>1, in particular beta_m=O(d_m^{-B_*}) with B_* kappa>2; and the stretched-exponential test is sum ell_m^B beta_m<infty for some B>sigma_p+1/kappa, in particular beta_m=O(ell_m^{-B_*}) with B_*>sigma_p+2/kappa. For countable random coefficient-law catalogs, beta_m equals n_m^{-1} sum_i sum_j pi_{m,i,j} iota_{m,i,j}^{(p)}(a), so marginal catalog weights alone verify random sparse contamination and random mixture schedules, with no independence among environment selections.

CURRENT paper14 independent bounded-catalog inverse-p Bernstein addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-environment-invp-bernstein-catalog-schedules sharpens the random-environment catalog criterion when the selected law indices are independent and the catalog inverse-p weights Z_{m,i}=iota_{m,i,Lambda_{m,i}}^{(p)}(a) are bounded by L_m. If beta_m=n_m^{-1} sum_i E Z_{m,i}<=alpha_m/2 and sum exp(-c_B n_m alpha_m/L_m)<infty, then the empirical inverse-p budget is at most alpha_m eventually almost surely, so any deterministic inverse-p schedule for alpha_m gives the same robust-fraction, vanishing-fraction, and all-robust polynomial-batch Toeplitz/Hankel conclusions. In concrete polynomial schedules, beta_m<=c d_m^{-B}, B kappa>1, plus n_m>=Q L_m d_m^B log(e+m) suffices; in stretched-exponential schedules, beta_m<=c ell_m^{-B}, B>1+(2-p)_+/2+1/kappa, plus n_m>=Q L_m ell_m^B log(e+m) suffices. CURRENT paper14 sample-size end-to-end active-prefix schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-environment-invp-end-to-end-auto-active-sample-sizes replaces the aggregate catalog moment series in the concrete end-to-end schedules by lower bounds on n_m under simple growth envelopes for H_m^{cat}. Stretched-exponential polynomial-active schedules with H_m^{cat}<=d_m^{eta_cat} use n_m >= d_m^{eta_cat/(s_cat-1)}ell_m^{(B s_cat+s_cat-1+1/kappa+eps)/(s_cat-1)}, while logarithmic-active schedules with H_m^{cat}<=ell_m^{eta_cat} use n_m >= ell_m^{(B s_cat+eta_cat+1/kappa+eps)/(s_cat-1)}log(e+m). Whenever these lower bounds are compatible with n_m<=d_m, d_m/n_m->infty, and log d_m=o(n_m), the same fixed-fraction, vanishing-fraction, and all-robust polynomial-batch certificates hold for every random active stream r<=N_m eventually.

CURRENT paper14 compatible end-to-end active-prefix sample-window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-environment-invp-end-to-end-auto-active-compatible-windows identifies when the sample-size lower bounds can coexist with n_m<=d_m, d_m/n_m->infty, and log d_m=o(n_m). For real catalog exponent gamma with H_m^{cat}<=m^gamma, all-integer windows exist when gamma<-s_cat(B-1)-2, using Q_n m^rho log(e+m)<=n_m<=m^{rho'} for max{0,(B s_cat+gamma+1)/(s_cat-1)}<rho<rho'<1. For polynomial schedules with H_m^{cat}<=d_m^gamma, windows exist when gamma<s_cat-1-B s_cat-1/kappa, using Q_n d_m^rho ell_m<=n_m<=d_m^{rho'} above the corresponding threshold. Stretched-exponential polynomial-active schedules only need gamma<s_cat-1 for H_m^{cat}<=d_m^gamma, using d_m^rho ell_m windows, while stretched-exponential logarithmic-active schedules with H_m^{cat}<=ell_m^gamma admit compatible windows for every real gamma by taking n_m between Q_n ell_m^r log(e+m) and d_m^{rho'}, with r>max{1,(B s_cat+gamma+1/kappa)/(s_cat-1)}. Thus dense all-integer/polynomial schedules require sufficiently decaying aggregate catalog moments, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules retain large feasible windows.

CURRENT paper14 per-stream catalog-budget compatible-window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-environment-invp-end-to-end-auto-active-per-stream-catalog-windows converts the aggregate H_m^{cat} exponent in the compatible active-prefix windows into the active-prefix exponent plus a uniform per-stream row-catalog exponent. With bar h_m^{*,J}=max_{r<=J_m} n_m^{-1}sum_i h_{m,i}^{(r)}, H_m^{cat}<=J_m bar h_m^{*,J}. Thus all-integer schedules with bar h_m^{*,J}<=m^theta require Gamma+theta<-s_cat(B-1)-2 and admit Q_n m^rho log(e+m)<=n_m<=m^{rho'}; polynomial schedules require Gamma+theta<s_cat-1-Bs_cat-1/kappa and admit Q_n d_m^rho ell_m<=n_m<=d_m^{rho'}; stretched-exponential polynomial-active schedules require Gamma+theta<s_cat-1; logarithmic-active stretched-exponential schedules admit compatible windows for every real theta with n_m between Q_n ell_m^r log(e+m) and d_m^{rho'}, r>max{1,(B s_cat+Gamma+theta+1/kappa)/(s_cat-1)}. This makes the active-prefix menu-size cost explicit at the per-stream catalog-budget level.

CURRENT paper14 first-moment-tuned per-stream compatible-window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-environment-invp-end-to-end-auto-active-mean-tuned-windows removes the remaining manual mean-budget/B choice from the per-stream active-prefix windows. With bar b_m^{*,J}=max_{r<=J_m} n_m^{-1}sum_i b_{m,i}^{(r)} and bar h_m^{*,J}=max_{r<=J_m} n_m^{-1}sum_i h_{m,i}^{(r)}, the corollary assumes bar b_m^{*,J} decays with exponent beta and bar h_m^{*,J} grows with exponent theta, then chooses the inverse-p schedule exponent B inside the legal interval. All-integer schedules are certified when beta>1 and Gamma+theta<-2, choosing 1<B<beta with Gamma+theta<-s_cat(B-1)-2. Polynomial schedules are certified when beta>1/kappa and Gamma+theta<s_cat(1-1/kappa)-1-1/kappa, choosing 1/kappa<B<beta with Gamma+theta<s_cat-1-Bs_cat-1/kappa. Stretched-exponential polynomial-active schedules need beta>sigma_p+1/kappa and Gamma+theta<s_cat-1; logarithmic-active stretched-exponential schedules need beta>sigma_p+1/kappa and allow every real theta after taking r>max{1,(B s_cat+Gamma+theta+1/kappa)/(s_cat-1)}. The result leaves the user-facing checks as per-stream first and s_cat-moment catalog rates plus the activation moment rates and the sample-window inequalities.

CURRENT paper14 bounded Gaussian scale-mixture coefficient-law addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:bounded-gaussian-scale-mixture-normalizers proves the one-matrix lower-normalizer tail for centered Gaussian scale mixtures xi=Sg/(E S^2)^{1/2} with 0<s_-<=S<=s_+<infty. Conditioning on the volatility field sigma, F_sigma(g)=||T_d(sigma_k g_k)||op is s_+ sqrt(2d)-Lipschitz, while sigma_k^2=s_-^2+tau_k^2 and Jensen give E_g F_sigma>=s_- E||T_d(g)||op>=c s_- sqrt(d log d). The usual upper bound gives E||T_d(xi)||op<=C s_+ sqrt(d log d), so Gaussian concentration yields P{||T_d(xi)||op<a E||T_d(xi)||op}<=C(1+d)^(-beta), with constants depending only on s_+/s_-. Hence realization-normalized full Toeplitz/Hankel Matrix Spencer, sampled operator certificates, robust fractions, polynomial-batch abundance, almost-sure sampled-batch schedules, and heterogeneous-library consequences extend to bounded Gaussian variance mixtures that need not be log-concave or visibly convex-concentrated.

CURRENT paper14 matrix-level radial-scaling addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:matrix-level-radial-normalization records the pathwise invariance of every realization-normalized conclusion under arbitrary positive scalar matrix amplitudes. If A_i is any covered full Toeplitz/Hankel array and R_i>0 are arbitrary random variables on the same probability space, with no independence, boundedness, identical-distribution, moment, or tail assumption, then (R_i A_i)/||R_i A_i||op=A_i/||A_i||op. Therefore all O(sqrt n) discrepancy, sampled operator-certificate, robust-fraction, polynomial-batch, and almost-sure sampled-batch conclusions for the normalized arrays remain valid after multiplying the matrices by positive heavy-tailed radial amplitudes.

CURRENT paper14 profile-normalized independent-lag addendum (local, not yet pushed): new thm:profile-normalized-fixed-sign proves fixed-sign O(sqrt n) bounds for full Toeplitz/Hankel arrays whose lags are independent centered real or complex sub-Gaussian variables with arbitrary deterministic envelopes sigma_{i,k}. With S_i=(sum_{|k|<d} sigma_{i,k}^2)^{1/2} and A_i=T_d(zeta_{i,k})/(S_i sqrt(log(e+d))) (zero when S_i=0), every deterministic sign vector satisfies ||sum eps_i A_i||op<=C sqrt n with failure <=d^{-L}, and every deterministic polynomial-size sign list is simultaneously controlled at the same scale. The proof uses only the symbol grid, Bernstein for trigonometric polynomials, and the root-value sub-Gaussian norm C sqrt(n/log d), so it covers non-identical lag variances, deterministic masks, missing lags, and triangular variance profiles beyond iid coefficient laws.

CURRENT paper14 profile-normalized sign-entropy addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:profile-normalized-sign-entropy sharpens thm:profile-normalized-fixed-sign from polynomial deterministic sign lists to arbitrary deterministic or matrix-independent random sign families. If |S|<=M, or E|S|<=M in the independent random-list case, then the same profile-normalized independent-lag model satisfies max_{eps in S} ||sum_i eps_i A_i||op <= C_K sqrt(n(L+1+log(eM)/log(e+d))) with probability at least 1-d^{-L}. Taking M=2^n gives the whole signing cube at scale C_K sqrt(n(L+1+n/log(e+d))), and therefore all sign patterns are simultaneously O_{L,K,lambda}(sqrt n) whenever n<=lambda log(e+d). The Hankel form follows by the same reversal.

CURRENT paper14 profile-normalized Borel sign-entropy addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:profile-normalized-sign-entropy-borel turns the profile-normalized sign-family entropy bound into an almost-sure triangular-array result. For profile-normalized independent-lag Toeplitz/Hankel arrays with uniform sub-Gaussian constant K and deterministic or matrix-independent random sign families S_m with E|S_m|<=M_m, summability of sum_m d_m^{-L_m} implies that eventually max_{eps in S_m} ||sum_i eps_i A_{m,i}||op <= C_K sqrt(n_m(L_m+1+log(eM_m)/log(e+d_m))). Taking M_m=2^{n_m} gives an eventual whole-signing-cube bound, and if n_m<=lambda log(e+d_m) with a fixed summable d_m^{-L} tail, every sign pattern is eventually O_{K,L,lambda}(sqrt n_m). No independence across m is needed.

CURRENT paper14 inverse-moment Borel abundance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-invmoment-borel-abundance promotes the finite inverse-moment abundance theorem to triangular arrays. Under summability of d_m^{-A}+ell_m nu_{m,a}^{(2)}/R plus the sign tails e^{-u_m}+binom(M_m,r_m)d_m^{-B_s r_m}, sampled lists eventually have the entropy-sensitive maximum, at least M_m-r_m+1 good signs, and at least M_m-r_m+1 logarithmic Hamming-stable robust centers. Fixed O_{rho,S}(1) lists give eventual 1-rho robust fractions when the additional d_m^{-S} series is summable, and polynomial batches M_m<=d_m^beta are eventually entirely robust when the additional d_m^{-sigma} series is summable. CURRENT paper14 inverse-moment vanishing-fraction addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-invmoment-borel-vanishing-fractions gives the arbitrary-batch vanishing discarded-fraction upgrade for the inverse-moment route. CURRENT paper14 inverse-moment schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-invmoment-borel-schedules extracts concrete rate regimes from the inverse-moment Borel theorem. In each schedule, summable d_m^{-alpha} upgrades the conditional package to actual sampled robust certificates.

CURRENT paper5 strengthening (local, not yet pushed): paper5 now records exact finite-degree endpoint sign identities and a finite-degree parity envelope. For m=2q+1, with E_q=sum_{ell=0}^q xi_{2ell} and O_q=sum_{ell=0}^q xi_{2ell+1}, P{G_m(1)G_m(-1)<0}=1/2(1-P{|E_q|=|O_q|}); in particular the endpoint sign is exactly fair for non-atomic coefficient laws. For Littlewood coefficients at odd degree, with n=(m+1)/2, the exact value is 1/2-binom(2n,n)/4^n+eta_n/2, where eta_n=4^{-n}binom(n,n/2)^2 for even n and 0 for odd n; hence it is 1/2-sqrt(2/(pi(m+1)))+O(m^{-1}). For Littlewood coefficients at even degree m=2q, the exact value is 1/2-theta_q/2, where theta_q=4^{-q}binom(q,q/2)^2 for even q and 0 for odd q; hence exact fairness holds when m=2 mod 4, while m=0 mod 4 gives 1/2-2/(pi m)+O(m^{-2}). For standard real Gaussian coefficients at every degree, P{G_m(1)G_m(-1)<0}=1/2-pi^{-1} arcsin((m+1)^{-1} sum_{j=0}^m (-1)^j), giving the balanced even-degree bias 1/2-pi^{-1}arcsin((m+1)^{-1}). New cor:finite-envelope says that if a_m=P{G_m(1)G_m(-1)<0} and z_m=P{G_m(1)G_m(-1)=0}, then P{nu=k}<=a_m+z_m for odd k and <=1-a_m for even k, while P(stable band r+s=m)<=a_m for odd r and <=1-a_m-z_m for even r. New cor:band-reversal gives exact finite-degree equality of complementary band stability probabilities P(stable for (r,s))=P(stable for (s,r)); under no circle zeros it also records P{nu=k}=P{nu=m-k} and the common stability probability P{nu=r}=P{nu=s}.
CURRENT paper5 finite-cap specialization (local, not yet pushed): new cor:special-envelope packages Gaussian and Littlewood caps explicitly: Gaussian caps are 1/2 +/- alpha_m; odd-degree Littlewood has point-mass cap 1/2+beta_n but stability cap 1/2-beta_n; even-degree Littlewood uses gamma_q=theta_q/2.
CURRENT paper15 curved uniformity refinement (local, not yet pushed): new def:resolved and prop:resolved-uniform isolate the extra geometry needed for curvature-band uniformity near multiply-tangent directions. On subfamilies where iterated slices keep a uniform curvature floor and the projected near-tangent normals have a uniform Gram lower bound, the curved slab lower bound and the UDC interpolation law are uniform with constants depending only on the structure constants plus Gamma and c_sl. New cor:onecut-uniform proves a fully uniform one-curved-cut theorem under a quantitative splaying condition <nu_K,n_1><=-chi: for k=1 the projected-normal Gram condition is automatic and the only slice has uniform curvature, giving the uniform law ell_N ~ (log N/N)^{2/(n+2)} over that curvature-banded class. New cor:onecut-curvdom extends the one-cut uniform theorem to curvature-dominated oblique intersections satisfying c_K-max(<nu_K,n_1>,0)C_g/gamma_0>=chi, so splaying is not the only uniform one-cut mechanism. The remaining curvature-band-uniform theorem is narrowed to one-cut curvature-separation failures outside this dominance regime and genuine multi-tangent degeneration, where the extra resolved constants are not forced by the original admissibility data.
CURRENT paper16 lattice strengthening (local, not yet pushed): paper16 now extends the Rademacher lattice analysis beyond the endpoints. New prop:rademacher-root-unity proves that for every non-real root of unity zeta of order q>=3, the fixed-point small-ball probability satisfies P{|G_m(zeta)-a|<=t} <= C q(1+t)^2/(m+1), by grouping signs by residue classes mod q and using two well-separated residue classes through an invertible real-linear map; fixed q recovers the two-dimensional N^{-1} rational-angle scale. prop:rademacher-root-fixed-radius-lower now proves the matching lower bound at every fixed non-real rational angle with no divisibility restriction: for some constant radius t_q, P{|G_m(zeta)|<=t_q} >= c_q/N for all N=m+1. New cor:rademacher-root-grid union-bounds this over the whole q-th-root grid: away from endpoints P{min_{zeta^q=1, zeta not +-1} |G_m(zeta)|<=t} <= C q^2(1+t)^2/(m+1), with an additional endpoint term C(1+t)/sqrt(m+1). New cor:rademacher-denom-grid union-bounds over all denominators q<=Q, giving C Q^3(1+t)^2/(m+1) away from endpoints plus the same endpoint term. New prop:rademacher-small-denom-lower proves the exact atom local limit at every fixed primitive q-th root: on every admissible residue class, P{G(zeta)=0}=gamma_q N^{-phi(q)/2}+o(N^{-phi(q)/2}) with gamma_q=(2q/pi)^{phi(q)/2}/covol(K), and the atom is zero on inadmissible residue classes. New cor:rademacher-finite-layer-zero sums this over any fixed finite set of cyclotomic orders: the leading exact-zero asymptotic comes only from the first nonempty totient layer, with pairwise intersections lower order. New cor:rademacher-denom-zero promotes this to bounded-denominator exact-zero grids: for fixed Q, non-real denominators <=Q have total zero probability O_Q(N^{-1}) and improve to O_Q(N^{-2}) after removing cyclotomic degree two. New cor:rademacher-denom-zero-layer gives the peeled bounded-denominator coefficient after removing all primitive orders below any fixed cyclotomic degree threshold, by summing the first surviving totient layer. New cor:rademacher-quadratic-atoms makes the degree-two admissible classes and constants explicit: q=3,6 have atoms exactly on 3|N with N P{G(zeta)=0}->2sqrt(3)/pi, and q=4 exactly on 4|N with N P{G(zeta)=0}->4/pi. New cor:rademacher-quadratic-overlaps makes the pairwise degree-two overlap layer explicit: E3∩E6 has N^2 constant 48/pi^2 on 6|N, while E3∩E4 and E4∩E6 have N^2 constant 8sqrt(3)/pi^2 on 12|N. New cor:rademacher-quadratic-second-order sharpens the degree-two single atoms themselves to second order: q=3 and q=6 have second coefficient -2sqrt(3)/pi on 3|N, and q=4 has second coefficient -4/pi on 4|N. New cor:rademacher-denom-zero-leading identifies the full fixed-Q leading exact-zero coefficient c_Q(N)/N+o_Q(N^{-1}): each primitive q=3 and q=6 conjugate pair contributes 2sqrt(3)/pi on 3|N, the q=4 pair contributes 4/pi on 4|N, and higher-degree roots plus overlaps are lower order. New cor:rademacher-degree-four-atoms makes the first nonquadratic layer explicit: primitive q=5 and q=10 have atoms exactly on 5|N with N^2 P{G(zeta)=0}->20sqrt(5)/pi^2, primitive q=8 exactly on 8|N with limit 64/pi^2, and primitive q=12 exactly on 6|N with limit 48/pi^2. New cor:rademacher-denom-zero-degree-four-leading is the D0=2 instance of the peeled formula and gives the full periodic N^{-2} coefficient from primitive orders 5,8,10,12, with all higher orders and overlaps lower order. New cor:rademacher-denom-zero-second-order combines the degree-two second-order corrections, their pairwise overlaps, and the degree-four layer into the full unpeeled fixed-Q expansion c_Q(N)/N+e_Q(N)/N^2+o_Q(N^{-2}). New cor:rademacher-denom-zero-with-endpoints adds z=+-1 and gives the full bounded-denominator root-of-unity exact-zero expansion through order N^{-1}: the exact central-binomial endpoint-union term plus the same non-real coefficient c_Q(N)/N. Thus fixed non-real rational angles have sharp N^{-1} fixed-radius scale, exact atoms are classified, the N^{-1} exact atom floor occurs exactly at cyclotomic degree two (q=3,4,6), the pairwise degree-two N^{-2} overlap constants and single-event second-order terms are explicit, the first nonquadratic N^{-2} layer is explicit, every fixed bounded-denominator peeled layer has a leading coefficient, the unpeeled non-real bounded-denominator exact-zero union is explicit through order N^{-2}, and the endpoint-inclusive bounded-denominator grid is explicit through order N^{-1}. The discrete open problem is narrowed: endpoints have the one-dimensional N^{-1/2} scale, fixed rational grids have order-q/q^2 bounds, bounded-denominator rational angles have the Q^3/N union bound, fixed-rational small balls are sharp, fixed finite-order exact-zero grids now have their first-layer asymptotic and fixed bounded-denominator exact-zero grids have peeled layer coefficients at every fixed cyclotomic threshold plus the unpeeled second-order expansion and endpoint-inclusive first-order expansion, and what remains is a continuum/local-CLT Rademacher boundary theorem above the lattice scale, uniformly over moving arcs, large denominators, and irrational angles.
CURRENT paper16 fixed-root atom refinement (local, not yet pushed): prop:rademacher-small-denom-lower now removes the q|N-only restriction and upgrades scale to a local limit. For every fixed primitive q-th root, P{G(zeta)=0} <= C_q N^{-phi(q)/2} for all N. On each residue class R mod 2q, the atom is identically zero unless the support parity vector intersects the cyclotomic kernel K=ker(sum s_l zeta^l); on admissible classes it satisfies N^{phi(q)/2} P{G(zeta)=0}->(2q/pi)^{phi(q)/2}/covol(K). The old q|N case is always admissible, but the exact-zero classification is now a parity-residue classification rather than only a divisible-subsequence statement. cor:rademacher-finite-layer-zero gives the finite-order union asymptotic by summing the minimal-totient admissible constants and bounding intersections by the combined cyclotomic rank. cor:rademacher-denom-zero-layer specializes this to all fixed bounded-denominator peeled layers: after excluding orders with phi(q)<=D0, the leading coefficient is the sum over the first surviving totient layer. cor:rademacher-quadratic-atoms makes degree-two explicit with constants: primitive q=3 and q=6 roots have atoms exactly when 3|N and satisfy N P{G(zeta)=0}->2sqrt(3)/pi, primitive q=4 roots exactly when 4|N and satisfy N P{G(zeta)=0}->4/pi, and all inadmissible classes have zero atom. cor:rademacher-quadratic-overlaps computes the pairwise degree-two overlap constants: E3∩E6 contributes 48/pi^2 at N^{-2} on 6|N, while E3∩E4 and E4∩E6 contribute 8sqrt(3)/pi^2 at N^{-2} on 12|N. cor:rademacher-quadratic-second-order gives the second-order single-event expansions for q=3,4,6. cor:rademacher-degree-four-atoms makes the degree-four orders explicit: q=5,10 require 5|N and have N^2 constants 20sqrt(5)/pi^2, q=8 requires 8|N and has constant 64/pi^2, and q=12 requires 6|N and has constant 48/pi^2. cor:rademacher-denom-zero adds the fixed bounded-denominator union consequence: O_Q(N^{-1}) total exact-zero probability on non-real roots and O_Q(N^{-2}) after excluding primitive orders 3,4,6. cor:rademacher-denom-zero-leading gives the exact leading fixed-Q coefficient by summing the degree-two conjugate-pair constants and using the explicit pairwise overlap constants as O_Q(N^{-2}) terms; cor:rademacher-denom-zero-degree-four-leading gives the exact first nonquadratic fixed-Q coefficient by summing the degree-four order constants and showing higher-degree orders are o_Q(N^{-2}); cor:rademacher-denom-zero-second-order gives the full unpeeled fixed-Q exact-zero expansion through N^{-2}; cor:rademacher-denom-zero-with-endpoints adds the endpoint union and gives the full bounded-denominator root-of-unity expansion through order N^{-1}.
CURRENT paper16 Gaussian grid strengthening (local, not yet pushed): new prop:gaussian-root-grid gives the exact DFT law on the N=m+1 roots of unity. On one representative from each non-real conjugate pair, the normalized evaluations N^{-1/2}G_m(omega^k) are independent standard complex Gaussians, so P{min_nonreal_grid |G_m|<=t}=1-exp(-floor((N-1)/2)t^2/N), hence the non-real root grid sees the t^2 pointwise scale. It now also gives the exact full-grid survival formula P{min_full_root_grid |G_m|>t}=(1-alpha(t/sqrt N))^{1+1_{2|N}} exp(-floor((N-1)/2)t^2/N), where alpha(x)=P{|Z|<=x} for Z~N(0,1); the real endpoint coordinates are independent DFT coordinates, not just an O(t/sqrt N) union-bound correction. New cor:gaussian-root-grid-rayleigh shows the fixed pointwise scale explicitly: both the non-real and full root-grid minima converge to the Rayleigh law with survival exp(-t^2/2), with errors O_T(1/N) and O_T(N^{-1/2}) on bounded t-intervals; moreover for every fixed p>0, with R_p=2^{p/2}Gamma(1+p/2), E min_nonreal^p=R_p+s_N p R_p/(2N)+O_p(N^{-2}), while E min_full^p=R_p-s_N p 2^{p/2}Gamma((p+1)/2)/(sqrt(pi N))+pR_p(s_N/2+s_N(s_N-1)/pi)/N+O_p(N^{-3/2}); the displayed Rayleigh mean is the case p=1. New cor:gaussian-root-grid-misses now sharpens the continuum-scale separation to the exact grid coefficient: uniformly for bounded tau, P{min_full_root_grid |G_m|<=tau/sqrt N}=(s_N sqrt(2/pi) tau+tau^2/2)/N+O_tau(N^{-2}), with s_N=1+1_{2|N}. Thus the DFT grid has vanishing detection probability at the continuum boundary-minimum scale, with an explicit parity-dependent endpoint contribution. This formalizes the grid-resolution warning: root-grid or coarse-grid scans cannot see the continuum sqrt(m)t curve-crossing law unless the angular mesh resolves the t m^{-3/2} excursion scale.
CURRENT paper16 bounded-density grid strengthening (local, not yet pushed): new cor:subquadratic-grid-misses applies prop:nongaussian-grid at the continuum threshold t=tau/sqrt(N). For deterministic grids Q_m whose non-endpoint points stay >=gamma/m from {+-1}, P{min_{Q_m}|G_m|<=tau/sqrt N} <= C(|Q_m|/N^2+1/N), uniformly for bounded tau and bounded-density coefficients. New cor:random-subquadratic-grid-misses gives the same bound for independent random grids Q_m with E|Q_m| in place of |Q_m|, by conditioning on the sampled grid. Hence deterministic o(N^2) grids, and independent randomized grids with expected size o(N^2), have vanishing detection probability at the continuum boundary-minimum scale. New lem:grid-speed-detection gives the matching deterministic sufficiency under a speed cutoff: on {sup_T |G_m'| <= L m^{3/2}}, any h-net with h <= tau/(2 L m^{3/2} sqrt N), equivalently O_L(N^2/tau) uniformly spaced points, detects a continuum excursion from tau/(2sqrt N) to tau/sqrt N. New cor:gaussian-grid-detects combines this with Salem--Zygmund for Gaussian coefficients: an O_{A,tau}(N^2 sqrt(log N)) grid detects continuum excursions at the N^{-1/2} scale except on an O_A(N^{-A}) exceptional event. New cor:uniform-random-grid-detects adds the independent uniform random-grid upper side: O_{A,L,tau}(N^2 log N) samples detect under a fixed speed cutoff, and O_{A,tau,L_A}(N^2 (log N)^{3/2}) samples detect under the natural sqrt(log N) speed tail, the extra log being the coupon-collector covering overhead. New cor:speedtail-grid-detects gives the same O_{A,tau}(N^2 sqrt(log N)) deterministic-grid detection conclusion for any coefficient law satisfying the natural polynomial derivative-speed tail, hence for the polynomial-moment and subexponential bounded-density classes below. New cor:natural-speed-sampling-bracket packages the sharp sampling bracket under that natural speed tail: subquadratic deterministic or independent randomized grids still see only the pointwise scale, deterministic O_{A,tau}(N^2 sqrt(log N)) grids detect every continuum excursion up to O_A(m^{-A}) miss probability, and independent uniform random grids do the same at O_{A,tau,L_A}(N^2 (log N)^{3/2}). cor:variance-grid-detects now gives the quantitative tail-free variance-only analogue: for every delta>1/2, an O_tau(N^{2+delta}) grid detects continuum N^{-1/2}-scale excursions with miss probability <= C_delta m^{1-2delta}. New cor:variance-grid-fixed-power packages this for fixed powers: every uniform N^beta grid with beta>5/2 has miss probability O_beta(N^{5-2beta}). New cor:variance-grid-polynomial-confidence packages the same input for arbitrary confidence: an O_{A,tau}(N^{(A+5)/2}) grid gives miss probability <= C_A m^{-A}. New cor:grid-sampling-bracket packages the sampling bracket: subquadratic bounded-density grids, deterministic or independent randomized, see only the pointwise scale, while sufficiently fine variance-only polynomial grids are complete for continuum excursions up to arbitrary polynomial failure. Thus quadratic angular resolution is the correct sampling scale up to the natural high-probability sqrt(log N) speed overhead when a natural speed tail is available, with one extra logarithm for iid uniform random sampling, while the variance-only fallback remains a cruder polynomial grid; the curve-crossing/continuum step is genuinely stronger than subquadratic fixed-grid delocalization.
CURRENT paper16 stratified-grid addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:stratified-grid-detects shows that one-point-per-equal-arc stratified grids, including random shifts of a uniform lattice, keep the deterministic sampling complexity. Under the speed cutoff {sup_T |G_m'| <= L m^{3/2}}, M >= C L m^{3/2} sqrt(N)/tau stratified points detect every continuum excursion from tau/(2sqrt N) to tau/sqrt N. Under the natural polynomial derivative-speed tail, M >= C_tau L_A N^2 sqrt(log N) gives O_A(m^{-A}) miss probability. Thus iid uniform sampling pays an extra coupon-collector log, but stratified/random-shifted sampling does not.
CURRENT paper16 moment/variance strengthening (local, not yet pushed): new cor:bd-polymoment-boundary uses Fuk--Nagaev to prove a derivative-speed tail at the natural m^{3/2} sqrt(log m) scale from a finite p-th moment with p>2A+4, giving error O(m^{-A}); consequently uniformly bounded moments of all orders imply hyp:boundary-law. cor:bd-subexp-boundary now states the same polynomial derivative-speed tail explicitly for uniformly subexponential bounded-density laws before deriving hyp:boundary-law. lem:variance-speed now gives the quantitative variance-only cutoff P{sup_T |G_m'| > m^{3/2+delta}} <= C_delta m^{1-2delta} for every delta>1/2. cor:variance-boundary-separation packages the bounded-density consequence with an explicit rate: for every A>1, P{min_T |G_m| <= m^{-A}} <= C_{A,K} m^{-2(A-1)/3}, with no coefficient tail assumption. thm:main has now been promoted to the no-tail bounded-density conditioning theorem with arbitrary prescribed polynomial-in-n exponent after increasing the deterministic threshold n_{0,b}(m): the proof only needs this one polynomial boundary-minimum event, so every bounded-density mean-zero variance-one coefficient law satisfying circle clearance gets the balanced-conditioning conclusion. The remaining continuum non-Gaussian gap is now the full polynomial-scale boundary law / sharp continuum crossing law without a natural-scale speed-tail input, or a local-limit replacement; it is no longer a conditioning-under-clearance gap.
CURRENT paper16 quantitative conditioning addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:quant-main makes the stable-event loss explicit and tunable. If the circle-clearance event has failure probability q_m and gives rho >= (C0 m^2 log^3 m)^{-1}, then for every A0>2, b>0, and deterministic coefficient envelope R_m>0 there is a deterministic threshold n_{0,b,A0,R}(m) such that the balanced polynomial conditioning conclusion holds on {nu=r} outside an event of probability at most q_m + C_{A0,K} m^{-2(A0-1)/3} + (m+1)/R_m^2. Choosing R_m=m^{(D+1)/2} makes the coefficient-size loss O(m^{-D}) for any fixed D, at the cost of changing only the deterministic finite-section threshold. Thus the theorem's o(1) loss decomposes into clearance failure, the tail-free boundary-separation error, and an arbitrarily small polynomial coefficient-size error.
CURRENT paper16 prescribed-loss addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:quant-main-polynomial-loss packages the quantitative conditioning theorem at a user-chosen polynomial confidence level. Under the same quantitative clearance input, for every b>0 and D>0 there are deterministic thresholds n_{0,b,D}(m) and events Omega_{m,D} with P(Omega_{m,D}^c)<=q_m+C_{D,K}m^{-D}; on Omega_{m,D} cap {nu=r}, the balanced symbol has no boundary zeros, zero winding, and all n>=n_{0,b,D}(m) satisfy s_min(T_n)>=n^{-b} and kappa(T_n)<=n^b. The proof chooses A0=1+3(D+1)/2 and R_m=m^{(D+2)/2}, so the boundary-separation and coefficient-envelope losses are both absorbed into O_D(m^{-D}).

CURRENT paper16 conditional stable-event addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:quant-main-conditional converts the prescribed-loss event to the natural conditional-on-zero-winding form. If p_m=P{nu=r}>0, then the event that P_{r,r} has no boundary zeros, zero winding, and all n>=n_{0,b,D}(m) satisfy s_min(T_n)>=n^{-b}, kappa(T_n)<=n^b has conditional probability at least 1-(q_m+C_{D,K}m^{-D})/p_m given {nu=r}. In particular, along any subsequence where P{nu=r} is bounded below, the stable-event conditional failure is q_m+O_D(m^{-D}).

CURRENT paper16 lower-tail transfer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new prop:bulk-secondmoment-transfer isolates the exact remaining Gaussian lower-tail input. On a fixed bulk B_delta at t_N=tau/sqrt(N), if the down-crossing count N_{t_N}^\downarrow(B_delta) has factorial second moment bounded by L times the square of its Kac--Rice mean mu_delta(tau)=(2pi-4delta)tau/sqrt(12pi), then Paley--Zygmund gives P{min_{B_delta}|G_m|<=tau/sqrt(N)} >= mu_delta(tau)/(1+L mu_delta(tau)) in the liminf. In particular, Poisson-scale decorrelation yields the matching small-tau lower-tail constant mu_delta(tau)+O_delta(tau^2). Thus the remaining global lower-tail problem is now precisely the anti-clustering/decorrelation estimate for the bulk down-crossing factorial second moment, not the transfer from that estimate to probability.
VERIFY addendum: pdflatex passed after the paper16 fixed-bulk lower-tail transfer proposition prop:bulk-secondmoment-transfer and summary updates (74pp), plus the master-index rebuild (11pp). Logs have no undefined references/citations or rerun requests, only the known hyperref footnote warning, font-expansion warning, and overfull/underfull boxes.
CURRENT paper16 global lower-tail transfer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:global-secondmoment-transfer removes the fixed-bulk endpoint cutoff at the level of conditional implications. If the same factorial second-moment bound for N_{tau/sqrt(N)}^\downarrow(B_delta) is uniform over fixed bulks as delta->0 with constant L_T, then the whole-circle event satisfies liminf P{min_T |G_m|<=tau/sqrt(N)} >= sqrt(pi/3) tau/(1+L_T sqrt(pi/3) tau). In the uniform Poisson-scale case L_T=1 this gives the small-tau lower-tail expansion sqrt(pi/3) tau - (pi/3) tau^2 + O(tau^3). The only remaining Gaussian lower-tail input is therefore the anti-clustering/decorrelation estimate uniform through the endpoint-cutoff removal.

CURRENT paper14 almost-sure normalizer-tail addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:normalizer-tail-borel packages the quantitative normalizer-tail criterion into a Borel-Cantelli triangular-array theorem. Along any sequence (d_m,n_m) with n_m<=d_m and n_m>=C log(e+d_m), if sum_m [d_m^{-A}+p_{d_m}(a) min(n_m,log^2(e+d_m))] is finite, then almost surely all but finitely many samples have the edge profile O(n_m/log d_m) and hence the signing, sign-averaged maximum, deterministic-certificate, and scalar moment package from cor:normalizer-tail-quant. New cor:normalizer-tail-borel-sparse makes this immediately usable at sparse scales: if p_d(a)<=C d^{-B}, or if p_d(a)<=C log^{-B}(e+d) with B>3, and d_m grows at least exponentially, then the summability condition is automatic for every admissible n_m window C log(e+d_m)<=n_m<=d_m.

CURRENT paper12 compact-Assouad addendum (local, not yet pushed): new def:upper-log-assouad-growth and cor:comm-af-compact-assouad extend the arbitrary compact-metric theorem from global covering profiles to local Assouad data. If Y has upper Assouad growth (A,s), then Holder tuples obey the same n^{s/(2alpha)-1} estimate and compact D-doubling targets are Spencer-scale when log_2 D<=2alpha. If Y has logarithmic upper Assouad growth (A,s,gamma), the bound has n^{s/(2alpha)-1}(log n)^gamma inside the logarithm; at s=2alpha, gamma<=0 remains Spencer-scale and gamma>0 gives only sqrt(n log log n).
CURRENT paper12 compact-equicontinuity addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-compact-equicontinuity extracts the qualitative compact consequence of the entropy-profile theorem. For every compact metric target Y and every sequence of real contraction n-tuples sharing one modulus omega(r)->0, there are scales rho_n with log N_Y(rho_n)=o(n) and omega(16rho_n)=o(1), hence disc(g_{n,1},...,g_{n,n})=o(n). This removes all quantitative entropy-growth assumptions for qualitative sublinear discrepancy under a fixed equicontinuity modulus.
CURRENT paper12 compact-function-class addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-compact-function-class upgrades compact equicontinuity to a hereditary function-class theorem. If F is relatively compact in C(Y) and consists of real contractions on a compact metric target, then Delta_n(F)=sup_{f_1,...,f_n in F} disc(f_1,...,f_n)=o(n). Compactness of the closure supplies the common modulus, so the scales in the compact-equicontinuity proof are uniform over all n-tuples from F.
CURRENT paper12 Banach-precompact addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:precompact-banach-class records the qualitative finite-net principle behind the compact function-class theorem. For any totally bounded K in the unit ball of a normed space E, Delta_n^E(K)<=N_K(eta)+n eta for every eta>0, where N_K uses centers in K; hence Delta_n^E(K)=o(n). The proof signs each finite-net fiber with imbalance at most one and then optimizes eta along a scale sequence. New cor:banach-entropy-rates records the quantitative envelope Delta_n^E(K)<=inf_eta {N_K(eta)+n eta}; polynomial Banach entropy N_K(eta)<=A eta^{-d} gives Delta_n=O(A^{1/(d+1)}n^{d/(d+1)}), pure stretched-exponential Banach entropy N_K(eta)<=exp(A eta^{-q}) gives Delta_n=O_{A,q}(n/(log n)^{1/q}), and signed-log stretched entropy N_K(eta)<=exp(A eta^{-q}(log(e/eta))^gamma) gives Delta_n=O(n(log n)^{-1/q}(log log n)^{gamma/q}). New cor:compact-operator-image applies the same finite-net envelope to compact linear images T(B_X): for ||T||<=1, sup_{||x_i||<=1} min_eps ||sum eps_i T x_i|| = o(n), with rates controlled by the entropy of T(B_X).
CURRENT paper12 dyadic entropy-number addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:compact-operator-entropy-number-rates packages direct internal dyadic entropy data for Banach compact classes and compact operator images. It records Delta_n^E(K)<=2^{k-1}+n e_k^{int}(K), hence e_k^{int}<=A k^{-s} gives Delta_n=O_{A,s}(n(log n)^{-s}), the log-refined bound e_k^{int}<=A k^{-s}(log(e+k))^beta gives the extra (log log n)^beta factor, and exponential dyadic entropy decay e_k^{int}<=A e^{-bk} gives Delta_n=O_{A,b}(n^{log 2/(b+log 2)}). This improves the approximation-number route whenever direct covering entropy numbers are known.

CURRENT paper12 standard entropy-number closure addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-external-entropy-union-sum turns the internal dyadic entropy package into the standard Banach-space entropy-number form. External dyadic entropy numbers satisfy e_k^{int}(K)<=2 e_k(K;E), so the rates from cor:compact-operator-entropy-number-rates apply to the usual entropy numbers. Finite unions obey e_{k+ceil(log_2 r)}(union_l K_l;E)<=max_l e_k(K_l;E), and finite Minkowski sums obey e_{1+sum_l(k_l-1)}(K_1+...+K_r;E)<=sum_l e_{k_l}(K_l;E). Thus polynomial and log-refined dyadic entropy decay, and exponential decay with finite-sum exponent degradation, pass directly to bounded compact class decompositions and their discrepancy rates.

CURRENT paper12 Banach Holder-image entropy addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-holder-image-entropy transfers the dyadic entropy-number discrepancy rates through nonlinear Holder parametrizations. If Phi:K->F is (L,alpha)-Holder on a totally bounded Banach compact class K, then e_k(Phi(K);F)<=L 2^alpha e_k(K;E)^alpha. Hence external entropy decay e_k(K)<=A k^{-s} yields Delta_n^F(Phi(K))=O(n(log n)^{-alpha s}), log-refined decay e_k(K)<=A k^{-s}(log(e+k))^beta adds (log log n)^{alpha beta}, and exponential entropy decay e_k(K)<=A e^{-bk} gives Delta_n=O(n^{log 2/(alpha b+log 2)}). This makes the Banach compact-class section usable for nonlinear parametrized model classes, not only linear compact images.

CURRENT paper12 summable Minkowski-series entropy addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-summable-minkowski extends the finite Minkowski closure rule to weighted infinite series sum_j a_j K_j in a Banach space whenever sum_j a_j R_j<infty. It proves total boundedness and the truncation entropy bound e_{1+sum_{j<=r}(k_j-1)}(sum_j a_j K_j)<=sum_{j<=r} a_j e_{k_j}(K_j)+sum_{j>r}a_j R_j. Under exponentially decaying weights and uniformly bounded radii, uniform component entropy e_k(K_j)<=B k^{-s}(log(e+k))^beta yields e_m(K)<=C m^{-s}(log(e+m))^{s+beta_+} and Delta_n^E(K)<=C n(log n)^{-s}(log log n)^{s+beta_+}; uniform exponential component entropy yields e_m(K)<=C exp(-gamma sqrt m) and Delta_n^E(K)<=C n exp(-gamma' sqrt(log n)).

CURRENT paper12 weighted-series profile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-weighted-profile packages the summable Minkowski-series theorem in reusable profile form. If e_k(K_j)<=varphi(k), A_r=sum_{j<=r}a_j, and T_r=sum_{j>r}a_jR_j, then e_m(K)<=inf_{1<=r<=m}{A_r varphi(1+floor((m-1)/r))+T_r}; moreover Delta_n^E(K)<=R_K2^{q-1}+2n inf_{1<=r<=q}{A_r varphi(1+floor((q-1)/r))+T_r}, hence choosing q~log n gives the direct discrepancy profile bound. This makes all weighted-tail optimizations explicit instead of baking them into the exponential/polynomial special cases.

CURRENT paper12 weighted-profile amplitude upgrade (local, not yet pushed): cor:banach-weighted-profile now allows nonuniform component amplitudes e_k(K_j)<=b_j varphi(k). The profile bound uses S_r=sum_{j<=r}a_jb_j in place of the uniform accumulated weight, giving e_m(K)<=inf_{1<=r<=m}{S_r varphi(1+floor((m-1)/r))+T_r} and the same dyadic Delta_n profile. The previous A_r formula is the special case b_j=1.

CURRENT paper12 weighted polynomial-profile optimization addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-weighted-polynomial-profile optimizes the nonuniform weighted-series profile directly from accumulated amplitude S_r and radius tail T_r. If S_r<=A r^delta(log r)^eta, T_r<=A r^{-gamma}(log r)^{-tau}, and varphi(k)<=B k^{-s}(log k)^beta, then e_m(K)<=C m^{-s gamma/(s+delta+gamma)}(log m)^{(gamma(beta+eta)-tau(s+delta))/(s+delta+gamma)}, with the matching dyadic Delta_n rate. If varphi(k)<=B exp(-bk), the rate is tail-limited: e_m(K)<=C m^{-gamma}(log m)^{gamma-tau}, again with the corresponding discrepancy bound.

CURRENT paper12 polynomial-amplitude weighted-series addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-polynomial-amplitude-minkowski gives a concrete nonuniform application of the weighted profile optimizer. For weights a_j<=A_0 j^{-u}(log(e+j))^{-v}, u>1, and component entropy amplitudes j^rho(log(e+j))^eta with rho>u-1, polynomial/log component entropy gives e_m(K)<=C m^{-s(u-1)/(s+rho)}(log m)^{((u-1)(beta+eta)-v(s+rho))/(s+rho)} and the matching dyadic Delta_n rate. Exponential component entropy is tail-limited at e_m(K)<=C m^{-(u-1)}(log m)^{u-1-v}.

CURRENT paper12 log-balanced polynomial-weight addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-log-polynomial-minkowski covers weights a_j<=A_0 j^{-u}(log(e+j))^{-v}, u>1, v real. With gamma=u-1 and component entropy e_k(K_j)<=B k^{-s}(log(e+k))^beta, it gives e_m(K)<=C m^{-sigma}(log(e+m))^lambda where sigma=s gamma/(s+gamma) and lambda=(gamma beta-sv)/(s+gamma), hence Delta_n^E(K)<=C n(log n)^(-sigma)(log log n)^lambda. For exponentially entropic components it gives the tail-limited e_m(K)<=C m^{-gamma}(log m)^{gamma-v} and the corresponding dyadic discrepancy rate.

CURRENT paper12 stretched-exponential weighted Minkowski-series addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-stretched-minkowski extends the weighted-series closure to a_j<=A_0 exp(-c j^theta), theta>0. Uniform component entropy e_k(K_j)<=B k^{-s}(log(e+k))^beta gives e_m(K)<=C m^{-s}(log(e+m))^{s/theta+beta_+} and Delta_n^E(K)<=C n(log n)^{-s}(log log n)^{s/theta+beta_+}. Uniform exponential component entropy gives e_m(K)<=C exp(-gamma m^{theta/(1+theta)}) and Delta_n^E(K)<=C n exp(-gamma' (log n)^{theta/(1+theta)}), recovering the previous exponential-weight sqrt-log case at theta=1.

CURRENT paper12 polynomially weighted Minkowski-series addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-polynomial-minkowski optimizes the summable-series truncation bound for weights a_j<=A_0 j^{-u}, u>1, and uniformly bounded component radii. Uniform component entropy e_k(K_j)<=B k^{-s}(log(e+k))^beta gives e_m(K)<=C m^{-sigma}(log(e+m))^{beta_+}, sigma=s(u-1)/(s+u-1), hence Delta_n^E(K)<=C n(log n)^{-sigma}(log log n)^{beta_+}. Uniform exponential component entropy is tail-limited: e_m(K)<=C m^{-(u-1)}(log(e+m))^{u-1} and Delta_n^E(K)<=C n(log n)^{-(u-1)}(log log n)^{u-1}.

CURRENT paper12 finite-rank Banach envelope addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:banach-finite-rank-envelope quantifies the compact-operator-image theorem by widths. For a totally bounded K in the unit ball of a real normed space, with a_m(K)=inf_{dim F<=m} sup_{x in K} dist(x,F), Delta_n^E(K)<=3(1+6/eta)^m+n(eta+a_m(K)); for compact T:X->E the same holds with approximation numbers a_m(T)=inf_{rank S<=m}||T-S||. New cor:compact-operator-approx-rates gives concrete consequences: a_m(T)<=A m^{-s} implies Delta_n(T)<=C n (log log n/log n)^s, log-refined decay a_m(T)<=A m^{-s}(log(e+m))^beta adds the factor (log log n)^beta, while a_m(T)<=A exp(-b m^theta) implies Delta_n(T)<=C n exp(-c (log n)^{theta/(1+theta)}), including the exp(-c sqrt(log n)) rate when theta=1.
CURRENT paper12 Hilbert singular-value addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:hilbert-singular-rates specializes the compact-operator approximation-number theorem to compact operators between real Hilbert spaces. Since a_m(T)=s_{m+1}(T) by Schmidt approximation, singular values s_j(T)<=A j^{-s} imply Delta_n(T)<=C_{A,s} n (log log n/log n)^s, the log-refined case s_j(T)<=A j^{-s}(log(e+j))^beta adds the factor (log log n)^beta, while s_j(T)<=A exp(-b j^theta) imply Delta_n(T)<=C_{A,b,theta} n exp(-c_{b,theta} (log n)^{theta/(1+theta)}). New cor:hilbert-schatten-rates packages the pointwise polynomial case for Schatten p-class and weak Schatten p-class operators, giving exponent 1/p. This makes the Banach finite-rank envelope directly usable from spectral decay data, with the previous exponential singular-value rate recovered at theta=1.

CURRENT paper12 stretched-entropy addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-stretched-entropy records the sublinear regime beyond polynomial compact entropy. If log N_Y(rho)<=A rho^{-q}(log(e/rho))^gamma with q>0 and the tuple is alpha-Holder, then disc <= C n^{(alpha+q)/(2alpha+q)}(log n)^{alpha gamma/(2alpha+q)}; in particular every finite-q stretched-exponential compact target has o(n) discrepancy for every alpha>0.
CURRENT paper12 Holder-quotient addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-holder-quotient transfers the compact-metric theorem through a beta-Holder quotient pi:X->Y. If the model X has ordinary covering exponent s and the tuple on Y is alpha-Holder, then the pulled-back tuple has exponent alpha beta on X, so the governing entropy exponent is s/beta and the Spencer threshold is s<=2alpha beta. Finite upper metric 2alpha beta-content on X gives the critical endpoint, and upper Assouad/doubling models transfer too: a beta-Holder quotient of a compact D-doubling model is Spencer-scale whenever log_2 D<=2alpha beta.
CURRENT paper12 Holder-quotient log-window addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-holder-quotient-log-window transfers signed logarithmic covering profiles through the same beta-Holder quotient. If N_X(rho)<=A rho^{-s}(log(e/rho))^gamma, then the bound has n^{s/(2alpha beta)-1}(log n)^gamma inside the logarithm; at s=2alpha beta, gamma<=0 remains Spencer-scale and gamma>0 gives only sqrt(n log log n).
CURRENT paper12 snowflake addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-snowflake makes the compact-metric theorem stable under snowflaking. If (Y,d) has ordinary covering exponent s and the discrepancy tuple is alpha-Holder for d^theta, then the governing exponent is s/theta, so the Spencer threshold is s<=2alpha theta; the endpoint is also covered under finite original s-content. New cor:comm-af-snowflake-log-window keeps signed logarithmic covering profiles under the same snowflake scaling: if N_Y(rho)<=A rho^{-s}(log(e/rho))^gamma in the original metric, then the bound has n^{s/(2alpha theta)-1}(log n)^gamma inside the logarithm; at s=2alpha theta, gamma<=0 remains Spencer-scale and gamma>0 gives only sqrt(n log log n).
CURRENT paper12 compact-metric union addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-compact-union records that finite unions of compact metric targets keep the largest ordinary covering exponent. If Y=union_l Y_l and N_{Y_l}(rho)<=A_l rho^{-d_l}, then Holder tuples on Y obey the same bound with d_*=max_l d_l, hence are Spencer-scale when d_*<=2alpha. New cor:comm-af-compact-union-log-window adds signed log-polynomial profiles: for finite unions, only pieces with the maximal polynomial exponent contribute to the top logarithmic power; at d_*=2alpha a nonpositive top log power remains sqrt n, while a positive one costs only sqrt(n log log n). Finite upper metric dimensions likewise take the maximum under finite unions, finite critical 2alpha-content on each piece gives the critical endpoint, and the same largest-exponent rule holds for upper Assouad growth and finite unions of doubling pieces.
CURRENT paper12 compact product-strata addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-compact-product-strata combines the compact union and product rules. If a compact target is a finite union of strata Z_l, each a Lipschitz image of a finite max product prod_j Y_{l,j}, then the Holder discrepancy threshold is governed by s_*=max_l sum_j d_{l,j}. New cor:comm-af-compact-product-log-window adds signed log-polynomial finite products: polynomial exponents and log powers both add over factors, with the same critical sqrt n / sqrt(n log log n) split according to the sign of the total log power. New cor:comm-af-compact-holder-product-strata extends this to Holder stratum parametrizations: if pi_l has Holder exponent beta_l, the governing stratum exponent is (sum_j d_{l,j})/beta_l. New cor:comm-af-compact-holder-product-log-window adds the signed logarithmic critical window: factor log powers gamma_{l,j} add across each product stratum, and at divided product exponent 2alpha only top-exponent strata control the log power; nonpositive top log power remains sqrt n, while positive top log power costs only sqrt(n log log n). Thus product-parametrized stratified targets are Spencer-scale whenever max_l sum_j d_{l,j} <= 2alpha in the Lipschitz case, and whenever max_l beta_l^{-1} sum_j d_{l,j} <= 2alpha in the Holder-parametrized case, with the same max-over-strata/sum-over-factors rule for ordinary upper metric dimensions, critical contents, Assouad growth, and doubling exponents.
CURRENT paper12 finite-Lipschitz-atlas addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-lipschitz-atlas turns the compact-metric machinery into a concrete geometric target theorem. If a compact metric target Y is covered by finitely many Lipschitz images phi_l(K_l), with K_l compact in [0,1]^{D_l}, then Holder tuples on Y satisfy the compact-metric estimate with exponent D_*=max_l D_l. Hence every compact target with a finite D-dimensional Lipschitz atlas, including compact Lipschitz manifolds, finite polyhedra, and finite unions of Lipschitz images of compact subsets of R^D, is Spencer-scale whenever D<=2alpha.
CURRENT paper12 finite-Holder-atlas addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-holder-atlas generalizes the Lipschitz atlas package. If Y is covered by finitely many beta_l-Holder images of compact subsets of [0,1]^{D_l}, then the governing covering exponent is s_*=max_l D_l/beta_l. Thus Holder tuples on finite beta-Holder D-atlas targets are Spencer-scale whenever D<=2alpha beta; the finite Lipschitz-atlas theorem is the beta=1 case. New cor:comm-af-holder-atlas-log-window allows arbitrary compact chart domains with N_{K_l}(rho)<=A_l rho^{-d_l}(log(e/rho))^{gamma_l}; the governing exponent is t_*=max_l d_l/beta_l, and among charts with t_l=t_* the top gamma_l controls the signed critical window. At t_*=2alpha, nonpositive top log power remains Spencer-scale and positive top log power costs only sqrt(n log log n).
CURRENT paper12 exhaustive finite-scale atlas addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-exhaustive-finite-scale-atlas extends the finite Holder-atlas package to compact targets approximated by compact stages Y_r with one-sided Hausdorff error delta_r->0. If N_{Y_r}(u)<=A_r u^{-s}(log(e/u))^gamma and r_n is any stage with delta_{r_n} below the signing scale rho_n~n^{-1/(2alpha)}, then Holder tuples obey disc <= C sqrt{n log(e + A_{r_n} n^{s/(2alpha)-1}(log n)^gamma)}. Thus stage complexity may grow up to the unused entropy slack; at the critical exponent s=2alpha, polylogarithmic stage growth gives the same sqrt(n) / sqrt(n log log n) split according to the sign of kappa+gamma.

CURRENT paper12 polynomial exhaustive-atlas schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-exhaustive-polynomial-atlas specializes the finite-scale atlas theorem to delta_r<=H r^{-eta} and A_r<=A r^lambda(log(e+r))^kappa. Choosing the first stage visible at the signing scale gives the effective exponent s_eff=s+lambda/eta and log power Gamma_eff=gamma+kappa: disc <= C sqrt{n log(e+n^{s_eff/(2alpha)-1}(log n)^{Gamma_eff})}. Hence s_eff<2alpha is Spencer-scale, and at equality the sign of Gamma_eff gives the same sqrt(n) versus sqrt(n log log n) critical-window split.

CURRENT paper12 stretched-exponential exhaustive-atlas schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:comm-af-exhaustive-stretched-atlas covers delta_r<=H exp(-c r^theta) with polynomial/log stage entropy A_r<=A r^lambda(log(e+r))^kappa. The first visible stage is only polylogarithmic in n, so the polynomial exponent remains s, the log power becomes Gamma_fast=gamma+lambda/theta, and the secondary stage-log contributes (log log n)^kappa. Thus s<2alpha remains Spencer-scale; at s=2alpha, Gamma_fast<0 or Gamma_fast=0 with kappa<=0 remains sqrt(n), Gamma_fast>0 costs sqrt(n log log n), and the exact Gamma_fast=0<kappa border costs only sqrt(n log log log n).

CURRENT paper12 random-matrix Matrix Spencer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-algebraic-lowfrobenius packages the Akbas--Sra low-Frobenius perturbative theorem as a random-matrix ensemble result. With no independence, identical distribution, or fixed deterministic algebra required, any random self-adjoint tuple that, outside an event E, decomposes as X_i=B_i+L_i with B_i in a random finite-dimensional C*-algebra of intrinsic dimension O(n), ||B_i||op<=1, ||L_i||op<=1, and ||L_i||F^2<=n/log^3(en), has Matrix Spencer discrepancy O(sqrt n) with failure probability at most P(E). This covers arbitrary random full D x D self-adjoint contractions when D^2=O(n), random common-block algebraic cores, and arbitrary dependent low-Frobenius, low-rank, spiked, compressed, or coordinate-supported self-adjoint tails of rank/support at most n/log^3(en). New cor:random-gaussian-tail-full adds a full-matrix average-case route in every ambient dimension: for centered, possibly dependent, self-adjoint ensembles whose scalar l2 sums satisfy the Gaussian operator-tail bound, deterministic scaling by K(sqrt D+sqrt(log(en))) makes the matrices contractions and the all-plus signing has norm at most sqrt n with probability at least 1-2(n+1)(en)^-3; this includes GOE/GUE, centered sub-Gaussian Wigner-type ensembles, and correlated centered ensembles whenever the standard operator-tail input is available. New cor:random-algebraic-plus-gaussian-noise combines the two routes: random algebraic cores of intrinsic dimension O(n) remain Spencer-scale after adding full random Gaussian-operator-tail noise in arbitrary ambient dimension at its natural spectral scale; after dividing by 2 the resulting matrices are contractions, and the noise coordinates may be dependent if their scalar linear combinations obey the tail bound.
VERIFY addendum: pdflatex passed after the paper12 random-matrix corollaries cor:random-algebraic-lowfrobenius, cor:random-gaussian-tail-full, and cor:random-algebraic-plus-gaussian-noise plus summary/page-count updates (97pp), plus the master-index rebuild (14pp). Logs have no undefined references/citations, fatal errors, or rerun requests, only the known hyperref footnote warning, rerunfilecheck banner, font-expansion warning, and overfull/underfull boxes.
CURRENT paper12 variance-profile Matrix Spencer addendum (local, not yet pushed): prop:square-function-khintchine records the noncommutative-Khintchine signing certificate disc(A_1,...,A_n) <= C sqrt(log(eD)) ||sum_i A_i^2||op^{1/2}. Consequently any random self-adjoint contraction tuple whose realized square-function profile satisfies ||sum_i A_i^2||op <= C_0 n/log(eD) has Matrix Spencer discrepancy O_{C_0}(sqrt n) with the same probability as that event. cor:random-low-overlap-support is now weighted: if A_i=P_i A_i P_i, ||A_i||op<=rho_i<=1, and the weighted empirical overlap/frame operator ||sum_i rho_i^2 P_i||op is O(n/log(eD)), then the tuple is Spencer-scale. This covers coordinate-supported random matrices with maximum weighted occupancy O(n/log(eD)), random localized Wigner/GOE/GUE blocks, and random projection/frame ensembles once their weighted empirical frame bound is verified.
CURRENT paper12 dependent self-adjoint overlap-budget addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-dependent-selfadjoint-overlap-budget upgrades the weighted low-overlap support theorem from one row to active menus/countable libraries with arbitrary dependent self-adjoint support laws. For random localized streams A_{m,h,i}=P_{m,h,i}A_{m,h,i}P_{m,h,i}, it is enough that the realized weighted overlap operator sum_i rho_{m,h,i}^2 P_{m,h,i} is at most C0 n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable overlap-budget events; no independence, negative association, common filtration, or deterministic support law is required. New cor:active-menu-tail-capped-dependent-selfadjoint-overlap-budget adds unbounded/heavy-tailed localized blocks by deterministic caps rho<=1 and cap-tail budgets, with explicit sub-Weibull caps rho=s ell^{1/alpha} and finite-moment caps rho=s exp(ell/p). This covers dependent sparse self-adjoint matrices, random regular graph supports, matchings, without-replacement coordinate choices, determinantal/negatively dependent supports, adaptive projection frontiers, and heavy-tailed localized Wigner/GOE/GUE blocks whenever the capped realized overlap budget is summably certified.
