CURRENT paper12 coordinate-support Bernstein addendum (local, not yet pushed): cor:random-coordinate-support-bernstein is now weighted and inhomogeneous. With deterministic amplitudes rho_i, coordinate inclusion bounds p_ij, mu=max_j sum_i rho_i^2 p_ij, sigma^2=max_j sum_i rho_i^4 p_ij, and b=max_i rho_i^2, arbitrary self-adjoint matrices supported on S_i with ||A_i||op<=rho_i satisfy Matrix Spencer with failure at most D exp{-t^2/(2(sigma^2+b t/3))} whenever mu+t <= C_0 n/log(eD). The homogeneous consequence is recovered from p sum_i rho_i^2 <= C_0 n/(2log(eD)), giving the old D exp{-3 C_0 n/(16log(eD))} failure; uniformly chosen fixed-size supports now allow nonuniform sizes via D^{-1} sum_i rho_i^2 r_i.
CURRENT paper12 random-subspace Matrix Spencer addendum (local, not yet pushed): cor:random-subspace-matrix-bernstein is now weighted. For independent random orthogonal projections P_i, deterministic amplitudes rho_i, and mu=||sum_i rho_i^2 E P_i||op, arbitrary self-adjoint matrices A_i=P_i A_i P_i with ||A_i||op<=rho_i satisfy Matrix Spencer with failure at most D exp{-t^2/(2(mu+b t/3))}, b=max_i rho_i^2, whenever mu+t <= C_0 n/log(eD). For independent Haar rank-r_i projections, mu=D^{-1}sum_i rho_i^2 r_i, so weighted average rank <= C_0 D/(2log(eD)) gives failure at most D exp{-3 C_0 n/(16log(eD))}. This covers weighted random subspace-localized Wigner/GOE/GUE blocks and arbitrary self-adjoint blocks on those random subspaces.
CURRENT paper12 weighted support-array addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-weighted-support-borel promotes the weighted inhomogeneous coordinate and weighted random-subspace Bernstein theorems to triangular arrays. Along any schedule (D_m,n_m), if the row thresholds satisfy mu_m+t_m <= C_0 n_m/log(eD_m) and the coordinate or matrix-Bernstein failure series sum_m D_m exp{-t_m^2/(2(sigma_m^2+b_m t_m/3))} or sum_m D_m exp{-t_m^2/(2(mu_m+b_m t_m/3))} is finite, then disc(A_{m,1},...,A_{m,n_m}) <= C(C_0)sqrt(n_m) eventually almost surely. Homogeneous coordinate supports and Haar weighted-rank subspaces get the concrete summability condition sum_m D_m exp{-3C_0 n_m/(16log(eD_m))}<infty.
CURRENT paper12 weighted support sampled-certificate addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-weighted-support-search makes the weighted low-overlap support route algorithmic. Under the pathwise weighted-overlap event, M independent random sign trials fail to find an O(sqrt n) signing with only an added 2^{-M}; under the coordinate and subspace Bernstein hypotheses, the finite failure bounds simply gain +2^{-M}. In triangular arrays satisfying cor:random-weighted-support-borel, if sum_m 2^{-M_m}<infty then the sampled signs themselves contain an O(sqrt n_m) certificate eventually almost surely. This packages coordinate, projection, frame, sparse-support, and Haar/random-subspace ensembles as actual randomized certificate models, not only existential discrepancy theorems.

CURRENT paper12 square-profile Matrix Spencer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-square-profile-bernstein makes the noncommutative-Khintchine variance-profile route probabilistic. Independent random self-adjoint contractions with mu=||sum_i E A_i^2||op satisfy Matrix Spencer with failure at most D exp{-t^2/(2(mu+t/3))} whenever mu+t <= C_0 n/log(eD); in particular mu <= C_0 n/(2 log(eD)) gives failure at most D exp{-3 C_0 n/(16 log(eD))}. The corollary also covers independent Haar-random rotations A_i=U_i T_i U_i^* of fixed or conditionally random spectra whenever D^{-1} sum_i tr(T_i^2) <= C_0 n/(2 log(eD)), adding only the probability that this Frobenius-energy event fails. This reaches low-stable-rank and soft-spectrum full-rank random contractions beyond hard subspace support.
CURRENT paper12 sign-symmetry/twirl random-matrix addendum (local, not yet pushed): new prop:sign-symmetric-transfer proves a Fubini transfer for coordinatewise sign-invariant random self-adjoint tuples: on any sign-invariant normalization event H, the discrepancy failure probability is bounded by the all-plus operator-norm tail for the same realization-normalized ensemble. New cor:conditional-square-twirl packages the square-profile theorem conditionally: arbitrary dependent random spectra T_i conjugated by conditionally independent random unitaries are Spencer-scale whenever the conditional square twirl ||sum_i E(U_i T_i^2 U_i^* | T)||op is O(n/log(eD)). New cor:finite-design-rotations specializes this to exact scalar twirls, including Haar rotations, finite irreducible unitary-group rotations, exact conjugation unitary 1-designs, and signed-permutation/monomial twirls, with the same average Frobenius-square profile D^{-1}sum_i tr(T_i^2)=O(n/log(eD)) and the same Matrix-Bernstein failure D exp{-3C_0 n/(16log(eD))}.
CURRENT paper12 sign-symmetric all-plus menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:sign-symmetric-all-plus-menu-borel upgrades the sign-symmetric transfer to Borel--Cantelli active menus. For coordinatewise sign-invariant random self-adjoint tuples A_{m,h,i} and sign-invariant normalization events H_{m,h} forcing max_i||A_{m,h,i}||op<=1, if the active-menu all-plus failure series sum_m sum_{h<=J_m} P(H_{m,h}^c or ||sum_i A_{m,h,i}||op>K_m sqrt(n_m)) is finite, then almost surely all active h<=J_m have discrepancy <=K_m sqrt(n_m) for all large m. The scaled form applies to raw tuples X_{m,h,i} with deterministic scales s_{m,h}, giving discrepancy <=K_m s_{m,h} sqrt(n_m). If J_m exhausts a countable library, every fixed sign-symmetric model is eventually covered.

CURRENT paper12 sign-symmetric Bernstein all-plus menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:sign-symmetric-bernstein-menu-borel makes the all-plus menu transfer checkable by matrix Bernstein. For active triangular arrays of independent bounded self-adjoint sign-symmetric matrices X_{m,h,i} with deterministic scale caps s_{m,h} and variance proxy sigma_{m,h}^2=||sum_i E X_{m,h,i}^2||op, if sum_m sum_{h<=J_m} 2D_m exp{-u_{m,h}^2/(2(sigma_{m,h}^2+s_{m,h}u_{m,h}/3))}<infty, then almost surely every active h<=J_m has disc(X_{m,h,1},...,X_{m,h,n_m})<=u_{m,h} for all large m. Taking u_{m,h}=K_m s_{m,h} sqrt(n_m) gives simultaneous normalized Matrix-Spencer signings, and J_m->infinity exhausts countable independent bounded sign-symmetric libraries.

CURRENT paper12 sign-symmetric Bernstein schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:sign-symmetric-bernstein-menu-schedules turns cor:sign-symmetric-bernstein-menu-borel into explicit active-prefix growth rules. If sigma_{m,h}^2<=beta s_{m,h}^2 n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable exceptions, then u=K s_{m,h} sqrt(n_m) succeeds whenever the active cost J_mD_m times the Bernstein decay is summable. In particular, if J_DD<=(eD)^Gamma and n_D>=a log^2(eD), all integer dimensions are covered when K^2/(2(beta+K/(3sqrt a)))>Gamma+1; along sparse stretched-exponential dimensions the threshold drops to >Gamma. If J_m->infinity, the same schedules exhaust countable independent bounded sign-symmetric libraries.

CURRENT paper12 sign-symmetric Bernstein closed-margin addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:sign-symmetric-bernstein-menu-closed-margins solves the schedule inequality from cor:sign-symmetric-bernstein-menu-schedules. With variance profile sigma_{m,h}^2<=beta s_{m,h}^2 n_m/log(eD_m), active cost J_DD<=(eD)^Gamma, and n_D>=a log^2(eD), all integer dimensions are covered for any K>Theta(Gamma+1,a,beta), where Theta(A,a,beta)=A/(3sqrt a)+sqrt(A^2/(9a)+2A beta). Sparse stretched-exponential schedules use K>Theta(Gamma,a,beta). Equivalently, for prescribed K and exponent A>0, it suffices that K^2>2A beta and a>(2AK/(3(K^2-2A beta)))^2.

CURRENT paper12 conditional sign-symmetric Bernstein addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:conditional-sign-symmetric-bernstein-menu lifts the sign-symmetric Bernstein menu theorem from deterministic independent tuples to conditionally independent tuples over random environments. Conditional symmetry and conditional independence are assumed given G_{m,h}; on a summable envelope-good event the conditional laws are supported in ||X_i||<=s_{m,h} and have conditional variance proxy ||sum_i E(X_i^2|G_{m,h})||<=v_{m,h}. If the bad-envelope probabilities plus 2D_m exp{-u_{m,h}^2/(2(v_{m,h}+s_{m,h}u_{m,h}/3))} are summable, then all active discrepancies are eventually <=u_{m,h}. With v_{m,h}<=beta s_{m,h}^2 n_m/log(eD_m), the same all-integer and sparse schedules and closed-form margins K>Theta(Gamma+1,a,beta) or K>Theta(Gamma,a,beta) apply. This covers random-direction scalar-multiplier models X_i=xi_i B_i with environment-measurable self-adjoint B_i and conditionally independent bounded real symmetric multipliers.

CURRENT paper12 symmetric scalar-multiplier Bernstein schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:symmetric-scalar-multiplier-bernstein-schedules packages the conditional sign-symmetric theorem for active menus of random scalar multiplier streams X_{m,h,i}=xi_{m,h,i}B_{m,h,i}. The directions B_{m,h,i} are environment-measurable self-adjoint contractions, the real multipliers are conditionally independent, conditionally symmetric, and bounded by 1, and the only matrix budget is nu_{m,h}=||sum_i E(xi_{m,h,i}^2|G_{m,h})B_{m,h,i}^2||op. If nu_{m,h}<=beta n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable active-menu exceptions, then all active discrepancies are eventually <=K sqrt(n_m) under the same general, all-integer, and sparse schedules as cor:sign-symmetric-bernstein-menu-schedules. In particular, all integer dimensions with J_DD<=(eD)^Gamma and n_D>=a log^2(eD) are covered for K>Theta(Gamma+1,a,beta), sparse stretched-exponential schedules for K>Theta(Gamma,a,beta), and pointwise q_i<=beta/log(eD_m) makes the variance exception empty.

CURRENT paper12 sub-Gaussian symmetric scalar-multiplier normalization addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:subgaussian-symmetric-scalar-multiplier-normalizations extends the sign-symmetric scalar-multiplier lane from bounded multipliers to cap-normalized unbounded symmetric sub-Gaussian multipliers. For A_{m,h,i}=L_{m,h}^{-1} xi_{m,h,i} B_{m,h,i}, with environment-measurable self-adjoint contractions B_{m,h,i}, conditionally independent real symmetric sub-Gaussian xi_{m,h,i} with deterministic proxies a_{m,h,i}, and normalized variance profile nu_{m,h}=L_{m,h}^{-2}||sum_i a_{m,h,i}^2 B_{m,h,i}^2||op, the active menus are eventually contraction-normalized and satisfy disc(A_{m,h,1},...,A_{m,h,n_m})<=K sqrt(n_m) whenever nu_{m,h}<=beta n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable exceptions, the cap-failure series sum exp{-L_{m,h}^2/(2a_{m,h,i}^2)} is summable, and J_mD_m(eD_m)^(-K^2/(2beta)) is summable. Thus all integer dimensions with J_DD<=(eD)^Gamma need K>sqrt(2beta(Gamma+1)), while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need K>sqrt(2beta Gamma). Uniform proxies have cap contribution 2n_m exp{-L^2/(2a^2)}.

CURRENT paper12 tail-capped symmetric scalar-multiplier schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:tail-capped-symmetric-scalar-multiplier-schedules extends the scalar-multiplier lane from bounded and sub-Gaussian multipliers to arbitrary conditionally independent symmetric real multipliers after deterministic cap normalization. For A_{m,h,i}=L_{m,h}^{-1} xi_{m,h,i} B_{m,h,i}, with environment-measurable self-adjoint contractions B_{m,h,i}, the relevant matrix budget is the truncated second-moment profile widehat{nu}_{m,h}=||sum_i L_{m,h}^{-2} E[xi_{m,h,i}^2 1_{|xi_{m,h,i}|<=L_{m,h}} | G_{m,h}] B_{m,h,i}^2||op. If widehat{nu}_{m,h}<=beta n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable exceptions and the cap failures sum_i P{|xi_{m,h,i}|>L_{m,h}} are summable over active menus, then the normalized matrices are eventually contractions and disc(A_{m,h,1},...,A_{m,h,n_m})<=K sqrt(n_m) under the same bounded-Bernstein schedules: general gamma(K,beta,B)=K^2/(2(beta+KB/3)), all integer dimensions for K>Theta(Gamma+1,a,beta), and sparse stretched-exponential schedules for K>Theta(Gamma,a,beta). For conditional sub-Weibull tails P{|xi|>t|G}<=2 exp{-(t/a_i)^alpha}, the cap contribution is 2sum_i exp{-(L/a_i)^alpha} and the variance budget follows from C_alpha L^{-2}||sum_i a_i^2 B_i^2||op<=beta n/log(eD), C_alpha=4 Gamma(2/alpha)/alpha.

CURRENT paper12 polynomial-moment scalar-multiplier cap addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:polynomial-moment-tail-capped-scalar-multiplier-schedules makes the tail-capped scalar-multiplier theorem checkable for polynomial moment tails of every order p>0. If E(|xi_{m,h,i}|^p | G_{m,h})<=a_{m,h,i}^p, r=min(p,2), L_{m,h}^{-r}||sum_i a_{m,h,i}^r B_{m,h,i}^2||op<=beta n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable exceptions, and sum_i(a_{m,h,i}/L_{m,h})^p is summable over active menus, then the normalized A_{m,h,i}=L_{m,h}^{-1}xi_{m,h,i}B_{m,h,i} are eventually contractions and satisfy the same Matrix Spencer discrepancy bound under the same general, all-integer, and sparse bounded-Bernstein schedules. For uniform proxies a_i<=a_h, beta>0, any delta>0, the explicit cap L_{m,h}>=a_h max{(log(eD_m)/beta)^{1/r}, (J_m n_m(e+m)^{1+delta})^{1/p}} makes both the variance and cap conditions automatic.

CURRENT paper12 environment-adaptive scalar-multiplier cap addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:environment-adaptive-tail-capped-scalar-multiplier-schedules removes the deterministic-cap restriction from the tail-capped scalar-multiplier lane. The cap L_{m,h}, and in the moment version the proxies a_{m,h,i}, may now be G_{m,h}-measurable, so they can be chosen after seeing the random directions/environment and before sampling the multipliers. If the adaptive truncated second-moment profile widehat{nu}_{m,h}=||sum_i L_{m,h}^{-2}E[xi_i^2 1_{|xi_i|<=L_{m,h}}|G_{m,h}]B_i^2||op is <= beta n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable exceptions and the conditional cap-failure series E sum_i P{|xi_i|>L_{m,h}|G_{m,h}} is summable over active menus, the same general, all-integer, and sparse bounded-Bernstein schedules give eventual contractions and disc<=K sqrt(n). For p-moment proxies, it suffices that L^{-r}||sum_i a_i^rB_i^2||op<=beta n/log(eD), r=min(p,2), and E sum_i(a_i/L)^p is summable. A uniform environment-measurable envelope a_h is handled by the adaptive cap L>=a_h max{(log(eD)/beta)^{1/r}, (J_m n_m(e+m)^{1+delta})^{1/p}}.

CURRENT paper12 rank-sensitive square-profile addendum (local, not yet pushed): prop:square-function-khintchine and cor:random-sign-search-square-profile now use the realized joint square-function rank rho=max(1,rank(sum_i A_i^2)) in place of the ambient D whenever possible: disc(A_1,...,A_n)<=C sqrt(log(e rho))||sum_i A_i^2||op^{1/2}, and m random sign trials fail at the corresponding rank-sensitive threshold with probability at most 2^-m. New cor:rank-sensitive-predictable-square-profile applies matrix Freedman on a common deterministic or random kappa-dimensional support projection P: adapted contractions A_i=PA_iP with predictable profile nu_kappa=||sum_i E(A_i^2|F_{i-1})||op satisfy Matrix Spencer with failure P{nu_kappa>v}+kappa exp{-t^2/(2(v+t/3))} whenever v+t<=C_0 n/log(e kappa), and sampled sign search adds only 2^-m. New cor:rank-sensitive-dominated-square-twirl specializes this to conditionally independent dominated twirl ensembles inside a common random kappa-dimensional corner: Haar, exact finite-design, signed-permutation/monomial, block-design, and approximate-design rotations pay the corner profile (1/r)sum_i tr(T_i^2), or the corresponding block/PSD-error dominated profile, with log(e kappa) and a kappa-dimensional tail instead of ambient D. New cor:finite-menu-rank-sensitive-dominated-square-twirl-schedules makes these dominated low-rank random-basis models simultaneous on active menus and countable exhaustions under the same summable rank tail, with common sampled batches paying J_m2^{-M_m}. New cor:rank-sensitive-predictable-dominated-twirl and cor:finite-menu-rank-sensitive-predictable-dominated-twirl-schedules extend the same kappa-dimensional profile and active-menu cost to online adaptive low-rank random-basis streams selected from the past, without conditional independence across times. New cor:finite-menu-rank-sensitive-square-profile-schedules gives active-menu almost-sure signings under the summability of sum_h kappa_{m,h} exp{-3C_0 n_m/(16 log(e kappa_{m,h}))}; the concrete schedule uses Lambda_m^kappa=sum_h kappa_{m,h} and ell_m^kappa=max_h log(e kappa_{m,h}). Thus the square-profile random-matrix route now covers common random low-rank corners with log(e kappa), a kappa-dimensional tail, dominated low-rank random-basis ensembles, online adaptive low-rank twirl streams, and active-menu cost sum_h kappa_{m,h} instead of log(eD), a D-dimensional tail, and J_mD_m.

CURRENT paper12 two-scale square-profile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new prop:two-scale-square-function-certificate proves a single-signing certificate for decompositions A_i=B_i+R_i with B_i supported in a common kappa-dimensional corner and R_i arbitrary ambient self-adjoint residuals: disc(A_1,...,A_n) <= C[sqrt(log(e kappa))||sum_i B_i^2||op^{1/2}+sqrt(log(eD))||sum_i R_i^2||op^{1/2}], and m random sign trials fail only with probability 2^-m at the corresponding two-scale threshold. New cor:two-scale-predictable-square-profile applies rank-sensitive Freedman to the core and ambient Freedman to the residual, giving Matrix Spencer whenever the predictable core profile is O(n/log(e kappa)) and the residual profile is O(n/log(eD)); the failure is the sum of the kappa-dimensional and D-dimensional Freedman tails plus profile exceptions and optional 2^-m search loss. New cor:finite-menu-two-scale-predictable-square-profile-schedules makes active menus and countable exhaustions simultaneous: the low-rank core pays sum_h kappa_{m,h} exp{-gamma_lo n_m/log(e kappa_{m,h})}, the residual pays J_m D_m exp{-gamma_hi n_m/log(eD_m)}, and a common sampled batch costs J_m2^{-M_m}. This extends the rank-sensitive random-matrix lane from exact low-rank streams to low-rank cores with full-dimensional diffuse martingale/twirl/scalar-multiplier/localized residual noise.

CURRENT paper12 finite-multiscale square-profile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new prop:finite-multiscale-square-function-certificate extends the two-scale certificate from one low-rank core to finitely many low-rank layers plus one full ambient residual under the same signs: disc(A_1,...,A_n) <= C[sum_q sqrt(log(e kappa_q))||sum_i (B_i^{(q)})^2||op^{1/2}+sqrt(log(eD))||sum_i R_i^2||op^{1/2}], and m sampled sign vectors fail only with probability 2^-m at the corresponding multiscale threshold. New cor:finite-multiscale-predictable-square-profile applies rank-sensitive Freedman independently to each low-rank layer and ambient Freedman to the residual; finite-depth random mixtures are Matrix-Spencer scale whenever sum_q sqrt(a_q)+sqrt(a_*) is bounded and the q-th layer and residual predictable profiles are O(a_q n/log(e kappa_q)) and O(a_* n/log(eD)). New cor:finite-menu-multiscale-predictable-square-profile-schedules makes active finite-depth menus and countable exhaustions simultaneous, with low-rank structural cost sum_{h,q} kappa_{m,h,q} exp{-3 a_{m,h,q} n_m/(16 log(e kappa_{m,h,q}))}, residual cost J_m D_m exp{-3 a_{m,h,*} n_m/(16 log(eD_m))}, and common sampled signs costing J_m2^{-M_m}. This covers hierarchical spiked, layered block, multi-resolution random-basis, and mixed localized/full residual random-matrix models without forcing all structured pieces into one common corner.

CURRENT paper12 summable-depth multiscale square-profile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new prop:summable-multiscale-square-function-certificate removes the finite-depth restriction from the multiscale certificate. If A_i=R_i+sum_{q>=1}B_i^{(q)} converges in operator norm, B_i^{(q)} is supported in a kappa_q-dimensional corner, and sum_q sqrt(log(e kappa_q))||sum_i(B_i^{(q)})^2||op^{1/2}+sqrt(log(eD))||sum_iR_i^2||op^{1/2}<infty, then one signing gives that bound and m random sign trials fail only with probability 2^-m. New cor:active-menu-summable-multiscale-square-profile-schedules gives active-menu and countable-library Borel-Cantelli control under summability of the profile exceptions, the triple structural series sum_{m,h,q} kappa_{m,h,q} exp{-3a_{m,h,q}n_m/(16log(e kappa_{m,h,q}))}, the residual series sum_{m,h}D_m exp{-3a_{m,h,*}n_m/(16log(eD_m))}, and the sampled-search series. This covers infinite-depth hierarchical, wavelet-like, multiresolution, and countably layered random-basis matrix models with summable energy across layers.

CURRENT paper12 flag-compressible multiscale addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:flag-summable-multiscale-square-function-certificate turns any nested initial projection flag P^(0)<=P^(1)<=... into a summable multiscale decomposition by using compression shells Delta_i^(q)=P^(q)A_iP^(q)-P^(q-1)A_iP^(q-1) and residual R_i=A_i-P^(infty)A_iP^(infty). The certificate pays sqrt(log(e kappa_q))||sum_i(Delta_i^(q))^2||op^{1/2} for each shell and sqrt(log(eD))||sum_iR_i^2||op^{1/2} for the terminal residual, with m sampled signs failing only by 2^-m. New cor:predictable-flag-summable-square-profile proves the predictable random-stream version for adapted contractions; shell and residual increments have norm at most 2, so the Freedman structural tails become kappa_q exp{-3a_q n/(64log(e kappa_q))} and D exp{-3a_* n/(64log(eD))} under half-budget profiles. New cor:active-menu-predictable-flag-summable-square-profile-schedules gives active-menu/countable-library Borel-Cantelli control under the corresponding summable profile exceptions, shell structural triple series, residual series, and sampled-search series. This makes the summable-depth theorem directly applicable to multiresolution, wavelet, spectral-window, hierarchical-basis, and flag-compressible random matrix streams without hand-building supported layer contractions.

CURRENT paper12 compression-error flag addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:compression-error-flag-square-profile converts common-flag approximation estimates into summable multiscale square profiles. If tau_{i,q} bounds ||A_i-P^(q)A_iP^(q)||op and tau_{i,*} bounds the terminal residual, then the q-th shell contributes at most (sum_i(tau_{i,q-1}+tau_{i,q})^2)^{1/2} with the log(e kappa_q) weight, and sampled signs still fail only by 2^-m. New cor:predictable-compression-error-flag-square-profile gives the adapted version using predictable budgets U_q=sum_i(tau_{i,q-1}+tau_{i,q})^2 and U_*=sum_i tau_{i,*}^2; under half-budget bounds it has the same flag structural tails kappa_q exp{-3a_q n/(64log(e kappa_q))} and D exp{-3a_* n/(64log(eD))}. New cor:active-menu-predictable-compression-error-flag-schedules gives the active-menu/countable-library Borel-Cantelli version. This makes common-flag spectral truncation, wavelet tail, hierarchical approximation, and low-rank approximation error estimates directly usable for Matrix Spencer without explicitly computing shell square functions.

Logs have no undefined references/citations, fatal errors, LaTeX errors, or rerun requests; only the known hyperref footnote warning, font-expansion warning, rerunfilecheck package banner/info, and overfull/underfull boxes remain.
CURRENT paper12 common-flag tail-envelope addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:common-flag-tail-envelope-square-profile turns common-flag approximation rates into an explicit Matrix Spencer bound. If ||A_i-P^(q)A_iP^(q)||op <= L_i b_q and the terminal residual is <=r_i, then disc(A_1,...,A_n) is bounded by C[B_flag(b,kappa)(sum_i L_i^2)^{1/2}+sqrt(log(eD))(sum_i r_i^2)^{1/2}], where B_flag=sum_q sqrt(log(e kappa_q))(b_{q-1}+b_q), and sampled signs fail only by 2^-m. The corollary records checkable regimes: polynomial tails b_q=O((1+q)^-p) work against kappa_q<=exp(gamma q^s) when p>1+s/2, and any positive power tail works for geometrically growing rank windows. New cor:active-menu-common-flag-tail-envelope-schedules gives the active-menu/countable-library Borel-Cantelli version, paying only summable envelope-exception probabilities and sampled-search losses. This directly covers random or deterministic spectral truncation, wavelet-tail, and low-rank approximation models with common flags and explicit approximation rates.

CURRENT paper12 rectangular square-profile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:rectangular-square-profile-certificate uses Hermitian dilation to turn rectangular/non-self-adjoint tuples X_i into a Matrix Spencer certificate controlled by max{||sum_i X_iX_i^*||op, ||sum_i X_i^*X_i||op}, with the logarithm at d_1+d_2 or at the realized left-plus-right square-profile rank, and sampled sign search failing only by 2^-m. New cor:predictable-rectangular-square-profile gives the adapted random-stream version: if the predictable left and right profiles are both O(n/log(e(d_1+d_2))), then the rectangular tuple has an O(sqrt n) signing with the same Freedman structural tail as the self-adjoint square-profile theorem. New cor:active-menu-rectangular-square-profile-schedules gives the finite-menu/countable-library Borel-Cantelli schedule, paying J_m(d_{m,1}+d_{m,2}) in the structural series and only the usual sampled-search losses. This extends the random-matrix lane to rectangular random operators, bipartite support models, and non-self-adjoint matrix-series models under left/right variance-profile control.

CURRENT paper12 centered independent-entry rectangular addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:centered-independent-entry-rectangular specializes the predictable rectangular square-profile theorem to whole rectangular random matrices with one discrepancy sign per matrix. For adapted X_i=(x_{i,r,s}) with ||X_i||op<=K, conditionally independent centered entries, and deterministic variance envelopes E(|x_{i,r,s}|^2|F_{i-1})<=sigma_{i,r,s}^2, the relevant profile is V^ent=max{max_r sum_i sum_s sigma_{i,r,s}^2, max_s sum_i sum_r sigma_{i,r,s}^2}. If V^ent<=C0 K^2 n/(2log(e(d_1+d_2))) and the structural series J_m(d_{m,1}+d_{m,2})exp{-c0 n_m/log(eD_m^rec)} is summable, then every active stream has eventual signings at scale C(C0)K sqrt(n_m), with sampled-sign certificates under the same 2^{-M} summability. For J_m<=(eD_m^rec)^Gamma and n_m>=a log^2(eD_m^rec), all integer dimensions need c0 a>Gamma+2 and sparse stretched-exponential schedules need c0 a>Gamma+1. This covers dense or sparse centered independent-entry rectangular random matrices after deterministic operator-norm normalization, without decomposing entries into separately signed atoms.

CURRENT paper12 tail-capped independent-entry rectangular addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:tail-capped-independent-entry-rectangular removes the almost-sure operator-norm bound from cor:centered-independent-entry-rectangular at the cost of summable whole-matrix cap tails. For adapted rectangular matrices with conditionally independent centered entries, deterministic variance profile V^ent=max{max_r sum_i sum_s sigma_{i,r,s}^2, max_s sum_i sum_r sigma_{i,r,s}^2}, caps K_{m,h}, and tail budgets eta_{m,h,i}=P{||X_{m,h,i}||op>K_{m,h}}, the half-budget V^ent<=C0 K_{m,h}^2 n_m/(2log(eD_m^rec)) plus summability of sum_i eta and D_m^rec exp{-c0 n_m/log(eD_m^rec)} gives eventual O(K_{m,h}sqrt(n_m)) signings with one sign per whole matrix and sampled-sign certificates. Sub-Weibull whole-matrix operator tails use K=S L_m^{1/alpha}; p-moment tails use K=S exp(L_m/p). Under J_m n_m<=(eD_m^rec)^Gamma and n_m>=a log^2(eD_m^rec), all integer dimensions need R>Gamma+1 and c0a>Gamma+2, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need R>Gamma and c0a>Gamma+1. This covers dense or sparse unbounded centered independent-entry rectangular matrices after tail normalization, without splitting entries into separately signed atoms.

CURRENT paper12 tail-capped Hermitian entry-variance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:tail-capped-hermitian-entry-variance-profile adds the self-adjoint/Wigner analogue of the rectangular independent-entry route. For adapted Hermitian matrices with conditionally independent centered upper-triangular entries, deterministic variance envelopes alpha_{i,p}^2 and beta_{i,p,q}^2, operator caps K_{m,h}, and cap-tail budgets eta_{m,h,i}=P{||A_{m,h,i}||op>K_{m,h}}, the relevant profile is W^herm=max_p sum_i(alpha_{i,p}^2+sum_{q<p} beta_{i,q,p}^2+sum_{p<q} beta_{i,p,q}^2). If W^herm<=C0 K_{m,h}^2 n_m/(2log(eD_m)) and the cap tails plus D_m exp{-c0 n_m/log(eD_m)} are summable, then every active stream has eventual O(K_{m,h}sqrt(n_m)) signings with one sign per whole Hermitian matrix and sampled-sign certificates. Sub-Weibull whole-matrix tails use K=S L_m^{1/alpha}; p0-moment tails use K=S exp(L_m/p0). Under J_m n_m<=(eD_m)^Gamma and n_m>=a log^2(eD_m), all integer dimensions need R>Gamma+1 and c0a>Gamma+2, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need R>Gamma and c0a>Gamma+1. This covers Wigner, GOE/GUE, random band, sparse Hermitian, and variance-profile upper-triangular random matrix libraries that are not literally rectangular independent-entry arrays because of conjugate symmetry.

CURRENT paper12 full operator-tail active-menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-full-operator-tail-random-matrices packages the full random-matrix Gaussian-operator-tail route into finite active menus and countable-library exhaustions. For active self-adjoint streams Y_{m,h,i} with environment-measurable scale K_{m,h} satisfying the conditional scalar-combination tail P{||sum_i a_i Y_{m,h,i}||op>K_{m,h}||a||_2(sqrt(D_m)+t)|H_m}<=2e^{-t^2}, the normalized matrices A_{m,h,i}=Y_{m,h,i}/(K_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m)) are eventually contractions and the all-plus signing gives disc<=sqrt(n_m) for every active h, provided sum_m J_m(n_m+1)e^{-tau_m^2}<infty. No coordinatewise sign symmetry is required. If J_m(n_m+1)<=(eD_m)^Gamma, all integer dimensions are covered by tau_m^2=Rlog(eD_m) with R>Gamma+1, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need only R>Gamma. This gives simultaneous natural-spectral-scale certificates for Gaussian, Wigner/GOE/GUE, correlated Gaussian, and other full operator-tail random matrix libraries, including environment-adaptive tail normalizations.

CURRENT paper12 Hermitian quadratic-form coefficient-series addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-quadratic-form-full-random-matrices makes the standalone self-adjoint all-plus operator-tail theorem checkable from scalar quadratic-form tails <u,sum_i a_iY_i u>, with normalization 5q_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m) and the same active-menu arbitrary-growth, all-integer, and sparse schedules. New cor:active-menu-hermitian-coefficient-series-quadratic specializes this to Hermitian sub-Gaussian atom expansions Y_i=sum_alpha xi_{i,alpha}B_{i,alpha}: the proxy Q^h=max_i sup_{||u||=1}(sum_alpha sigma_{i,alpha}^2 |<u,B_{i,alpha}u>|^2)^{1/2} is bounded by the atom square function max_i ||sum_alpha sigma_{i,alpha}^2 B_{i,alpha}^2||op^{1/2}. Normalizing by 5C_hser Q^h(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m), all active matrices are eventually contractions and the all-plus signing gives discrepancy <=sqrt(n_m). This covers Hermitian sub-Gaussian coefficient expansions, correlated Wigner-type atom patterns, random Hermitian feature blocks, and structured self-adjoint measurement libraries without sign symmetry or entry independence beyond the scalar coefficient tail.

CURRENT paper12 standalone Hermitian independent-entry addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-hermitian-independent-entry-quadratic is the direct all-plus version of the upper-triangular entrywise sub-Gaussian route, no finite-lift core needed. For active Hermitian streams with independent centered upper-triangular entries and diagonal/off-diagonal proxies alpha_{i,p}, beta_{i,pq}, the quadratic-form proxy is Q^ent=max_i sup_{||u||=1}(sum_p alpha_{i,p}^2 |u_p|^4+4 sum_{p<q} beta_{i,pq}^2 |u_p|^2 |u_q|^2)^{1/2}. Normalizing by 5C_ent Q^ent(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m), all active matrices are eventually contractions and the all-plus signing gives discrepancy <=sqrt(n_m) whenever sum_m J_m(n_m+1)e^{-tau_m^2}<infty, with the same arbitrary-growth, all-integer, and sparse schedules. Homogeneous Wigner/GOE/GUE-type entries have Q^ent<=sqrt(2)sigma, while sparse or banded profiles are controlled by row variance budgets alpha_p^2+2 sum_{q ne p} beta_{pq}^2.

CURRENT paper12 covariance/Wishart Bernstein-form addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-bernstein-quadratic-form-random-matrices extends the direct all-plus active-menu route from sub-Gaussian quadratic-form tails to Bernstein quadratic-form tails of the form q||a||_2 s+b||a||_infty s^2. Normalizing by C_Bq(q(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m)+b(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m)^2), all active matrices are eventually contractions and the all-plus signing gives discrepancy <=sqrt(n_m) under the same summable J_m(n_m+1)e^{-tau_m^2}, all-integer, and sparse schedules. New cor:active-menu-centered-covariance-wishart specializes this to conditionally independent centered covariance/Wishart blocks Y_i=r_i^{-1}sum_l(x_{i,l}x_{i,l}^*-E x_{i,l}x_{i,l}^*), with q_cov=C max_i(r_i^{-2}sum_l kappa_{i,l}^4)^{1/2} and b_cov=C max_{i,l} kappa_{i,l}^2/r_i; homogeneous blocks have scale kappa^2((sqrt(D)+tau)/sqrt(r)+(sqrt(D)+tau)^2/r). This covers rank-one covariance, averaged real/complex Wishart increments, sample-covariance blocks, and random feature covariance streams without forcing squared variables into a sub-Gaussian tail hypothesis.

CURRENT paper12 Hermitian Bernstein-entry addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-hermitian-independent-entry-bernstein specializes the Bernstein quadratic-form active-menu criterion to independent upper-triangular Hermitian entries with scalar Bernstein variance proxies alpha,beta and jump proxies lambda,mu. The quadratic variance proxy is Q^eB=max_i sup_{||u||=1}(sum_p alpha_{i,p}^2|u_p|^4+4sum_{p<q}beta_{i,pq}^2|u_p|^2|u_q|^2)^{1/2}, the jump proxy is B^eB=max_i max{lambda_{i,p},mu_{i,pq}}, and normalization by C_Bq C_eB(Q^eB(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m)+B^eB(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m)^2) makes all active matrices eventual contractions with all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n_m) under the same active-menu schedules. Homogeneous Bernstein entries have Q^eB<=sqrt(2)sigma and B^eB<=L, covering centered bounded, Poissonized, exponential-entry, gamma-entry, and other light-tailed non-sub-Gaussian Hermitian libraries.

CURRENT paper12 Haar rectangular active-menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-haar-unitary-orthogonal specializes the rectangular bilinear-form active-menu theorem to conditionally independent Haar unitary and Haar orthogonal matrices. For fixed bilinear forms, the spherical-coordinate tail gives q_Haar=C_Haar D^{-1/2}; after normalizing by 5q_Haar(sqrt(2D)+tau), all active matrices are eventually contractions and the all-plus signing gives discrepancy <=sqrt(n_m) whenever sum_m J_m(n_m+1)e^{-tau_m^2}<infty, with the same arbitrary-growth, all-integer, and sparse schedules. If tau=O(sqrt(D)), this is only a universal constant normalization, so independent Haar unitary, Haar orthogonal, and mixed Haar libraries are Matrix-Spencer scale without entry independence or sign symmetry.

CURRENT paper12 rectangular full operator-tail active-menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-rectangular-full-operator-tail-random-matrices extends the direct all-plus operator-tail route from self-adjoint full random matrices to rectangular/non-self-adjoint streams. For active rectangular matrices X_{m,h,i} with environment-measurable scale K_{m,h} satisfying P{||sum_i a_i X_{m,h,i}||op>K_{m,h}||a||_2(sqrt(D_m^rec)+t)|H_m}<=2e^{-t^2}, the normalized matrices X/(K_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m^rec)+tau_m)) are eventually contractions and the all-plus signing gives discrepancy <=sqrt(n_m) for every active h whenever sum_m J_m(n_m+1)e^{-tau_m^2}<infty. CURRENT paper12 independent-entry bilinear proxy addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-independent-entry-bilinear-rectangular computes the scalar bilinear proxy in the rectangular all-plus theorem from entrywise sub-Gaussian data. For active conditionally independent centered rectangular entries with proxies sigma_{m,h,i,r,s}, the relevant scale is Q_{m,h}=max_i sup_{||u||=||v||=1}(sum_{r,s}|u_r|^2|v_s|^2 sigma_{m,h,i,r,s}^2)^{1/2}, bounded by max_{i,r,s}sigma_{m,h,i,r,s}. Homogeneous independent-entry rectangular sub-Gaussian matrices therefore fall under the direct all-plus route at the natural sigma(sqrt(d_1+d_2)+tau) spectral scale, without variance-profile or sign-symmetry assumptions.

CURRENT paper12 explicit bounded Bernstein schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-bounded-bernstein-all-plus-schedules converts the nonsymmetric bounded self-adjoint and rectangular all-plus Bernstein criteria into concrete active-menu schedules. With Delta=D or D^rec, ell=log(e Delta), variance budget v<=beta s^2 n/ell outside summable exceptions, and ell<=B sqrt(n), normalization by K s gives contractions and all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n) whenever sum_m J_m Delta_m(e Delta_m)^(-gamma(K,beta,B))<infty, where gamma=K^2/(2(beta+KB/3)). If J_m Delta_m<=(e Delta_m)^Gamma and n_m>=a ell_m^2, the all-integer threshold is gamma(K,beta,a^{-1/2})>Gamma+1 and the sparse stretched-exponential threshold is gamma(K,beta,a^{-1/2})>Gamma.

CURRENT paper12 bounded Freedman all-plus addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-bounded-freedman-all-plus and cor:active-menu-rectangular-bounded-freedman-all-plus extend the nonsymmetric bounded all-plus route from conditionally independent centered matrices to adapted self-adjoint and rectangular martingale-difference streams. With ||X_i||op<=s and predictable quadratic variation ||sum_i E(X_i^2|F_{i-1})||op<=v, or the rectangular row/column analogues, matrix Freedman gives the same active all-plus tail 2 Delta exp{-u^2/(2(v+s u/3))}. The schedule cor:active-menu-bounded-bernstein-all-plus-schedules now covers Bernstein and Freedman streams uniformly: under v<=beta s^2 n/log(e Delta), log(e Delta)<=B sqrt(n), and summable exceptions, normalization by K s gives contractions and all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n) whenever sum_m J_m Delta_m(e Delta_m)^(-gamma(K,beta,B))<infty. This covers online feature blocks, adaptive measurement increments, and bounded self-adjoint or rectangular matrix martingale-difference libraries with predictable variance budgets.

CURRENT paper12 proxy-scale bounded all-plus addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-bounded-proxy-scale-all-plus-schedules separates the typical variance scale q from the worst jump b for the bounded Bernstein/Freedman all-plus route. In any of the self-adjoint/rectangular independent or adapted settings, if b<=R q, v<=beta q^2 n/log(e Delta), log(e Delta)<=B sqrt(n), and the environment exceptions are summable, then normalization by M q with M=max{R,K} gives contractions and all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n) whenever sum_m J_m Delta_m(e Delta_m)^(-gamma_pr(K,R,beta,B))<infty, where gamma_pr=K^2/(2(beta+KRB/3)). If J_m Delta_m<=(e Delta_m)^Gamma and n_m>=a log^2(e Delta_m), the all-integer threshold is gamma_pr(K,R,beta,a^{-1/2})>Gamma+1 and the sparse stretched-exponential threshold is gamma_pr(K,R,beta,a^{-1/2})>Gamma. This lets heterogeneous bounded matrix libraries use an RMS/typical proxy scale rather than the worst increment scale whenever the worst jump is within a fixed factor R.

CURRENT paper12 tail-capped centered Bernstein all-plus addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:tail-capped-centered-bernstein-all-plus-schedules extends the nonsymmetric independent all-plus route from bounded streams to unbounded self-adjoint and rectangular streams after deterministic norm capping. For caps b<=R q, summable cap failures, capped centered variance <=beta q^2 n/log(e Delta), and truncated mean drift <=eta q sqrt(n), normalization by max{R,K+eta} q gives contractions and all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n) whenever sum_m J_m Delta_m(e Delta_m)^(-gamma_tc(K,R,beta,B))<infty, with gamma_tc=K^2/(2(beta+2KRB/3)). If J_m Delta_m<=(e Delta_m)^Gamma and n_m>=a log^2(e Delta_m), the all-integer and sparse thresholds are the closed-form margins K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,beta,R) and K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,beta,R). For conditionally centered inputs, the drift is just the truncated tail mean.

CURRENT paper12 tail-capped Freedman all-plus addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:tail-capped-freedman-all-plus-schedules extends the tail-capped nonsymmetric all-plus route from independent streams to adapted martingale-difference streams. After deterministic capping, Y_i=T_i-E(T_i|F_{i-1}) is a bounded martingale difference with jump <=2Rq; if the predictable row/column capped variance is <=beta q^2 n/log(e Delta), cap failures are summable, and the predictable truncated-tail drift ||sum_i E(T_i|F_{i-1})|| is <=eta q sqrt(n), then normalization by max{R,K+eta}q gives contractions and all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n) whenever sum_m J_m Delta_m(e Delta_m)^(-gamma_tc(K,R,beta,B))<infty. The same closed-form margins Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,beta,R) and Theta_tc(Gamma,a,beta,R) give all-integer and sparse schedules.

CURRENT paper12 sub-Weibull norm-tail cap-selection addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:subweibull-norm-tail-capped-all-plus-schedules makes the tail-capped Bernstein/Freedman route directly checkable for independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular random matrix streams with conditional operator-norm sub-Weibull tails. With tail scale s, cap level L, cap b=s L^{1/alpha}, and variance scale q=s max{L^{1/alpha},sqrt(log(e Delta))}, a truncated row/column variance profile <= beta s^2 n implies the required capped Bernstein/Freedman variance because q^2/log(e Delta)>=s^2. The cap cost is only A_* n exp(-L), while the matrix-tail cost is J_m Delta_m(e Delta_m)^(-gamma_tc(K,1,beta,B)); normalization by max{1,K+eta}q gives contractions and all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n). If J_m Delta_m<=(e Delta_m)^Gamma, J_m n_m<=(e Delta_m)^Lambda, n_m>=a log^2(e Delta_m), and L_m=c log(e Delta_m), the all-integer thresholds are c>Lambda+1 and K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,beta,1), while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need c>Lambda and K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,beta,1). For centered or martingale-difference streams the conditional sub-Weibull tail makes the truncated-tail drift automatic for every fixed eta>0 under those cap choices.

CURRENT paper12 finite-moment norm-tail cap-selection addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:moment-norm-tail-capped-all-plus-schedules adds the polynomial-tail companion to the sub-Weibull norm-tail selector. If E(||X_i||op^p | P_i)<=A_* s^p, choose cap b=s U^{1/p} and variance scale q=s max{U^{1/p},sqrt(log(e Delta)) U^{(2-p)_+/(2p)}}. Markov gives cap cost A_* n U^{-1}; the truncated self-adjoint or rectangular row/column variance is <= A_* s^2 U^{(2-p)_+/p} n, hence <= A_* q^2 n/log(e Delta), so the tail-capped Bernstein/Freedman theorem applies with beta=A_* and normalization max{1,K+eta}q. If J_m Delta_m<=(e Delta_m)^Gamma, J_m n_m<=(e Delta_m)^Lambda, n_m>=a log^2(e Delta_m), and U_m=(e Delta_m)^c, all integer dimensions need c>Lambda+1 and K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,A_*,1), while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need c>Lambda and K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,A_*,1). For p>=1 centered or martingale-difference streams, the finite moment envelope also makes the truncated-tail drift automatic for every fixed eta>0 under those cap choices.

CURRENT paper12 heterogeneous norm-tail cap-profile addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:heterogeneous-norm-tail-cap-profiles allows each independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular increment to use its own deterministic cap b_i. If b_i<=R q, cap failures and environment exceptions are summable, the truncated-tail drift is <=eta q sqrt(n), and the uncentered truncated self-adjoint or rectangular row/column second-moment profile is <= beta q^2 n/log(e Delta), then the same tail-capped Bernstein/Freedman proof gives normalization by max{R,K+eta}q and all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n) whenever sum_m J_m Delta_m(e Delta_m)^(-gamma_tc(K,R,beta,B))<infty. The all-integer and sparse thresholds are still K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,beta,R) and K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,beta,R). For heterogeneous sub-Weibull norm tails P{||X_i||op>s_i u | P_i}<=A_* exp(-u^alpha), choosing b_i=s_i L^{1/alpha} and q=max{max_i b_i,(log(e Delta)/n sum_i s_i^2)^{1/2}} gives beta=C_alpha A_* and cap cost A_* n exp(-L). This keeps the variance scale sensitive to average tail energy rather than a worst-increment reduction.

CURRENT paper12 heterogeneous finite-moment norm-tail selector addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:heterogeneous-moment-norm-tail-selection specializes the heterogeneous cap-profile theorem to increment-dependent moment orders p_i, scales s_i, and Markov cap parameters U_i. Under conditional envelopes E(||X_i||op^{p_i}|P_i)<=A_*s_i^{p_i}, choose b_i=s_iU_i^{1/p_i}, rho_i=s_i^2U_i^{(2-p_i)_+/p_i}, and q=max{max_i b_i,(log(e Delta)/n sum_i rho_i)^{1/2}}. The cap-tail cost is A_*sum_i U_i^{-1}, while the uncentered self-adjoint or rectangular row/column variance profile is <=A_*sum_i rho_i<=A_*q^2n/log(e Delta), so the same gamma_tc(K,1,A_*,B), all-integer threshold K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,A_*,1), and sparse threshold K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,A_*,1) apply. For common U=(e Delta)^c, cap summability needs c>Lambda+1 for all integer dimensions and c>Lambda on stretched-exponential sparse schedules; if p_i>=1 and the stream is centered or martingale-difference, the truncated drift is automatic under those common-U schedules. This covers heterogeneous heavy-tailed matrix streams without reducing to the worst moment scale.

CURRENT paper12 heterogeneous tail-profile norm-tail selector addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:heterogeneous-tail-profile-norm-tail-selection lets each independent or adapted self-adjoint/rectangular increment use an arbitrary deterministic conditional survival profile pi_i for its operator norm, not just a sub-Weibull or moment envelope. If P{||X_i||op>s_i t | P_i}<=pi_i(t), choose cap b_i=s_i u_i, rho_i=2s_i^2 int_0^{u_i} t pi_i(t) dt, and q=max{max_i b_i,(log(e Delta)/n sum_i rho_i)^{1/2}}. The cap-tail cost is sum_i pi_i(u_i), the uncentered self-adjoint or rectangular row/column second-moment profile is <=sum_i rho_i<=q^2n/log(e Delta), and the same heterogeneous cap-profile theorem applies with beta=R=1. The matrix-tail thresholds are K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,1,1) for all integer dimensions and K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,1,1) on sparse schedules, provided the profile cap-tail series is summable. For centered or martingale-difference streams, the truncated drift follows from the deterministic tail-drift sum_i s_i[u_i pi_i(u_i)+int_{u_i}^\infty pi_i(t)dt]. This admits exact distributional survival curves, quantile tables, lognormal/log-Weibull profiles, and mixed custom tails directly.

CURRENT paper12 quantile-budget norm-tail selector addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:quantile-budget-norm-tail-profile-selection turns the heterogeneous tail-profile theorem into an explicit cap-allocation rule. For each increment profile pi_i, choose upper profile quantiles Q_i(delta) with pi_i(Q_i(delta))<=delta, a stream cap-failure budget epsilon, and shares omega_i>0 with sum omega_i<=1. Taking u_i=Q_i(epsilon omega_i), b_i=s_i u_i, rho_i=2s_i^2 int_0^{u_i} t pi_i(t)dt, and q=max{max_i b_i,(log(e Delta)/n sum_i rho_i)^{1/2}} makes the total cap-tail cost <=epsilon, so the tail-profile theorem applies with beta=R=1 under summability of P(E)+epsilon and the same matrix-tail series. If J_m<=(e Delta_m)^Lambda and epsilon=(e Delta_m)^(-c), cap budgets are summable for all integer dimensions when c>Lambda+1 and for sparse schedules when c>Lambda; the matrix thresholds remain K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,1,1) and K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,1,1). Shares may be optimized over the simplex to reduce the normalization scale for strongly heterogeneous tails.

CURRENT paper12 mixed sub-Weibull quantile-budget selector addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:mixed-subweibull-quantile-budget-norm-tail-selection specializes the quantile-budget tail-profile rule to increment-dependent sub-Weibull operator-norm tails P{||X_i||op>s_i t|P_i}<=min{1,A_i exp(-t^{alpha_i})}. For a stream cap budget epsilon and shares omega_i, choose lambda_i=log(e A_i/(epsilon omega_i)), cap b_i=s_i lambda_i^{1/alpha_i}, variance energy rhobar_i=(2A_i/alpha_i)Gamma(2/alpha_i)s_i^2, and q=max{max_i b_i,(log(e Delta)/n sum_i rhobar_i)^{1/2}}. The cap-tail charge is <=epsilon and the exact truncated second-moment profile is <=sum_i rhobar_i, so the quantile-budget theorem applies with beta=R=1 and the same matrix-tail thresholds. If J_m<=(e Delta_m)^Lambda and epsilon=(e Delta_m)^(-c), cap budgets are summable for all integer dimensions when c>Lambda+1 and for sparse schedules when c>Lambda; shares can now tune mixed tail constants/scales/exponents without replacing the variance energy by a worst-increment bound.

CURRENT paper12 mixed finite-moment quantile-budget selector addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:mixed-moment-quantile-budget-norm-tail-selection gives the polynomial-tail analogue of the mixed sub-Weibull budget rule. If E(||X_i||op^{p_i}|P_i)<=A_i s_i^{p_i}, choose a stream budget epsilon and shares omega_i, set lambda_i=eA_i/(epsilon omega_i), cap b_i=s_i lambda_i^{1/p_i}, and use moment energy rhobar_i=A_i s_i^2 lambda_i^{(2-p_i)/p_i} for p_i<2 and rhobar_i=A_i^{2/p_i}s_i^2 for p_i>=2. Markov charges at most epsilon to the whole stream, while the uncentered truncated self-adjoint or rectangular row/column second-moment profile is <=sum_i rhobar_i. Hence q=max{max_i b_i,(log(e Delta)/n sum_i rhobar_i)^{1/2}} gives the same beta=R=1 tail-capped Bernstein/Freedman conclusion and the same matrix thresholds; if J_m<=(e Delta_m)^Lambda and epsilon=(e Delta_m)^(-c), cap budgets are summable for all integer dimensions when c>Lambda+1 and for sparse schedules when c>Lambda. For p_i>1 centered or martingale-difference streams, the displayed drift follows from sum_i A_i s_i lambda_i^{-(p_i-1)/p_i}.

CURRENT paper12 optimized common-moment budget selector addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:optimized-common-moment-quantile-budget-norm-tail-selection evaluates the mixed finite-moment budget rule when the moment order is common. With S=sum_i A_i s_i^p, choose shares omega_i=A_i s_i^p/S, so every Markov cap is equal to r=(eS/epsilon)^{1/p}. The aggregate moment-energy term becomes V=(e/epsilon)^{(2-p)/p}S^{2/p} for p<2 and V=sum_i A_i^{2/p}s_i^2 for p>=2, hence q=max{r,(log(e Delta)V/n)^{1/2}}. The stream cap-tail charge is still <=epsilon, the matrix-tail thresholds remain K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,1,1) and K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,1,1), and for p>1 centered or martingale-difference streams the drift reduces to S r^{1-p}. This removes hand-chosen shares and reduces common-order heavy-tail inputs to one aggregate p-moment mass plus the p>=2 second-moment proxy.

CURRENT paper12 optimized common-exponent sub-Weibull budget selector addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:optimized-common-exponent-subweibull-budget-norm-tail-selection evaluates the mixed sub-Weibull budget rule when the operator-norm tail exponent alpha is common. With Phi(r)=sum_i A_i exp(-(r/s_i)^alpha), the smallest common cap is the inverse tail-budget level r*=inf{r: Phi(r)<=epsilon/e}; equivalently, this is exactly the smallest cap obtainable by any budget-share choice in the mixed sub-Weibull corollary. The exact capped variance energy is V=2 sum_i s_i^2 int_0^{r/s_i} t min{1,A_i exp(-t^alpha)} dt, bounded by (2/alpha)Gamma(2/alpha)sum_i A_i s_i^2, and q=max{r,(log(e Delta)V/n)^{1/2}}. This removes manual share tuning for common-exponent Gaussian/sub-Gaussian/sub-exponential/sub-Weibull matrix streams while preserving the same cap-budget summability and matrix-tail thresholds K>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,1,1) and K>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,1,1).

CURRENT paper12 closed-form common-exponent sub-Weibull schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:closed-form-common-exponent-subweibull-schedules turns the optimized common-exponent inverse-budget cap into a plug-in envelope. With A^Sigma=sum_i A_i, s^max=max_i s_i, and C_alpha=(2/alpha)Gamma(2/alpha), the deterministic cap r_env=s^max(log(e A^Sigma/epsilon))^{1/alpha} and variance envelope V_env=C_alpha sum_i A_i s_i^2 dominate the optimized common-exponent scale, so q_env=max{r_env,(log(e Delta)V_env/n)^{1/2}} gives the same eventual Matrix Spencer normalization under the same cap-budget and matrix-tail summability. If all scales are common, r_env is exactly the optimized cap; if A^Sigma<=(e Delta)^chi and epsilon=(e Delta)^(-c), then r_env<=s^max((chi+c+1)log(e Delta))^{1/alpha}. This gives a closed-form Gaussian/sub-Gaussian/sub-exponential/sub-Weibull random-matrix schedule without solving the inverse equation.

CURRENT paper12 closed-form common-moment growth schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:closed-form-common-moment-growth-schedules turns the optimized common-moment finite-tail selector into a deterministic envelope rule. If S=sum_i A_i s_i^p is bounded by Sbar, and for p>=2 the second-moment proxy sum_i A_i^{2/p}s_i^2 is bounded by Wbar, then r_env=(e Sbar/epsilon)^{1/p}, V_env=(e/epsilon)^{(2-p)/p}Sbar^{2/p} for p<2 and V_env=Wbar for p>=2 dominate the exact optimized scale. Thus q_env=max{r_env,(log(e Delta)V_env/n)^{1/2}} gives the same eventual Matrix Spencer normalization under the same cap-budget and matrix-tail summability; for p>1 the drift is implied by Sbar r_env^{1-p}<=eta q_env sqrt(n). If epsilon=(e Delta)^(-c), Sbar<=(e Delta)^chi, and Wbar<=(e Delta)^upsilon, then r_env<=(e Delta)^((chi+c+1)/p), V_env<=(e Delta)^(((2-p)(c+1)+2chi)/p) for p<2 and V_env<=(e Delta)^upsilon for p>=2. This gives a plug-in polynomial-growth schedule for heavy-tailed random matrix streams with common moment order.

CURRENT paper12 uniform common-moment operator-tail schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:uniform-common-moment-operator-tail-schedules specializes the closed-form common-moment envelope to dominated increments with E(||X_i||_op^p | P_i)<=A s^p. It sets r_unif=s(e n A/epsilon)^{1/p}, V_unif=s^2(e/epsilon)^((2-p)/p)(nA)^{2/p} for 0<p<2 and V_unif=n A^{2/p}s^2 for p>=2, and q_unif=max{r_unif,(log(e Delta)V_unif/n)^{1/2}}. Under the same cap-budget and matrix-tail summability, the streams are eventually normalized by max{1,K+eta}q_unif and the all-plus signing gives discrepancy <=sqrt(n). For p>1 the centered/martingale drift is implied by n A s^p r_unif^{1-p}=s(nA)^{1/p}(e/epsilon)^{-(p-1)/p}<=eta q_unif sqrt(n). If epsilon=(e Delta)^(-c), A<=(e Delta)^chi, and n<=(e Delta)^nu, then r_unif<=s(e Delta)^((nu+chi+c+1)/p), V_unif<=s^2(e Delta)^(((2-p)(c+1)+2(nu+chi))/p) for p<2, and V_unif<=s^2(e Delta)^(nu+2chi/p) for p>=2. This is the ready-to-use finite-moment random-matrix schedule for uniformly dominated operator tails.

CURRENT paper12 uniform common-exponent sub-Weibull schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:uniform-common-exponent-subweibull-schedules specializes the closed-form common-exponent tail selector to dominated tails P(||X_i||_op>t | P_i)<=A exp(-(t/s)^alpha). It sets r_psi_unif=s(log(e n A/epsilon))^{1/alpha}, V_psi_unif=C_alpha n A s^2 with C_alpha=(2/alpha)Gamma(2/alpha), and q_psi_unif=max{r_psi_unif,(log(e Delta)V_psi_unif/n)^{1/2}}. Under the same cap-budget and matrix-tail summability, the streams are eventually normalized by max{1,K+eta}q_psi_unif and all-plus discrepancy is <=sqrt(n). If epsilon=(e Delta)^(-c), A<=(e Delta)^chi, and n<=(e Delta)^nu, then r_psi_unif<=s((nu+chi+c+1)log(e Delta))^{1/alpha}, V_psi_unif<=C_alpha s^2(e Delta)^(nu+chi), and q_psi_unif is bounded by the max of that cap and s(C_alpha log(e Delta)(e Delta)^(nu+chi)/n)^{1/2}. This is the ready-to-use common sub-Weibull random-matrix schedule for uniformly dominated operator tails.

CURRENT paper12 mixed dominated operator-tail active-library addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:mixed-dominated-operator-tail-active-libraries lets each active stream in a triangular row be tagged either psi (uniformly dominated sub-Weibull tails with per-stream alpha) or mu (uniformly dominated p-moment tails with per-stream p). It uses equal cap-budget shares to set r and V by the appropriate dominated schedule, then q=max{r,(log(e Delta)V/n)^{1/2}}. Under the common cap-budget and matrix-tail summability, plus the corresponding truncated-tail drift outside E, all active streams are eventually normalized by max{1,K+eta}q and have all-plus discrepancy <=sqrt(n). The all-integer and sparse thresholds are unchanged: c>Lambda+1 and c>Lambda with the same Theta_tc margins. In centered/martingale cases, the psi drift is implied by s[epsilon u+nA int_u^\infty e^{-t^alpha}dt]<=eta q sqrt(n), while the mu drift for p>1 is implied by n A s^p r^{1-p}<=eta q sqrt(n). This packages countable mixed sub-Weibull/finite-moment dominated random-matrix libraries into one active-prefix criterion.

CURRENT paper12 signed-core mixed dominated operator-tail addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:signed-core-mixed-dominated-operator-tail-libraries combines the mixed dominated active-library selector with any active-prefix core library that already has one common measurable signing. For each active perturbation stream, tagged psi or mu, the theorem uses the same r,V,q as cor:mixed-dominated-operator-tail-active-libraries and scales by lambda=max{1,K_tail+eta}q. After adding a summable core-failure event to the mixed cap-budget and matrix-tail summability, the same core signs balance B+Y/lambda with norm at most (K_core+1)sqrt(n), the perturbations are contraction-normalized, and X/2 is a contraction tuple with common active-prefix discrepancy at most (K_core+1)sqrt(n)/2. The all-integer and sparse thresholds remain c>Lambda+1 and c>Lambda with the same Theta_tc margins for K_tail. This converts finite-lift, algebraic, Toeplitz/Hankel, and other already-signed core libraries into heavy-tailed mixed sub-Weibull/finite-moment random-perturbation classes under per-stream normalization.

CURRENT paper12 raw noncentered core-plus-tail addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:raw-mean-core-mixed-dominated-operator-tail keeps raw matrices W=B+Y instead of first scaling the perturbation inside the model. Under the same signed-core and mixed dominated tail assumptions, with lambda=max{1,K_tail+eta}q and Lambda=max{1,lambda}, the same core signing balances W/Lambda at (K_core+1)sqrt(n), the normalized rows W/Lambda have norm <=2, and W/(2Lambda) is a contraction tuple with common active-prefix discrepancy <=(K_core+1)sqrt(n)/2. In particular, structured conditional means B=E(W|H) absorb mixed dominated centered fluctuations Y=W-E(W|H), so noncentered random matrix libraries inherit the finite-lift/algebraic/Toeplitz/group-convolution mixed-tail schedules without rewriting them as already-scaled perturbations.

CURRENT paper12 scaled raw noncentered core-plus-tail addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:scaled-raw-mean-core-mixed-dominated-operator-tail removes the unit-size restriction on structured conditional means in the raw wrapper. If the core rows and their common signed sum are bounded at an environment-measurable scale rho_{m,h}, while the centered fluctuations have the same mixed dominated scale lambda_{m,h}=max{1,K_tail+eta}q_{m,h}, then the raw matrices W=B+Y are normalized by Lambda^sc=max{rho_{m,h},lambda_{m,h}}. The same active-prefix signs balance W/Lambda^sc at (K_core+1)sqrt(n_m), the normalized rows have norm <=2, and W/(2Lambda^sc) is a contraction tuple with common active-prefix discrepancy <=(K_core+1)sqrt(n_m)/2. This covers noncentered random matrix libraries whose structured conditional means have random or environment-dependent size rather than unit norm, with no change to the mixed tail summability or all-integer/sparse thresholds.

CURRENT paper12 two-scale raw noncentered core-plus-tail addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:two-scale-raw-mean-core-mixed-dominated-operator-tail decouples the structured conditional-mean row-size scale from the signed-core certificate scale. If ||B_{m,h,i}||op<=rho_{m,h} but the common signs only certify ||sum eps_i B_{m,h,i}||op<=K_core sigma_{m,h}sqrt(n_m), and the centered fluctuations have scale lambda_{m,h}=max{1,K_tail+eta}q_{m,h}, then W=B+Y is normalized by Lambda^rs=max{rho_{m,h},sigma_{m,h},lambda_{m,h}}. The same signs balance W/Lambda^rs at (K_core+1)sqrt(n_m), the normalized rows have norm <=2, and W/(2Lambda^rs) is a contraction tuple with common active-prefix discrepancy <=(K_core+1)sqrt(n_m)/2. This covers structured conditional means whose row bound and available Matrix Spencer signing bound come from different estimates, including finite-lift, algebraic, Toeplitz/Hankel, group-convolution, selected structured, and random-basis core constructions, with unchanged mixed-tail thresholds.

CURRENT paper12 two-scale raw structured-core addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:two-scale-raw-structured-core-mixed-dominated-operator-tail makes the abstract two-scale raw wrapper directly checkable for finite-lift and intrinsic algebraic core menus. After normalizing the structured conditional mean by a certificate scale sigma_{m,h}, the normalized cores B/sigma either admit active-prefix finite lifts with M_m^{(J)}<=M0 and E_m^{(J)}<=C_E n_m or lie in active finite-dimensional C*-algebras with total intrinsic dimension <=C0 n_m. A separate row bound rho_{m,h} is retained, the mixed centered fluctuation scale is lambda_{m,h}, and raw W=B+Y is normalized by Lambda^str=max{rho_{m,h},sigma_{m,h},lambda_{m,h}}. The same common signs balance W/Lambda^str at (K_str+1)sqrt(n_m), rows have norm <=2, W/(2Lambda^str) is a contraction tuple, and all mixed-tail all-integer/sparse thresholds are unchanged. This turns finite-lift, Toeplitz/Hankel, algebraic, group-convolution, selected structured, and random-basis raw conditional means into plug-in noncentered random-matrix classes when row and signing scales differ.

CURRENT paper12 finite-lift mixed dominated operator-tail menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-mixed-dominated-operator-tail-menu-borel specializes the signed-core mixed dominated theorem to active-prefix finite-lift core menus. Outside the usual summable finite-lift event, cor:finite-lift-common-menu gives one H_m-measurable signing with K_fl=C M0 sqrt(log(e+C_E)). Each active perturbation stream may then be tagged psi or mu, use the same r,V,q as cor:mixed-dominated-operator-tail-active-libraries, and be scaled by lambda=max{1,K_tail+eta}q. Under the mixed cap-budget, drift, and matrix-tail summability, the same finite-lift signs balance B+Y/lambda with norm at most (K_fl+1)sqrt(n), give contraction-normalized X/2, and cover every fixed menu element. The all-integer and sparse thresholds remain c>Lambda+1 and c>Lambda, with K_tail>Theta_tc(Gamma+1,a,1,1) and K_tail>Theta_tc(Gamma,a,1,1), after adding the summable finite-lift failure series. This turns the abstract signed-core mixed-tail result into a direct finite-lift random-matrix schedule.
