CURRENT paper12 algebraic-core mixed dominated operator-tail menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:algebraic-core-mixed-dominated-operator-tail-menu-borel specializes the signed-core mixed dominated theorem to active algebraic core menus. If outside a summable algebraic failure event each active core lies in a finite-dimensional C*-subalgebra and the active-prefix direct-sum intrinsic dimension satisfies sum_{h<=J_m} dim_C A_{m,h}<=C0 n_m, Akbas--Sra applied to the direct sum gives one H_m-measurable signing with K_alg=C(C0). Each active perturbation stream may be tagged psi or mu and scaled by lambda=max{1,K_tail+eta}q from the mixed dominated selector. Under the same cap-budget, drift, and matrix-tail summability, the same signs balance B+Y/lambda, X/2 is contraction-normalized, and all-integer/sparse thresholds remain c>Lambda+1 and c>Lambda with the same Theta_tc margins. This extends dimension-controlled random algebraic core menus from Gaussian operator-tail perturbations to mixed sub-Weibull/finite-moment dominated operator tails.
CURRENT paper12 structured-core mixed dominated catalogue addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:structured-core-catalogue-mixed-dominated-operator-tail records the named random-matrix families covered by the finite-lift and algebraic mixed dominated theorems. Finite-lift structured core menus include scalar Toeplitz/Hankel, one-level fixed-block Toeplitz/Hankel, fixed-level multilevel block Toeplitz/Hankel when the active-prefix symbol dimension is O(n_m), and banded multilevel block Toeplitz/Hankel when the active-prefix sum of anisotropic bandwidth products is O(n_m). Intrinsic algebraic menus include finite-dimensional C*-algebra cores and finite group convolution cores whenever the active-prefix sums of dim_C A_{m,h}, or |G_{m,h}|, are O(n_m). Each active perturbation stream may still be tagged psi or mu and scaled by its own lambda=max{1,K_tail+eta}q, with the same cap-budget, drift, matrix-tail, all-integer, and sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 concrete structured size-budget mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:structured-size-budget-mixed-dominated-operator-tail turns the structured catalogue into explicit active-prefix size tests. For finite-lift structured core menus with levels/block sizes bounded by r0,b0, the mixed dominated conclusion follows when the active-prefix sum of S_{m,h} is O(n_m), where S is d for scalar Toeplitz/Hankel, ambient D for one-level or fixed-level full-bandwidth block Toeplitz/Hankel, and prod_j(2w_{m,h,j}+1) for banded multilevel block Toeplitz/Hankel. For algebraic/group menus the same conclusion follows when sum_h dim_C A_{m,h} or sum_h |G_{m,h}| is O(n_m). This makes the abstract entropy/intrinsic-dimension budgets directly checkable for structured random matrix libraries with mixed sub-Weibull/finite-moment operator-tail perturbations.
CURRENT paper12 random-orbit closure structured mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:structured-orbit-closure-mixed-dominated-operator-tail shows the concrete structured mixed dominated theorem is stable under environment-measurable unitary or permutation conjugacy, corners/compressions, fixed-size matrix/tensor amplification, block direct sums, bounded self-adjoint images, and algebraic direct sums/amplifications. The active-prefix charge is sum_{h,a} c_{m,h,a}^2 S_{m,h,a} for finite-lift structured components, or c^2 dim_C A and c^2 |G| for algebraic/group components; O(n_m) charge gives the same mixed dominated perturbation conclusion and unchanged cap-budget, all-integer, and sparse thresholds. This expands the covered random matrix libraries to random-basis structured cores, randomly compressed or cornered structured cores, matrix-valued decorated symbols, and direct-sum structured blocks with mixed sub-Weibull/finite-moment operator-tail perturbations.
CURRENT paper12 selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail lets the active structured library itself be chosen randomly or adaptively from the row environment. Outside a summable selection event, the selected set only needs a deterministic cardinality envelope J_m and realized O(n_m) total charge, using the same c^2S, c^2 dim_C A, and c^2|G| charges from the orbit-closure theorem. After ordering the selected labels and padding by zero streams, the signed-core mixed dominated theorem gives one H_m-measurable signing for all selected structured core-plus-tail streams, with unchanged mixed cap-budget, matrix-tail summability, all-integer, and sparse thresholds. This covers random model-selection sublibraries, adaptive structured menus, random-basis/cornered/decorated structured cores selected from a countable catalogue, and mixed sub-Weibull/finite-moment perturbations.
CURRENT paper12 Bernstein-selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:bernstein-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail turns the selected-submenu structural event into explicit scalar-Bernstein checks. For a finite candidate catalogue with conditionally independent selection indicators eta_{m,k}, deterministic probability envelopes p_{m,k}, and charges mathfrak{s}_{m,k}<=b_m, the selected cardinality and charge are summably controlled once the Bernstein tails for sum eta_{m,k} and sum eta_{m,k} mathfrak{s}_{m,k} are summable and the deterministic envelopes satisfy mu^#+u<=J_m and mu^ch+t<=C0 n_m. Under the same candidate mixed dominated tail/drift and matrix-tail summability assumptions, the selected structured core-plus-tail streams inherit the common signing and unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds. This makes random model-selection sublibraries and independent Bernoulli thinning of structured catalogues directly checkable.
CURRENT paper12 Laplace-dominated selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:laplace-dominated-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail abstracts the Bernstein-selected proof to any selected catalogue whose selected charge and selected cardinality have product-Bernoulli Laplace transform domination with deterministic envelopes p_{m,k}. The same scalar Bennett/Bernstein tails control sum eta_{m,k} and sum eta_{m,k} mathfrak{s}_{m,k}; conditional negative association with marginal bounds E[eta_{m,k}|G_m]<=p_{m,k} is recorded as a sufficient condition. This brings negatively dependent, determinantal-style, and product-mgf-dominated structured sublibrary selections into the same mixed dominated common-signing theorem with unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 random-envelope Laplace selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-envelope-laplace-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail lets the Bernoulli/Laplace domination envelopes P_{m,k} be environment-measurable random variables. A summable environment event certifies deterministic aggregate bounds for expected selected cardinality, expected selected charge, and quadratic charge; scalar Bennett/Bernstein tails then make the selected cardinality and total charge summably small outside the environment and certificate failures. Conditional negative association with conditional means bounded by the random envelopes is recorded as a sufficient condition. The same selected-submenu theorem then yields common Spencer signings for these data-dependent random structured sublibraries with unchanged mixed dominated tail assumptions and unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 random-envelope Poisson-count selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-envelope-poisson-count-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail lets structured catalogues be activated by nonnegative integer proposal counts rather than direct Bernoulli indicators. Conditional compound-Poisson Laplace domination of the count-weighted cardinality and charge, with summable certification of the random aggregate intensity, quadratic-charge, and count profiles, controls the occupied support because 1_{N_{m,k}>=1}<=N_{m,k}. The selected-submenu theorem then gives the same common mixed dominated Matrix Spencer signing for Poissonized model proposals, Cox/occupancy libraries, random hyperparameter proposal clouds, and repeated-draw structured catalogues, with unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 Cox-Poisson occupied-support selected structured submenu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:cox-poisson-occupied-support-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail sharpens the Poisson-count selection theorem when, conditionally on the environment, the proposal counts are independent Poisson variables. The occupied indicators are independent Bernoulli variables with probabilities Q_{m,k}=1-exp(-Lambda_{m,k}), so the certified aggregate cardinality, charge, and quadratic-charge budgets use Q_{m,k} rather than the raw intensity Lambda_{m,k}. This keeps duplicate proposals from consuming extra selected-support budget and gives the same common mixed dominated Matrix Spencer signing for Poissonized repeated-draw catalogues, Cox proposal clouds, and occupancy libraries with unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 product-categorical selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:product-categorical-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail covers randomized one-template-per-slot structured libraries. The candidate list is partitioned into slots, each slot independently chooses at most one finite-lift/algebraic/group/orbit-closure candidate with deterministic probability envelopes, and the selected cardinality is deterministically bounded by the number of slots. A scalar Bernstein bound for the selected charge is the only selection tail needed before invoking the selected-submenu mixed dominated theorem. This adds product mixtures of structured templates, randomized hyperparameter menus, with-replacement draw copies, and blockwise architecture/library choices to the same common-signing theorem with unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 adaptive-categorical selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:adaptive-categorical-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail removes independence from the one-template-per-slot model. Slot choices are exposed through a filtration, each slot chooses at most one candidate, and deterministic predictable probability envelopes bound the conditional label probabilities. The selected cardinality is still deterministic, while scalar Freedman controls the selected charge. This covers online/state-dependent template choices, Markov hyperparameter menus, adaptive blockwise model selection, and sequential architecture/library choices under the same mixed dominated common-signing theorem and unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 random-envelope adaptive-categorical selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-envelope-adaptive-categorical-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail lets one-template-per-slot adaptive choices use state-dependent predictable probability envelopes. A summable environment event certifies deterministic bounds on the predictable aggregate charge drift and quadratic charge, and scalar Freedman then gives a summable charge-tail failure while the selected cardinality remains deterministically bounded by the number of slots. This covers posterior/state-dependent template probabilities, online hyperparameter menus, adaptive blockwise architecture choices, and sequential model-selection sublibraries under the same mixed dominated common-signing theorem and unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 Freedman-selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:freedman-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail removes conditional independence from the selected-submenu structural event. For sequential indicators eta_{m,k} adapted to a filtration and satisfying predictable envelopes E[eta_{m,k}|F_{m,k-1}]<=p_{m,k}, scalar Freedman bounds the selected cardinality and selected charge using the same deterministic mu^#, mu^ch, and v^ch profiles as the Bernstein-selected theorem. Once the Freedman tail series is summable and mu^#+u<=J_m, mu^ch+t<=C0 n_m, the selected-submenu theorem applies with unchanged mixed dominated perturbation assumptions and unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds. This covers adaptive model-selection sublibraries, online thinning, stopping-rule catalogues, and sequential random structured libraries.
CURRENT paper12 random-envelope Freedman-selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-envelope-freedman-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail lets arbitrary sequential adaptive selection indicators use state-dependent predictable inclusion envelopes P_{m,k}. A summable environment event certifies deterministic bounds on expected selected cardinality, expected selected charge, and quadratic charge; scalar Freedman then controls both selected cardinality and selected charge without conditional independence. The selected-submenu theorem gives the same mixed dominated common-signing conclusion and unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds for adaptive thinning, stopping-rule catalogues, online structured-library selection, and general sequential model-selection submenus.
CURRENT paper12 Serfling-selected structured submenu mixed dominated addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:serfling-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail covers dependent fixed-size selection of structured random-matrix libraries. A finite candidate catalogue is partitioned into strata, exactly R_{m,a} labels are sampled uniformly without replacement from each stratum, and Hoeffding--Serfling finite-population tails control the selected charge around R_{m,a} times the stratum average charge. If the stratum tail series is summable and the deterministic charge envelope sum_a(R_{m,a} bar{s}_{m,a}+t_{m,a}) is at most C0 n_m, then the selected-submenu theorem applies with cardinality J_m=sum_a R_{m,a}. The same mixed dominated tail/drift and matrix-tail assumptions give common Spencer signings and unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds for fixed-size model selection, stratified sublibrary sampling, minibatch-style catalogues, and sampling without replacement.
CURRENT paper12 hybrid selected structured submenu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:hybrid-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail lets a structured random-matrix library be partitioned into finitely many components, each selected by any mechanism already certified in the preceding selected-submenu corollaries or by a deterministic pathwise check. If the component selection failures are summable and their deterministic cardinality and charge budgets add to J_m and C0 n_m, then the union submenu satisfies the selected-submenu event. No independence, negative association, or common filtration is needed between components, so Bernoulli/Laplace, Cox-Poisson, categorical, adaptive Freedman, and stratified without-replacement sublibraries can be mixed in one active menu with unchanged mixed dominated tail assumptions and unchanged all-integer/sparse thresholds.
CURRENT paper12 random-budget hybrid selected structured submenu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-budget-hybrid-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail lets each component of a hybrid selected library have environment-measurable random cardinality and charge envelopes. A summable aggregate budget event certifies that the realized component envelopes add to deterministic J_m and C0 n_m bounds, while summable component selection failures give the pathwise selected-submenu event. This lets posterior component masses, Cox intensities, adaptive slot budgets, random stratum counts, and data-dependent mixture weights be combined across different selection mechanisms with no cross-component independence and unchanged mixed dominated tail assumptions.
CURRENT paper12 hierarchical random-budget selected structured submenu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:hierarchical-random-budget-selected-structured-submenu-mixed-dominated-operator-tail turns recursive random trees and adaptive search frontiers into ordinary selected structured submenus by flattening the realized terminal antichain. If the frontier size and total structural charge are summably certified by deterministic J_m and C0 n_m envelopes, and candidate mixed dominated tail failures are summable over the whole countable tree, then the same common Matrix Spencer signing, contraction normalization, and all-integer/sparse thresholds hold. This explicitly covers finite-depth or countably generated random trees, branching Poisson/Cox catalogues, adaptive beam-search libraries, hierarchical mixtures, random forests of structured cores, and recursive random basis/corner choices without requiring independence between generations, siblings, or branches.

CURRENT paper12 rectangular support addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:rectangular-low-overlap-support specializes the rectangular square-profile certificate to row/domain support projections. If X_i=P_i^L X_i P_i^R, ||X_i||op<=rho_i, and both weighted overlap operators sum_i rho_i^2 P_i^L and sum_i rho_i^2 P_i^R have norm <=C_0 n/log(e(d_1+d_2)), then the rectangular tuple has an O(sqrt n) signing, and sampled search adds only 2^-m. New cor:rectangular-coordinate-support-bernstein makes the bipartite coordinate-support case checkable: inhomogeneous row and column inclusion probabilities p^L_{ia}, p^R_{ib} give a scalar Bernstein tail with dimension factor d_1+d_2 and budgets mu=max row/column expected weighted occupancy and sigma^2=max row/column weighted second occupancy. New cor:active-menu-rectangular-coordinate-support-schedules gives the active-menu/countable-library Borel-Cantelli version. This directly covers sparse rectangular random operators, bipartite support masks, rectangular localized blocks, and row-column support models under left/right weighted occupancy control.
CURRENT paper12 dependent rectangular overlap-budget addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-dependent-rectangular-overlap-budget removes support independence from the rectangular low-overlap route. For arbitrary active rectangular streams X_{m,h,i}=P^L_{m,h,i}X_{m,h,i}P^R_{m,h,i} with random amplitudes rho_{m,h,i}, it is enough that the realized left and right weighted overlap operators sum_i rho^2 P^L and sum_i rho^2 P^R are at most C0 n_m/log(e(d1+d2)) outside summable overlap-budget events. Borel-Cantelli then gives simultaneous eventual Spencer-scale signings for active menus/countable exhaustions, with the usual sampled-certificate add-on. This covers arbitrary dependent rectangular support laws, random regular or biregular bipartite supports, matchings, without-replacement row/column choices, determinantal or negatively dependent supports, and adaptive block/subspace frontiers whenever their realized overlap budgets are summably certified.
CURRENT paper12 tail-capped dependent rectangular overlap-budget addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:active-menu-tail-capped-dependent-rectangular-overlap-budget makes the unbounded-coefficient version of the dependent rectangular low-overlap route explicit. Deterministic caps rho_{m,h,i}<=1 localize arbitrary rectangular streams to random left/right projections, and summability of the cap-tail budgets plus the realized capped overlap-budget exceptions gives simultaneous eventual Spencer-scale signings for active menus/countable exhaustions. Deterministic site caps q_{m,h,i,r,s} in [0,1] and joint cap-tail budgets delta_{m,h,i,r,s} give the weighted profile Xi^ent=max{max_r sum_i sum_s q_{i,r,s}^2 p_{i,r,s}, max_s sum_i sum_r q_{i,r,s}^2 p_{i,r,s}}. If Xi^ent<=C0 n_m/(2log(eD_m^rec)) and the cap tails plus D_m^rec exp{-c0 n_m/log(eD_m^rec)} are summable, every active stream has eventual Spencer-scale signings and sampled-sign certificates. Entrywise sub-Weibull site tails use q_{i,r,s}=s_{i,r,s}L_m^{1/alpha}; entrywise p0-moment site tails use q_{i,r,s}=s_{i,r,s}exp(L_m/p0). The cap-tail cost is A_*e^{-L_m}B_{m,h}^{pair}, B^{pair}=sum_i sum_{r,s}p_{i,r,s}, so when B^{pair}<=n_m the same all-integer and sparse thresholds as the closed-form theorem apply, but rare heavy coordinates are charged by q_{i,r,s}^2p_{i,r,s} instead of by a worst-case cap times the whole expected degree.

CURRENT paper12 block-design twirl addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:block-design-twirl extends the finite-design random-rotation theorem from fully isotropic bases to partially randomized block bases. Given an environment-measurable orthogonal decomposition I=sum_alpha P_{i,alpha} for each sample and conditionally independent block-Haar/block finite-design/block signed-permutation unitaries, the Matrix Spencer failure is controlled by the block square profile ||sum_{i,alpha} d_{i,alpha}^{-1} tr(P_{i,alpha}T_i^2P_{i,alpha}) P_{i,alpha}||op. If this profile is <=C_0 n/(2log(eD)) outside E, then disc(U_iT_iU_i^*)=O_{C_0}(sqrt n) with failure at most P(E)+D exp{-3C_0 n/(16log(eD))}. This covers sample-dependent random orthogonal decompositions and blockwise discrete random bases, not only global Haar or global irreducible designs.
CURRENT paper12 dominated approximate-twirl addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:dominated-approximate-twirl removes exact twirl identities from the random-basis route. If conditionally independent random unitaries satisfy E(U_i T_i^2 U_i^* | G) <= V_i in Loewner order and ||sum_i V_i||op <= C_0 n/(2log(eD)) outside E, then disc(U_iT_iU_i^*)=O_{C_0}(sqrt n) with failure at most P(E)+D exp{-3C_0 n/(16log(eD))}. In particular, approximate scalar 1-designs with E(UXU^*|G) <= tr(X)I/D + Gamma_i and approximate block designs with block twirl plus Gamma_i pay only the aggregate PSD error ||sum_i Gamma_i||op in the same square-function budget.

CURRENT paper12 predictable dominated-twirl addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-dominated-twirl-freedman upgrades the dominated approximate-twirl route from batch conditional independence to online/adapted random-basis streams. If T_i is predictable, U_i is chosen at time i, and E(U_i T_i^2 U_i^* | F_{i-1}) <= V_i with ||sum_i V_i||op <= C_0 n/(2log(eD)) outside E, then disc(U_iT_iU_i^*)=O_{C_0}(sqrt n) with failure at most P(E)+D exp{-3C_0 n/(16log(eD))}; independent random sign search adds only 2^{-M}. Predictable approximate scalar 1-designs and predictable approximate block designs pay only the one-step aggregate PSD twirl error in the same square-function budget, with no independence required between different times.

CURRENT paper12 predictable twirl-array addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-dominated-twirl-borel turns the online/adapted dominated-twirl theorem into an almost-sure triangular-array statement. If row m has predictable profile Xi_m=||sum_i V_{m,i}||op, deterministic thresholds v_m+t_m<=C_0 n_m/log(eD_m), and sum_m [P{Xi_m>v_m}+D_m exp{-t_m^2/(2(v_m+t_m/3))}]<infty, then disc(U_{m,i}T_{m,i}U_{m,i}^*)<=C(C_0)sqrt(n_m) eventually almost surely. If sampled sign batches have sum_m 2^{-M_m}<infty, the sampled signs themselves contain an O(sqrt n_m) certificate eventually almost surely. The concrete half-budget case uses Xi_m<=C_0 n_m/(2log(eD_m)) outside E_m and the summability of P(E_m)+D_m exp{-3C_0 n_m/(16log(eD_m))}. This packages predictable exact/approximate scalar and block random-basis arrays.

CURRENT paper12 predictable twirl schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-dominated-twirl-schedules makes the Borel predictable-twirl theorem checkable by growth rates. Under the half-budget event Xi_m<=C_0 n_m/(2log(eD_m)) outside summable E_m, the row failures are summable if n_m >= (16/(3C_0)) log(eD_m)(log(eD_m)+(1+delta)log(e+m)) eventually. In particular, along all integer dimensions D=2,3,... it is enough that n(D)>=a log^2(eD) with a>32/(3C_0), while along stretched-exponential sparse schedules D_m>=exp(c m^kappa) it is enough that n_m>=a log^2(eD_m) with a>16/(3C_0). Sampled sign certificates are eventual almost surely whenever additionally sum_m 2^{-M_m}<infty. This gives explicit all-dimension and sparse-dimension regimes for predictable exact/approximate scalar and block random-basis arrays.

CURRENT paper12 finite-menu predictable twirl schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-menu-predictable-dominated-twirl-schedules upgrades the predictable dominated-twirl Borel schedule from one stream to an active finite menu h<=J_m. If each stream has Xi_{m,h}<=C_0 n_m/(2log(eD_m)) outside E_{m,h}, sum_m sum_{h<=J_m} P(E_{m,h})<infty, and L_m^tw=J_mD_m, then simultaneous almost-sure signings for all active h hold whenever sum_m L_m^tw exp{-3C_0 n_m/(16log(eD_m))}<infty. The explicit general schedule is n_m >= (16/(3C_0)) log(eD_m)(log(eL_m^tw)+(1+delta)log(e+m)); if L_m^tw<=(eD_m)^Gamma, all integer dimensions need n_D>=a log^2(eD) with a>16(Gamma+1)/(3C_0), while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need a>16Gamma/(3C_0). Independent sampled sign batches add sum_h 2^{-M_{m,h}}; one common batch adds J_m2^{-M_m}. This makes finite active libraries and countable exhaustions of predictable scalar/block/approximate twirl streams simultaneous.

CURRENT paper12 predictable scalar-multiplier array addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-scalar-multiplier-borel and cor:predictable-scalar-multiplier-schedules give the Borel-Cantelli and explicit schedule analogues of cor:predictable-scalar-multiplier-freedman. For triangular arrays A_{m,i}=theta_{m,i}H_{m,i} with H_{m,i} predictable self-adjoint contractions, |theta_{m,i}|<=1, q_{m,i}=E(theta_{m,i}^2|F_{m,i-1}), and nu_m=||sum_i q_{m,i}H_{m,i}^2||op, summability of P{nu_m>v_m}+D_m exp{-t_m^2/(2(v_m+t_m/3))} gives eventual O(sqrt n_m) signings, and sampled sign certificates follow if sum_m 2^{-M_m}<infty. Under the half-budget event nu_m<=C_0 n_m/(2log(eD_m)) outside summable E_m, the same explicit schedules as the twirl case apply: general n_m >= (16/(3C_0))log(eD_m)(log(eD_m)+(1+delta)log(e+m)), all integer dimensions from n(D)>=a log^2(eD) with a>32/(3C_0), and stretched-exponential sparse dimensions from a>16/(3C_0). This covers arbitrary adaptive matrix directions after bounded online thinning or scalar amplitudes at logarithmic conditional second-moment density.

CURRENT paper12 signed-core predictable scalar-noise array addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-martingale-scalar-noise-borel turns the finite-row signed-core predictable scalar martingale-noise theorem into an almost-sure triangular-array theorem. If row m has a G_m-measurable core signing with ||sum_i eps^*_{m,i}B_{m,i}||op<=K_m sqrt(n_m), predictable scalar martingale noise X_{m,i}=B_{m,i}+theta_{m,i}H_{m,i}, and nu_m=||sum_i E(theta_{m,i}^2|F_{m,i-1})H_{m,i}^2||op, then summability of P(E_m)+P{nu_m>v_m}+2D_m exp{-s_m^2/(2(v_m+s_m/3))} gives disc(X_{m,1},...,X_{m,n_m})<=K_m sqrt(n_m)+s_m eventually almost surely. In the profile form K_m<=K, nu_m<=Q n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable events, and log(eD_m)<=B sqrt(n_m), choosing L with L^2/(2(Q+LB/3))>=A+1 and sum_m(eD_m)^-A<infty gives eventual (K+L)sqrt(n_m). Thus all integer dimensions with n(D)>=a log^2(eD) and sparse stretched-exponential dimensions with the same logarithmic-square sample scale preserve already-known algebraic/finite-lift/Toeplitz/Hankel core signings under bounded predictable scalar martingale noise.

CURRENT paper12 signed-core matrix martingale array addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-matrix-martingale-noise-borel gives the matrix-valued analogue of the signed-core predictable scalar-noise array theorem. For triangular arrays X_{m,i}=B_{m,i}+Y_{m,i}, where B_{m,i} has a G_m-measurable K_m sqrt(n_m) core signing outside E_m and Y_{m,i} are bounded self-adjoint martingale differences with nu_m=||sum_i E(Y_{m,i}^2|F_{m,i-1})||op, summability of P(E_m)+P{nu_m>v_m}+2D_m exp{-s_m^2/(2(v_m+s_m/3))} gives disc(X_{m,1},...,X_{m,n_m})<=K_m sqrt(n_m)+s_m eventually almost surely. In the profile form K_m<=K, nu_m<=Q n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable events, log(eD_m)<=B sqrt(n_m), and sum_m(eD_m)^-A<infty, every L with L^2/(2(Q+LB/3))>=A+1 gives eventual (K+L)sqrt(n_m). Hence all integer dimensions with n(D)>=a log^2(eD) and sparse stretched-exponential dimensions at the same sample scale preserve already-known cores under arbitrary bounded matrix-valued martingale noise, with the scalar theorem as a special case.

CURRENT paper12 signed-core sampled localized matrix-noise array addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-sampled-localized-matrix-noise-borel specializes the signed-core matrix martingale array theorem to conditionally sampled localized matrix noise. For triangular arrays X_{m,i}=B_{m,i}+Y_{m,i}, where Y_{m,i}=P_{m,i}Y_{m,i}P_{m,i}, ||Y_{m,i}||op<=rho_{m,i}, rho_{m,i} is predictable, and mu_m=||sum_i rho_{m,i}^2 E(P_{m,i}|F_{m,i-1})||op, summability of P(E_m)+P{mu_m>v_m}+2D_m exp{-s_m^2/(2(v_m+s_m/3))} gives disc(X_m)<=K_m sqrt(n_m)+s_m eventually almost surely. In the profile form mu_m<=Q n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable events, the same all-integer n(D)>=a log^2(eD) and sparse stretched-exponential schedules give eventual (K+L)sqrt(n_m). The theorem also replaces mu_m by the coordinate weighted occupancy W_m=max_j sum_i rho_{m,i}^2 q_{m,ij} or the conditional Haar weighted average-rank W_m=sum_i rho_{m,i}^2 r_{m,i}/D_m, so adaptive sparse-coordinate and Haar-subspace matrix martingale noise now have explicit almost-sure schedules on top of any already-signed algebraic/finite-lift/Toeplitz/Hankel core.

CURRENT paper12 scalar-multiplier Matrix Spencer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-scalar-multiplier-bernstein applies the square-profile Bernstein route conditionally to arbitrary random self-adjoint contraction environments B_i after independent bounded scalar multipliers theta_i. With q_i=E(theta_i^2|B) and nu=||sum_i q_i B_i^2||op, the Matrix Spencer failure probability is at most P{nu>v}+D exp{-t^2/(2(v+t/3))} whenever v+t <= C_0 n/log(eD). In particular, if q_i<=p<=C_0/(2 log(eD)) almost surely, then arbitrary deterministic or random matrix directions become Spencer-scale after Bernoulli thinning of density p, or any bounded scalar amplitudes with the same conditional second-moment bound, with failure at most D exp{-3 C_0 n/(16 log(eD))}; no support, commutation, or low-rank structure is required for the B_i.

CURRENT paper12 core-plus-centered-noise Matrix Spencer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-centered-scalar-noise shows that any random core tuple B_i with an environment-measurable K sqrt(n) signing remains balanced after adding centered independent bounded scalar noise theta_i H_i in arbitrary self-adjoint contraction directions H_i. With q_i=E(theta_i^2|B,H) and nu=||sum_i q_i H_i^2||op, the same core signs give failure at most P(E)+P{nu>v}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(v+s/3))} for threshold K sqrt(n)+s. In particular, if q_i<=p, the variance exception disappears with v=pn. This packages algebraic cores, finite-lift cores, Toeplitz/Hankel cores, and other already-signed classes plus centered Bernoulli or bounded scalar-amplitude noise, with explicit matrix-Bernstein tails.

After centered full operator-tail noise Y_i whose scalar linear combinations obey the Gaussian tail conditionally on the core, scaled by K_0(sqrt(D)+sqrt(3log(en))), the same core signs give discrepancy at most (K+1)sqrt(n) and normalized contractions with failure at most P(E)+2(n+1)(en)^-3. This packages finite-lift, Toeplitz, Hankel, algebraic, and other already-signed random cores plus GOE/GUE/Wigner-type/correlated-Gaussian operator-tail noise in arbitrary ambient dimension. New cor:random-sign-search-square-profile also makes the square-function route algorithmic: under ||sum_i A_i^2||op<=C_0 n/log(eD), m independent random sign trials fail to find a C sqrt(C_0 n) signing with only an additional 2^-m probability.

CURRENT paper12 predictable square-profile Matrix Spencer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-square-profile-freedman extends the square-function/Matrix-Bernstein route from independent random contractions to adapted dependent random contractions. For a filtration F_i and self-adjoint contractions A_i measurable at time i, with predictable profile nu=||sum_i E(A_i^2|F_{i-1})||op, matrix Freedman gives Matrix Spencer failure at most P{nu>v}+D exp{-t^2/(2(v+t/3))} whenever v+t<=C_0 n/log(eD). If nu<=C_0 n/(2log(eD)) almost surely, this gives the same D exp{-3C_0 n/(16log(eD))} failure as the independent Bernstein theorem. The corollary also gives the randomized sign-search certificate under the same predictable profile, adding only 2^-m for m independent trials.

CURRENT paper12 matrix-valued martingale core-noise Matrix Spencer addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-matrix-martingale-noise upgrades the signed-core noise package from scalar amplitudes to arbitrary bounded self-adjoint matrix martingale differences. With core matrices B_i and a K sqrt(n) core signing measurable in an initial sigma-field, adapted self-adjoint martingale differences Y_i with ||Y_i||op<=1 obey the same core-signing failure bound P(E)+P{nu>v}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(v+s/3))}, where nu=||sum_i E(Y_i^2|F_{i-1})||op. New cor:core-plus-localized-matrix-martingale-noise specializes this to online coordinate-supported, subspace-localized, or frame-localized matrix martingale noise using the predictable overlap operator ||sum_i P_i||op. This packages algebraic, finite-lift, Toeplitz/Hankel, and other already-signed random cores plus bounded matrix-valued martingale noise without independence.

CURRENT paper12 conditionally sampled localized matrix-noise addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-sampled-localized-matrix-martingale-noise removes the predictability requirement from the support projections in the signed-core matrix martingale-noise theorem. If P_i is sampled at time i, Y_i=P_iY_iP_i is a bounded self-adjoint matrix martingale difference, and the core B_i has an initial K sqrt(n) signing outside E, then the same core signs give failure at most P(E)+P{mu>v}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(v+s/3))}, where mu=||sum_i E(P_i|F_{i-1})||op. In particular, predictable conditional overlap sum_i E(P_i|F_{i-1}) <= p n I gives the explicit tail P(E)+2D exp{-s^2/(2(pn+s/3))}. This covers already-signed algebraic, finite-lift, Toeplitz/Hankel, and other random cores plus conditionally sampled coordinate-supported or random-subspace matrix martingale noise.

CURRENT paper12 sampled coordinate/Haar core-noise specialization (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-sampled-coordinate-haar-matrix-noise makes the previous conditional-overlap theorem explicit for two high-use random matrix noise models on top of any already-signed core. For adaptive coordinate supports, conditional inclusion probabilities P{j in S_i|F_{i-1}}<=p_i and sum_i p_i<=V imply the core signs fail only with P(E)+2D exp{-s^2/(2(V+s/3))}. For conditional Haar rank-r_i subspaces, the same tail holds under sum_i r_i/D<=V, since E(P_i|F_{i-1})=(r_i/D)I. Thus algebraic, finite-lift, Toeplitz/Hankel, and other core signings survive adaptive sparse-coordinate or conditional Haar-subspace matrix martingale noise at the Freedman scale.

CURRENT paper12 weighted sampled-localized matrix-noise addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-weighted-sampled-localized-matrix-noise adds predictable amplitude weights to the signed-core sampled-projection martingale-noise theorem. If P_i is sampled at time i, Y_i=P_iY_iP_i is a bounded self-adjoint matrix martingale difference with predictable size ||Y_i||op<=rho_i, and the core B_i has an initial K sqrt(n) signing outside E, then the same core signs give failure at most P(E)+P{mu_rho>v}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(v+s/3))}, where mu_rho=||sum_i rho_i^2 E(P_i|F_{i-1})||op. In particular, deterministic weighted conditional overlap sum_i rho_i^2 E(P_i|F_{i-1}) <= V I gives the explicit tail P(E)+2D exp{-s^2/(2(V+s/3))}. This sharpens adaptive sparse-coordinate, conditional Haar-subspace, and other sampled-projection matrix martingale noise whenever the amplitudes are predictably small.

CURRENT paper12 weighted coordinate/Haar core-noise strengthening (local, not yet pushed): cor:core-plus-sampled-coordinate-haar-matrix-noise is now strengthened from the unweighted coordinate/Haar specialization to the weighted sampled version. For coordinate supports, predictable coordinate-dependent bounds P{j in S_i|F_{i-1}}<=q_{ij} and max_j sum_i rho_i^2 q_{ij}<=V imply the same core-signing failure tail P(E)+2D exp{-s^2/(2(V+s/3))}. For conditional Haar rank-r_i projections the corresponding hypothesis is sum_i rho_i^2 r_i/D<=V. The previous theorem is recovered by taking rho_i=1 and q_{ij}=p_i, while the new form also covers nonuniform coordinate sampling and predictably small matrix-noise amplitudes.

CURRENT paper12 random-budget weighted coordinate/Haar addendum (local, not yet pushed): cor:core-plus-sampled-coordinate-haar-matrix-noise now exposes the random weighted sampling budget instead of requiring an almost-sure deterministic budget up front. In the coordinate case W=max_j sum_i rho_i^2 q_{ij}, where P{j in S_i|F_{i-1}}<=q_{ij}; in the conditional Haar case W=sum_i rho_i^2 r_i/D. The signed-core failure tail is now P(E)+P{W>v}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(v+s/3))} for every deterministic v>=0, with the previous deterministic V theorem recovered from W<=V a.s. This covers adaptive/nonuniform sampling models whose predictable weighted occupancy is controlled only with high probability.

CURRENT paper12 coordinate-noise random-amplitude addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-bernoulli-coordinate-random-amplitude-noise makes the random-budget signed-core theorem explicit for coordinate-supported matrix martingale noise with independent random amplitudes. With deterministic coordinate inclusion bounds p_{ij}, independent amplitudes rho_i in [0,1], a_i=E rho_i^2, mu=max_j sum_i p_{ij} a_i, sigma^2=max_j sum_i p_{ij}^2 a_i, and p_*=max_{i,j}p_{ij}, the same core signing fails with probability at most P(E)+D exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+p_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu+u+s/3))}. This packages signed algebraic/finite-lift/Toeplitz/Hankel cores plus sparse coordinate matrix martingale noise whose coordinate sampling profile is deterministic but whose amplitudes are random.

CURRENT paper12 homogeneous coordinate random-amplitude addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-homogeneous-coordinate-random-amplitude-noise specializes the coordinate-noise random-amplitude theorem to the common uniform-density case p_{ij}<=p. With total amplitude energy A=sum_i E rho_i^2, the random weighted coordinate budget has scalar-Bernstein profile governed by pA, p^2A, and p: the signed-core failure is at most P(E)+D exp{-u^2/(2(p^2A+pu/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(pA+u+s/3))}. Taking u=pA gives the simple tail P(E)+D exp{-3A/8}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(2pA+s/3))}. Thus small amplitude energy improves the sparse-coordinate noise scale from pn to pA for already-signed algebraic, finite-lift, Toeplitz/Hankel, and other cores.

CURRENT paper12 Haar-subspace random-amplitude addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-haar-rank-random-amplitude-noise specializes the random weighted Haar-subspace budget to independent random amplitudes. For fixed ranks r_i, alpha_i=r_i/D, and a_i=E rho_i^2, the budget W=sum_i alpha_i rho_i^2 has scalar-Bernstein profile with mean mu=sum_i alpha_i a_i, variance proxy sigma^2=sum_i alpha_i^2 a_i, and jump alpha_*=max_i alpha_i. The signed-core failure is at most P(E)+exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+alpha_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu+u+s/3))}. If all rank fractions are <=alpha and A=sum_i E rho_i^2, the effective overlap scale is alpha A, with the simple u=alpha A tail P(E)+exp{-3A/8}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(2alpha A+s/3))}. This is the Haar-subspace analogue of the coordinate random-amplitude corollary, without the coordinate-union factor in the budget exception.

CURRENT paper12 random-rank Haar-subspace random-amplitude addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-random-rank-haar-random-amplitude-noise removes the fixed-rank restriction from the Haar-subspace random-amplitude theorem. Independent pairs (R_i,rho_i), with alpha_i=R_i/D and alpha_i<=alpha_* a.s., give weighted budget W=sum_i alpha_i rho_i^2. Scalar Bernstein gives mean mu=sum_i E(alpha_i rho_i^2), variance proxy sigma^2=sum_i E(alpha_i^2 rho_i^2), and jump alpha_*, so the signed-core failure is at most P(E)+exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+alpha_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu+u+s/3))}. If alpha_i<=alpha a.s. and A=sum_i E rho_i^2, the effective overlap scale is alpha A. This covers random-rank conditional Haar subspace martingale noise on top of algebraic, finite-lift, Toeplitz/Hankel, and other already-signed cores.

CURRENT paper12 isotropic projection random-amplitude addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-isotropic-projection-random-amplitude-noise replaces the Haar-distribution requirement by the intrinsic conditional-mean hypothesis E(P_i|F_{i-1})=alpha_i I. This covers Haar projections, random tight fusion-frame draws, unitary-design projection laws, and transitive group-orbit subspace sampling whenever the conditional projection mean is scalar.

CURRENT paper12 scalar variance-dominated martingale-noise addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-scalar-variance-random-amplitude-noise removes the support-projection model from the signed-core matrix martingale-noise tail. Scalar Bernstein gives mean mu=sum_i E(theta_i rho_i^2), variance proxy sigma^2=sum_i E(theta_i^2 rho_i^2), and failure tail P(E)+exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+theta_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu+u+s/3))}. This covers random matrix martingale noise through conditional second moments alone, before imposing coordinate, block, Haar, or projection support structure.

CURRENT paper12 anisotropic matrix-profile variance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-matrix-profile-variance-random-amplitude-noise upgrades scalar variance domination to random PSD variance profiles. This covers anisotropic random covariance/variance profiles and leverage-profile matrix martingale noise beyond scalar or projection-isotropic models.

CURRENT paper12 trace-budget variance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-trace-budget-variance-random-amplitude-noise gives a complementary signed-core matrix martingale-noise tail when only the total trace of the random variance profile is concentrated. Under E(Y_i^2|F_{i-1}) <= rho_i^2 V_i and tr(V_i)<=gamma_* with independent pairs (V_i,rho_i), the scalar trace budget T=sum_i rho_i^2 tr(V_i) controls ||sum_i E(Y_i^2|F_{i-1})||op. Scalar Bernstein gives mean mu_tr=sum_i E(rho_i^2 tr V_i), variance proxy sigma_tr^2=sum_i E(rho_i^4(tr V_i)^2), and failure tail P(E)+exp{-u^2/(2(sigma_tr^2+gamma_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu_tr+u+s/3))}. This removes the ambient D factor from the budget-exception term at the cost of using trace rather than operator-norm variance.

CURRENT paper12 trace-envelope/low-rank variance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-trace-envelope-low-rank-random-amplitude-noise turns the trace-budget theorem into a usable envelope theorem. If E(Y_i^2|F_{i-1}) <= rho_i^2 V_i and independent scalar envelopes Gamma_i satisfy tr(V_i)<=Gamma_i<=gamma_*, then the same signed-core martingale-noise tail holds with mu_Gamma=sum_i E(rho_i^2 Gamma_i), sigma_Gamma^2=sum_i E(rho_i^4 Gamma_i^2), and failure tail P(E)+exp{-u^2/(2(sigma_Gamma^2+gamma_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu_Gamma+u+s/3))}. The low-rank specialization takes Gamma_i=R_i tau_i whenever rank(V_i)<=R_i and ||V_i||op<=tau_i, giving explicit random low-rank/spiked/effective-dimension variance-profile coverage.

CURRENT paper12 Schatten-envelope variance addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-schatten-envelope-random-amplitude-noise interpolates the trace-envelope theorem across Schatten profiles. For 1<=p<=infinity, beta=1-1/p (beta=1 at p=infinity), rank(V_i)<=R_i, ||V_i||_{S_p}<=L_i, and R_i^beta L_i<=gamma_* give the same signed-core martingale-noise tail with mu_p=sum_i E(R_i^beta L_i rho_i^2), sigma_p^2=sum_i E(R_i^{2 beta} L_i^2 rho_i^4), and failure term P(E)+exp{-u^2/(2(sigma_p^2+gamma_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu_p+u+s/3))}. This packages trace control (p=1), Frobenius-plus-rank control (p=2), and operator-norm-plus-rank control (p=infinity) in one random variance-profile statement.

CURRENT paper12 sub-gamma trace-envelope addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-subgamma-trace-envelope-random-amplitude-noise removes the bounded-envelope hypothesis from the trace-envelope route. With independent envelopes Gamma_i satisfying tr(V_i)<=Gamma_i and Z_i=rho_i^2 Gamma_i, if the centered sum sum_i(Z_i-EZ_i) obeys the sub-gamma mgf bound log E exp{lambda sum_i(Z_i-EZ_i)} <= lambda^2 sigma^2/(2(1-c lambda)), then the same signed-core martingale-noise failure tail is P(E)+exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+c u))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu+u+s/3))}, where mu=sum_i E Z_i. This covers unbounded random covariance/variance trace envelopes whenever the total weighted envelope has a Bernstein-type upper tail.

CURRENT paper12 compound-Poisson trace-envelope addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-compound-poisson-trace-envelope-random-amplitude-noise specializes the unbounded trace-envelope route to compound-Poisson dominated weighted envelopes. If rho_i^2 Gamma_i <= W_i, W_i=sum_{ell<=N_i} Z_{iell}, N_i~Poisson(lambda_i), 0<=Z_{iell}<=a_i, and the W_i are independent, then the signed-core tail is P(E)+exp{-u^2/(2(sigma_P^2+a_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu_P+u+s/3))}, with mu_P=sum_i lambda_i E Z_{i1}, sigma_P^2=sum_i lambda_i E Z_{i1}^2, and a_*=max_i a_i. The deterministic-jump case W_i=a_i N_i covers Poisson random counts of bounded trace-envelope atoms and bounded spike-count variance profiles.

CURRENT paper12 quantile trace-envelope addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-quantile-trace-envelope-random-amplitude-noise gives a confidence-level version of the trace-envelope route. If the weighted trace budget sum_i rho_i^2 Gamma_i is dominated by an arbitrary nonnegative random variable S, with no independence required among the envelope data, then P{disc>K sqrt n+s}<=P(E)+P{S>v}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(v+s/3))}. Hence any upper delta-quantile q_delta of S gives failure P(E)+delta+2D exp{-s^2/(2(q_delta+s/3))}, and a finite r-th moment E S^r<=M_r gives q_delta=(M_r/delta)^{1/r}. This extends the signed-core random-matrix coverage to heavy-tailed trace-class covariance, spike, low-rank, and Schatten variance profiles outside the Bernstein/sub-gamma regime.

CURRENT paper12 almost-sure quantile trace-envelope addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-quantile-trace-envelope-borel-random-amplitude-noise turns the heavy-tail quantile theorem into a triangular-array Borel-Cantelli transfer. For random instances X_{m,1},...,X_{m,n_m} with dominating budgets S_m, deterministic upper quantiles q_m, budget failures delta_m, and noise radii s_m, summability of P(E_m)+delta_m+2D_m exp{-s_m^2/(2(q_m+s_m/3))} implies almost surely eventual disc(X_m)<=K_m sqrt(n_m)+s_m. In the concrete Spencer-scale profile, if q_m<=Q n_m/log(eD_m), log(eD_m)<=B sqrt(n_m), and P(E_m)+delta_m+(eD_m)^{-A} is summable, then choosing L with L^2/(2(Q+LB/3))>=A+1 gives eventual disc(X_m)<=(K+L)sqrt(n_m). Moment bounds E S_m^r<=M_{m,r} feed this by q_m=(M_{m,r}/delta_m)^{1/r}. This gives almost-sure Matrix Spencer bounds for heavy-tailed trace-envelope random-matrix arrays with no independence across dimensions.

CURRENT paper12 moment-summable trace-envelope addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-moment-trace-envelope-borel-random-amplitude-noise removes explicit quantile bookkeeping from the heavy-tail Borel theorem. If K_m<=K, ell_m=log(eD_m)<=B sqrt(n_m), and for some r,Q,A the series sum_m [P(E_m)+E(ell_m S_m/(Q n_m))^r+(eD_m)^(-A)] is finite, then choosing L with L^2/(2(Q+LB/3))>=A+1 gives almost surely eventual disc(X_{m,1},...,X_{m,n_m})<=(K+L)sqrt(n_m). Equivalently, E S_m^r<=Q^r(n_m/ell_m)^r a_m with sum a_m<infty is enough. Thus normalized moment summability of heavy-tailed trace budgets at the n/log(eD) scale implies eventual Matrix Spencer bounds without exponential tails or independence across dimensions.

CURRENT paper12 random coordinate-profile random-amplitude addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-random-coordinate-profile-random-amplitude-noise removes the deterministic inclusion-profile restriction from the coordinate random-amplitude theorem. Independent pairs (Q_i,rho_i), with Q_i=(Q_{i1},...,Q_{iD}) and Q_{ij}<=q_* a.s., give random weighted coordinate budget W=max_j sum_i Q_{ij} rho_i^2. Scalar Bernstein plus a coordinate union bound gives mean mu=max_j sum_i E(Q_{ij}rho_i^2), variance proxy sigma^2=max_j sum_i E(Q_{ij}^2 rho_i^2), and jump q_*: the signed-core failure is at most P(E)+D exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+q_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu+u+s/3))}. If Q_{ij}<=p a.s. and A=sum_i E rho_i^2, the effective overlap scale is pA. This covers sparse coordinate matrix martingale noise whose sampling profile is itself random but independent across samples.

CURRENT paper12 random block-profile random-amplitude addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-random-block-profile-random-amplitude-noise extends the coordinate random-profile theorem from one-dimensional coordinate supports to arbitrary fixed orthogonal block decompositions Pi_1,...,Pi_m. If T_i is the random selected block set, P{ell in T_i|F_{i-1}}<=Q_{iell}, and independent random profiles/amplitudes obey Q_{iell}<=q_* a.s., then W=max_ell sum_i Q_{iell}rho_i^2 controls the signed-core matrix martingale noise. Scalar Bernstein gives mean mu=max_ell sum_i E(Q_{iell}rho_i^2), variance proxy sigma^2=max_ell sum_i E(Q_{iell}^2rho_i^2), and failure tail P(E)+m exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+q_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu+u+s/3))}. Thus random block-supported matrix noise pays a budget-exception union over the number of structural blocks, not over the ambient matrix size.

CURRENT paper12 mixed coordinate/Haar random-amplitude addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-mixed-coordinate-haar-random-amplitude-noise allows each independent sample to choose between a coordinate-supported matrix martingale noise model and a Haar-subspace matrix martingale noise model. With mode M_i in {0,1}, random coordinate profile Q_i, random Haar rank R_i, amplitude rho_i, and beta_{ij}=M_iQ_{ij}+(1-M_i)R_i/D bounded by b_*, the random weighted budget W=max_j sum_i beta_{ij}rho_i^2 controls the same signed-core failure. Scalar Bernstein gives mean mu=max_j sum_i E(beta_{ij}rho_i^2), variance proxy sigma^2=max_j sum_i E(beta_{ij}^2rho_i^2), and tail P(E)+D exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+b_*u/3))}+2D exp{-s^2/(2(mu+u+s/3))}. This packages sparse coordinate and random Haar-subspace matrix noise in one random menu on top of algebraic, finite-lift, Toeplitz/Hankel, and other already-signed cores.

CURRENT paper12 localized random-amplitude menu array addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-localized-random-amplitude-menu-borel turns the finite-row random-amplitude localized matrix-noise tails into an almost-sure triangular-array theorem. It applies uniformly to deterministic/random coordinate profiles, fixed block decompositions, fixed or random Haar ranks, conditionally isotropic or spectrally dominated projection laws, and the mixed coordinate/Haar menu. For row parameters N_m (budget-union count), mu_m, sigma_m^2, b_m and deterministic u_m,s_m, summability of P(E_m)+N_m exp{-u_m^2/(2(sigma_m^2+b_m u_m/3))}+2D_m exp{-s_m^2/(2(mu_m+u_m+s_m/3))} gives disc(X_m)<=K_m sqrt(n_m)+s_m eventually almost surely. The profile form gives eventual (K+L)sqrt(n_m) when mu_m+u_m<=Q n_m/log(eD_m), the scalar Bernstein budget exceptions are summable, and log(eD_m)<=B sqrt(n_m). A half-budget schedule with mu_m<=Q n_m/(2log(eD_m)), sigma_m^2<=S n_m/log(eD_m), b_m<=b, N_m<=(eD_m)^Gamma, and n_m>=a log^2(eD_m) is summable along all integer dimensions if Q^2 a/(8S+4bQ/3)>Gamma+1 and along sparse stretched-exponential schedules if the same quantity exceeds Gamma.

CURRENT paper12 homogeneous localized random-amplitude schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-homogeneous-localized-random-amplitude-schedules extracts the concrete energy rule from the random-amplitude localized menu. For homogeneous coordinate, random-coordinate-profile, fixed block, fixed/random Haar, isotropic/dominated projection, and mixed coordinate/Haar rows, write H_m=sum_i E rho_{m,i}^2, let beta_m bound the coordinate inclusion probability, block inclusion probability, rank fraction/intensity, or mixed beta_{m,ij}, and let N_m be the budget union count. Then P{disc>K_m sqrt(n_m)+s_m}<=P(E_m)+N_m exp{-3H_m/8}+2D_m exp{-s_m^2/(2(2 beta_m H_m+s_m/3))}. Thus summability gives eventual almost-sure core signings. In the profile form, if 2 beta_m H_m<=Q n_m/log(eD_m), H_m>=R log(eD_m), N_m<=(eD_m)^Gamma, and n_m>=a log^2(eD_m), then all integer dimensions are covered when R>8(Gamma+1)/3, while sparse stretched-exponential dimensions are covered when R>8Gamma/3. This separates the budget scale beta H from the concentration-energy scale H.

CURRENT paper12 weighted random-support schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-weighted-support-all-dim-schedules turns the existing weighted coordinate-support and random-subspace Bernstein tails into explicit Borel-Cantelli dimension schedules. Under the half-budget condition max_j sum_i rho_{m,i}^2 p_{m,ij} <= C_0 n_m/(2 log(eD_m)) for coordinate supports, or ||sum_i rho_{m,i}^2 E P_{m,i}||op <= C_0 n_m/(2 log(eD_m)) for random subspaces, the row failure is at most D_m exp{-3 C_0 n_m/(16 log(eD_m))}. Hence the Matrix Spencer O(sqrt n_m) bound holds eventually almost surely along all integer dimensions from n_D >= a log^2(eD), a>32/(3C_0), and along sparse stretched-exponential dimension schedules from a>16/(3C_0). Uniform fixed-size coordinate supports and Haar rank-r_i projections reduce to D_m^{-1} sum_i rho_{m,i}^2 r_{m,i} <= C_0 n_m/(2 log(eD_m)).

CURRENT paper12 sampled weighted-support certificate addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-weighted-support-search-all-dim-schedules combines the explicit weighted random-support half-budget schedules with sampled sign certificates. Under the same coordinate-support or random-subspace half-budget hypotheses as cor:random-weighted-support-all-dim-schedules, independent sign batches satisfy P{all M_m sampled signs miss the C(C_0) sqrt(n_m) certificate} <= D_m exp{-3 C_0 n_m/(16 log(eD_m))}+2^{-M_m}. Thus eventual sampled certificates hold along all integer dimensions when n_D>=a log^2(eD), a>32/(3C_0), and M_D>=(1+delta)log_2(eD), and along sparse stretched-exponential schedules when a>16/(3C_0) and M_m>=(1+delta)log_2(e+m). This turns the weighted coordinate/Haar/subspace random-support existence theorem into an eventual randomized certificate with logarithmically many sign trials per row.

CURRENT paper12 weighted random-support growth schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-weighted-support-growth-schedules gives the arbitrary-triangular-array growth rule behind the all-integer and sparse weighted-support schedules. In the same coordinate-support or random-subspace half-budget setting, if ell_m=log(eD_m), L_m=log(e+m), and n_m >= (16/(3C_0)) ell_m(ell_m+(1+gamma)L_m) eventually, then D_m exp{-3 C_0 n_m/(16 ell_m)} <= e^{-1}(e+m)^{-(1+gamma)}, so the Matrix Spencer O(sqrt n_m) signing holds eventually almost surely. With sampled sign batches, M_m >= (1+delta)log_2(e+m) makes the additional 2^{-M_m} search failure summable, giving eventual sampled certificates for arbitrary dimension growth schedules.

CURRENT paper12 random-amplitude weighted-support addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-amplitude-weighted-support-bernstein removes the deterministic-amplitude restriction from the weighted coordinate-support and random-subspace Bernstein ensembles. For independent pairs (rho_i,S_i) with P{j in S_i | rho_i}<=p_ij, scalar Bernstein controls max_j sum_i rho_i^2 1_{j in S_i} with mean budget max_j sum_i p_ij E rho_i^2 and variance proxy max_j sum_i p_ij E rho_i^4. For independent pairs (rho_i,P_i), matrix Bernstein controls ||sum_i rho_i^2 P_i||op with budget ||sum_i E(rho_i^2 P_i)||op. Under either half-budget condition the same row failure D exp{-3 C_0 n/(16 log(eD))} holds, so all Borel-Cantelli, all-dimension, growth-rate, and sampled-certificate schedules carry over. Uniform random support sizes or Haar random ranks use budget D^{-1}sum_i E(rho_i^2 R_i).

Thus all-integer dimensions need n_D>a log^2(eD), a>32/(3C_0), sparse stretched-exponential schedules need a>16/(3C_0), arbitrary growth needs n_m >= (16/(3C_0)) log(eD_m)(log(eD_m)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)), and sampled certificates need only the additional 2^{-M_m} summability. CURRENT paper12 random-amplitude block-support addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-amplitude-block-support-bernstein and cor:random-amplitude-block-support-schedules extend the weighted-support route from coordinate supports to arbitrary fixed orthogonal block decompositions. For independent pairs (rho_i,T_i) selecting block sets with P{alpha in T_i | rho_i}<=q_{i alpha}, scalar Bernstein controls max_alpha sum_i rho_i^2 1_{alpha in T_i} with mean budget max_alpha sum_i q_{i alpha} E rho_i^2 and variance proxy max_alpha sum_i q_{i alpha} E rho_i^4. Under the half-budget condition max_alpha sum_i q_{i alpha} E rho_i^2 <= C_0 n/(2 log(eD)), the failure is B exp{-3 C_0 n/(16 log(eD))}, paying the structural block count B rather than ambient D. The schedule theorem gives summability with B_m in the row tail; if B_m<=(eD_m)^Gamma, all-integer dimensions need a>16(Gamma+1)/(3C_0), sparse stretched-exponential dimensions need a>16 Gamma/(3C_0), and arbitrary schedules need n_m >= (16/(3C_0)) log(eD_m)(log(eB_m)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)). Sampled certificates again add only 2^{-M_m}. Uniform random block-set sizes use budget B_m^{-1}sum_i E(rho_{m,i}^2 R_{m,i}).

CURRENT paper12 random-profile weighted-support addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:random-profile-weighted-support-bernstein removes the deterministic inclusion-profile restriction from the direct weighted-support route. For independent triples (rho_i,Q_i,S_i) with conditional coordinate inclusion P{j in S_i | rho_i,Q_i}<=Q_{ij}, the row tail uses mean max_j sum_i E(rho_i^2 Q_{ij}) and variance max_j sum_i E(rho_i^4 Q_{ij}) with union factor D. For fixed orthogonal block decompositions and independent triples (rho_i,Q_i,T_i), the analogous block tail uses max_alpha sum_i E(rho_i^2 Q_{i alpha}) and union factor B. Under the half-budget condition the row failure is N exp{-3 C_0 n/(16 log(eD))}, N=D or B, and the all-dimension, sparse, arbitrary-growth, and sampled-certificate schedules from the random-amplitude support and block-count corollaries carry over. Uniform random coordinate-support or block-support sizes R_i now rigorously use budgets D^{-1}sum_i E(rho_i^2 R_i) and B^{-1}sum_i E(rho_i^2 R_i), even when R_i and rho_i are correlated.

If mu_m+t_m<=C_0 n_m/log(eD_m) and sum_m N_m exp{-t_m^2/(2(sigma_m^2+t_m/3))}<infty, then the Matrix Spencer O(sqrt n_m) signing holds eventually almost surely. CURRENT paper12 predictable-profile support addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-profile-weighted-support-freedman removes the independence-across-i requirement from the direct random-profile support route. For coordinate supports, rho_i and Q_ij are F_{i-1}-measurable, S_i is F_i-measurable, and P{j in S_i | F_{i-1}}<=Q_ij; for fixed block decompositions the same holds with block profiles Q_{i alpha}. Let U=max_j sum_i rho_i^2 Q_ij (or max_alpha sum_i rho_i^2 Q_{i alpha}) and V=max_j sum_i rho_i^4 Q_ij (or block max). If mu+t<=C_0 n/log(eD), then the row failure is P{U>mu or V>sigma^2}+N exp{-t^2/(2(sigma^2+t/3))}, N=D or B. Pathwise half-budget U<=C_0 n/(2log(eD)) gives N exp{-3C_0 n/(16log(eD))}; sampled sign certificates add only 2^{-M}; predictable uniform support/block sizes use realized budgets D^{-1}sum_i rho_i^2 R_i or B^{-1}sum_i rho_i^2 R_i. Triangular arrays follow by summability.

CURRENT paper12 predictable-profile schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-profile-weighted-support-schedules spells out the Borel-Cantelli schedules for online predictable coordinate/block support sampling. The structural tail is summable under arbitrary growth n_m >= (16/(3C_0)) log(eD_m)(log(eN_m)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)); if N_m<=(eD_m)^Gamma, the all-integer threshold is a>16(Gamma+1)/(3C_0) and the sparse stretched-exponential threshold is a>16Gamma/(3C_0), up to the summable predictable-budget exceptions. CURRENT paper12 predictable-profile finite-menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-profile-menu-schedules upgrades the predictable-profile support schedule theorem to finite menus of online coordinate/block support models in the same ambient dimension. If sum_m sum_h P{U_{m,h}>C_0 n_m/(2log(eD_m))}<infty and sum_m L_m exp{-3C_0 n_m/(16log(eD_m))}<infty with L_m=sum_h N_{m,h}, then all menu elements simultaneously have O(sqrt n_m) Matrix Spencer signings eventually almost surely. Sampled certificates add sum_h 2^{-M_{m,h}}, or |H_m|2^{-M_m} for a common sign batch. The arbitrary-growth, all-integer, and sparse thresholds are the predictable-profile schedules with N_m replaced by L_m and with the budget exceptions summed over the menu; predictable uniform coordinate/block sizes use the corresponding D_m^{-1}sum_i rho^2R or B_{m,h}^{-1}sum_i rho^2R exceptions.

CURRENT paper12 adaptive deterministic-envelope countable-exhaustion addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:adaptive-envelope-countable-exhaustion-schedules extends the deterministic-envelope finite-menu schedules to a countable library by controlling a deterministic active prefix h<=J_m. With L_m^(J)=sum_{h<=J_m}N_{m,h}, if max_{h<=J_m} mu_{m,h}^env<=C_0 n_m/(2log(eD_m)) eventually and sum_m L_m^(J) exp{-3C_0 n_m/(16log(eD_m))}<infty, then all active-prefix envelope models are signed simultaneously eventually almost surely, and every fixed envelope model is signed for all sufficiently large m. Sampled certificates add sum_{h<=J_m}2^{-M_{m,h}}, or J_m 2^{-M_m} for a common sign batch. The arbitrary-growth, all-integer, and sparse thresholds are the finite-menu thresholds with L_m replaced by L_m^(J), and predictably uniform coordinate/block sizes use the active-prefix maximum of D_m^{-1}sum_i a^2r or B_{m,h}^{-1}sum_i a^2r half-budgets. This gives countable coordinate/block envelope libraries with no predictable-budget exception series.

CURRENT paper12 predictable scalar-multiplier finite-menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-scalar-multiplier-menu-schedules upgrades the adaptive scalar-multiplier schedule theorem to finite menus of arbitrary predictable thinning/amplitude streams in the same ambient dimension. For each menu element h, A_{m,i}^{(h)}=theta_{m,i}^{(h)}H_{m,i}^{(h)} with H predictable self-adjoint contractions, |theta|<=1, q=E(theta^2|F_{i-1}), and nu_{m,h}=||sum_i q_{m,i}^{(h)}(H_{m,i}^{(h)})^2||op. If all half-budget events nu_{m,h}<=C_0 n_m/(2log(eD_m)) fail only on summable E_{m,h}, and sum_m |H_m|D_m exp{-3C_0 n_m/(16log(eD_m))}<infty, then every menu element simultaneously has an O(sqrt n_m) Matrix Spencer signing eventually almost surely. Sampled certificates add sum_h 2^{-M_{m,h}}, or |H_m|2^{-M_m} for a common batch. The arbitrary-growth, all-integer, and sparse thresholds are the scalar-multiplier schedules with D_m replaced by |H_m|D_m; the half-budget is automatic when all conditional second moments satisfy q<=C_0/(2log(eD_m)).

CURRENT paper12 predictable scalar-multiplier countable-exhaustion addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:predictable-scalar-multiplier-countable-exhaustion-schedules extends finite scalar-multiplier menus to a countable adaptive stream library by controlling a deterministic active prefix h<=J_m. With L_m^(J)=J_m D_m, if all half-budget events nu_{m,h}<=C_0 n_m/(2log(eD_m)) for h<=J_m fail only on a summable prefix exception series and sum_m J_m D_m exp{-3C_0 n_m/(16log(eD_m))}<infty, then all active-prefix streams are signed simultaneously eventually almost surely, and every fixed stream is signed for all sufficiently large m. Sampled certificates add sum_{h<=J_m}2^{-M_{m,h}}, or J_m2^{-M_m} for a common sign batch. General, all-integer, and sparse schedules are the finite-menu thresholds with |H_m|D_m replaced by J_mD_m; the half-budget is automatic on the active prefix when all conditional second moments satisfy q<=C_0/(2log(eD_m)).

CURRENT paper12 signed-core matrix martingale-noise countable-exhaustion addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-matrix-martingale-noise-countable-exhaustion upgrades the signed-core matrix martingale-noise Borel theorem from one row family to countable libraries controlled on deterministic active prefixes h<=J_m. For each menu element, X_{m,i}^{(h)}=B_{m,i}^{(h)}+Y_{m,i}^{(h)} has a G_m^{(h)}-measurable K sqrt(n_m) core signing outside E_{m,h}, while Y is a bounded self-adjoint matrix martingale difference with predictable quadratic variation nu_{m,h}. If active-prefix variance budgets obey nu_{m,h}<=Q n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable F_{m,h}, if sum_m J_m(eD_m)^(-A)<infty, and if log(eD_m)<=B_0 sqrt(n_m), then every active-prefix core-plus-noise model is signed simultaneously eventually almost surely with threshold (K+L)sqrt(n_m) for L^2/(2(Q+LB_0/3))>=A+1; every fixed model in the countable library is eventually covered. All-integer dimensions with J_D<=(eD)^Gamma need A>Gamma+1 and n_D>=a log^2(eD), while sparse stretched-exponential dimensions need A>Gamma.

CURRENT paper12 signed-core sampled localized matrix-noise countable-exhaustion addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-sampled-localized-matrix-noise-countable-exhaustion specializes the signed-core matrix martingale countable-library theorem to checkable localized budgets. Each active-prefix model h<=J_m has X_{m,i}^{(h)}=B_{m,i}^{(h)}+Y_{m,i}^{(h)}, a G_m^{(h)}-measurable K sqrt(n_m) core signing outside E_{m,h}, and sampled localized martingale noise Y=PYP with predictable amplitude rho. The active-prefix budget is mu_{m,h}=||sum_i rho_{m,i,h}^2 E(P_{m,i,h}|F_{m,i-1,h})||op, or the dominating coordinate occupancy max_j sum_i rho^2 q_{ij}, or the conditional Haar average-rank sum_i rho^2 r_i/D_m. If these budgets are <=Q n_m/log(eD_m) outside summable F_{m,h}, if sum_m J_m(eD_m)^(-A)<infty, and if log(eD_m)<=B_0 sqrt(n_m), then every active-prefix localized core-plus-noise model is signed simultaneously eventually almost surely with threshold (K+L)sqrt(n_m), and every fixed model in the countable localized library is eventually covered. All-integer dimensions with J_D<=(eD)^Gamma need A>Gamma+1 and n_D>=a log^2(eD), while sparse stretched-exponential dimensions need A>Gamma.

CURRENT paper12 random-amplitude localized menu countable-exhaustion addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-countable-localized-random-amplitude-menu-borel extends the signed-core localized random-amplitude menu Borel theorem from one row family to countable libraries controlled on deterministic active prefixes h<=J_m. Each active-prefix model may be coordinate-profile, random-coordinate-profile, fixed block, fixed/random Haar rank, isotropic/dominated projection, or mixed coordinate/Haar noise with independent random amplitudes. For row parameters N_{m,h}, mu_{m,h}, sigma_{m,h}^2, b_{m,h}, summability of the active-prefix core exceptions, scalar Bernstein budget exceptions N_{m,h} exp{-u^2/(2(sigma^2+bu/3))}, and matrix-Freedman tails gives simultaneous active-prefix signings, hence eventual coverage of every fixed localized random-amplitude model. In the half-budget schedule, Lambda_m^(J)=sum_{h<=J_m} N_{m,h} replaces the one-row union count; if Lambda_m^(J)<=(eD_m)^Gamma, n_m>=a log^2(eD_m), and delta=Q^2 a/(8S+4bQ/3), all-integer dimensions need delta>Gamma+1 while sparse stretched-exponential dimensions need delta>Gamma.

CURRENT paper12 homogeneous random-amplitude localized countable-exhaustion addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-countable-homogeneous-localized-random-amplitude-schedules specializes the countable random-amplitude localized menu theorem to homogeneous coordinate-profile, random-coordinate-profile, fixed block, fixed/random Haar rank, isotropic/dominated projection, and mixed coordinate/Haar models. For active prefixes h<=J_m it uses the simple energy parameters H_{m,h}=sum_i E rho_{m,i,h}^2, beta_{m,h}, and N_{m,h}; summability of core exceptions, sum_h N_{m,h} exp{-3H_{m,h}/8}, and the matrix-Freedman row tails gives simultaneous active-prefix signings and eventual coverage of each fixed homogeneous localized model. In the explicit schedule, Lambda_m^(J)=sum_{h<=J_m}N_{m,h} replaces the one-model union count; active-prefix conditions 2 beta_{m,h}H_{m,h}<=Q n_m/log(eD_m), H_{m,h}>=R log(eD_m), and Lambda_m^(J)<=(eD_m)^Gamma give all-integer coverage when R>8(Gamma+1)/3 and sparse stretched-exponential coverage when R>8Gamma/3.

CURRENT paper12 common-sign localized random-amplitude countable-exhaustion addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:core-plus-common-sign-countable-localized-random-amplitude-menu-borel strengthens the countable random-amplitude localized menu theorem when the active-prefix cores share one environment-measurable signing. If a single eps_m^* signs every core B_{m}^{(h)} for h<=J_m at K_{m,h}sqrt(n_m), then the same eps_m^* signs every perturbed row X_{m}^{(h)}=B_{m}^{(h)}+Y_{m}^{(h)} after the usual active-prefix union over the random-budget Bernstein terms and matrix-Freedman tails. The profile and half-budget schedules keep the same costs as the existential countable theorem: Lambda_m^(J)=sum_{h<=J_m}N_{m,h}, all-integer delta>Gamma+1, and sparse delta>Gamma. Thus countable localized random-amplitude robustness can be certified by one row signing whenever the deterministic/algebraic core certificate is itself common across the active prefix.

CURRENT paper12 finite-lift common-menu addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-common-menu direct-sums finite-lift algebras across a finite menu and applies the finite block-lift Matrix Spencer theorem once. CURRENT paper12 finite-lift core operator-tail schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-operator-tail-menu-schedules makes the full operator-tail menu theorem explicit. With L_m^ot=J_m(n_m+1), arbitrary dimension growth is handled by taking tau_m^2 >= log(2L_m^ot)+(1+gamma)log(e+m), leaving only the summable lift-failure exceptions. If L_m^ot<=(eD_m)^Gamma, then the all-integer schedule D=2,3,... is summable for tau_D^2=Rlog(eD) with R>Gamma+1, while sparse stretched-exponential dimension schedules need only R>Gamma. Thus finite-lift core libraries plus countable active-prefix GOE/GUE/Wigner-type/correlated-Gaussian operator-tail noise have explicit scaling margins parallel to the localized random-amplitude schedules.

CURRENT paper12 row-variance entry-envelope addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-entrywise-row-variance-menu removes the remaining vector supremum from the entrywise sub-Gaussian theorem in sparse/banded settings. If each row of the entry-proxy variance matrix has alpha_p^2+2 sum_{q ne p} beta_pq^2 <= R_{m,h}^2, then Q_{m,h}=R_{m,h} is admissible in cor:finite-lift-core-entrywise-subgaussian-menu, so all active-prefix finite-lift Borel schedules hold after scaling by C_ent R_{m,h}(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m). In particular graph-supported profiles of max degree Delta_{m,h} have scale (alpha_{m,h}^2+2 Delta_{m,h} beta_{m,h}^2)^{1/2}(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m), which keeps normalized sparse/banded Wigner-type noise at the natural O(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m) scale.

CURRENT paper12 random graph-mask entry schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-random-graph-mask-entry-schedules extends the row-variance finite-lift menu theorem from deterministic sparse/banded supports to random support masks. For independent masks it conditions on the random graph, pays a scalar Bernstein max-degree exception sum_h n_m D_m exp{-u_{m,h}^2/(2(lambda_{m,h}+u_{m,h}/3))}, and then applies the same entrywise scaling C_ent(a_{m,h}^2+2b_{m,h}^2)^{1/2}(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m) with off-diagonal proxy b_{m,h}/sqrt(max(1,lambda_{m,h}+u_{m,h})). Inhomogeneous Erdos--Renyi masks with expected degree dominating the menu union logarithm therefore have expected-degree normalization up to constants. Dependent bounded-degree support laws, including random regular masks and random matchings, skip the Bernstein exception by conditioning on their degree bound.

CURRENT paper12 bounded weighted random-mask incidence schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-bounded-weighted-random-mask-incidence-schedules turns the weighted random-mask Bernstein schedule into a normalized-mean-row-variance test. If deterministic parameters A, rho, omega satisfy a^2<=A^2, mu<=rho, sigma^2<=omega rho, and b<=omega, then taking t=rho gives scale C_ent(A^2+2rho)^{1/2}(sqrt(D_m)+tau_m) and support exponent Phi>=3rho/(8omega). Thus independent or environment-predictable inhomogeneous Wigner/GOE/GUE-type masks are covered whenever Lambda_m^mask=min_h rho_{m,h}/omega_{m,h} >= (8/3)(log(eL_m^var)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)) and tau_m^2 >= log(2L_m^ot)+(1+gamma)log(e+m). If max{L_m^var,L_m^ot}<=(eD_m)^Gamma, all integer dimensions need R>8(Gamma+1)/3 and S>Gamma+1, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need R>8Gamma/3 and S>Gamma. The predictable-profile variant follows by replacing the deterministic profile by barred envelopes and adding the summable F_m^pr exception.

CURRENT paper12 dependent weighted-budget mask addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-dependent-weighted-budget-masks removes support independence from the finite-lift entrywise random-mask route once a realized weighted row-budget event is controlled. CURRENT paper12 predictable block-support profile schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-predictable-block-support-profile-schedules makes the heterogeneous predictable support theorem a direct Borel-Cantelli schedule. If L_m^pbs<=(eD_m)^Gamma, all integer dimensions need Theta_D,tau_D^2 >= R,S log(eD) with R,S>Gamma+1, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need only R,S>Gamma. If the predictable amplitudes obey c_{m,h,a}<=a_{m,h}, the row-block inclusion masses max_{i,beta} sum_{a:i(a)=i} p_{m,h,a,beta} are at most lambda_{m,h}^{ind}, the total block masses max_beta sum_a p_{m,h,a,beta} are at most lambda_{m,h}^{tot}, and the half-budget bounds 2a_{m,h}^2 lambda_ind<=q^2 and 2a_{m,h}^2 lambda_tot<=nq^2 hold, then scalar Freedman gives exponent 3lambda/8. Thus the finite-lift active-prefix conclusions hold at scale C_ms q_{m,h}(sqrt(log(e kappa_{m,h}))+tau_m) whenever lambda_m >= (8/3)(log(eL_m^pinh)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)) and tau_m^2 >= log(2J_m(n_m+1))+(1+gamma)log(e+m). If L_m^pinh<=(eD_m)^Gamma, all integer dimensions need R>8(Gamma+1)/3 and S>Gamma+1, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need R>8Gamma/3 and S>Gamma. CURRENT paper12 weighted block-load profile schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-weighted-block-load-profile-schedules gives one active-prefix Borel-Cantelli schedule for both random weighted block-load atomic matrix-series noise and predictable weighted block-load atomic matrix-series noise. With Psi_m the minimum of the individual and total Bernstein/Freedman exponents over h<=J_m and L_m^wbl=(n_m+1)(J_m+sum_h |B_{m,h}|), the finite-lift active-prefix conclusions hold whenever the half-budget inequalities hold, sum_m P(F_m^lift)<infty, Psi_m >= log(eL_m^wbl)+(1+gamma)log(e+m), and tau_m^2 >= log(2L_m^wbl)+(1+gamma)log(e+m). If L_m^wbl <= (eD_m)^Gamma, all integer dimensions need Psi_D,tau_D^2 >= R,S times log(eD) with R,S>Gamma+1, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need only R,S>Gamma. Thus random amplitudes/intensities, adaptive atom sampling, online weighted dictionaries, and without-replacement block exposure share the same active-prefix schedule once their scalar Bernstein or Freedman exponents dominate the active block count.

CURRENT paper12 bounded weighted block-load incidence schedule addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-lift-core-bounded-weighted-block-load-incidence-schedules turns the weighted block-load profile schedule into a checkable normalized-mean-load test. If the deterministic envelopes satisfy mu_ind<=rho_ind, sigma_ind^2<=omega_bd rho_ind, omega<=omega_bd, mu_tot<=rho_tot, sigma_tot^2<=omega_bd rho_tot, and the half-budget bounds 2rho_ind<=q^2 and 2rho_tot<=nq^2 hold, then choosing slacks u_ind=rho_ind and u_tot=rho_tot gives Bernstein/Freedman exponent at least 3rho/(8omega_bd). Thus random or predictable weighted block-atomic perturbations are absorbed at scale C_ms q_{m,h}(sqrt(log(e kappa_{m,h}))+tau_m) whenever Lambda_m^bw=min_h min{rho_ind/omega_bd,rho_tot/omega_bd} >= (8/3)(log(eL_m^bw)+(1+gamma)log(e+m)) and tau_m^2 >= log(2L_m^bw)+(1+gamma)log(e+m), with L_m^bw=(n_m+1)(J_m+sum_h |B_{m,h}|). If L_m^bw<=(eD_m)^Gamma, all integer dimensions need R>8(Gamma+1)/3 and S>Gamma+1, while sparse stretched-exponential schedules need R>8Gamma/3 and S>Gamma. This covers bounded random intensities, bounded compound-thinned atom fields, adaptive weighted dictionaries, and without-replacement weighted exposure using only normalized mean incident loads.
