CURRENT paper15 atlas addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:finite-atlas records the useful middle ground between a single fixed curved configuration and the open curvature-band-uniform family theorem. Any finite atlas of fixed admissible curved configurations has a uniform slab lower bound and the same UDC interpolation law, with constants allowed to depend on that finite atlas; this follows by taking the minimum resolved Gram and slice-curvature constants over the finite menu. New cor:compact-atlas extends the same compactness package to compact parameter atlases whenever the resolved Gram and slice-curvature constants vary continuously and stay positive on the parameter space. New cor:exhaustive-atlas extends this to sigma-compact/exhaustive resolved atlases: each compact stage has uniform constants, and the whole atlas is uniform exactly when the resolved constants have a positive global floor.
CURRENT paper15 resolved-neighborhood addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:resolved-neighborhood makes resolved curved geometry open in C^2-continuous parametrized families. If one configuration is (Gamma_0,c_0)-resolved, then every nearby configuration is (Gamma_0/2,c_0/2)-resolved after a harmless slice-depth shrink, so the slab lower bound and UDC interpolation law are uniform on a genuine C^2 neighborhood. New cor:c2-compact-atlas removes the need to preselect continuous global resolved-constant functions on compact atlases: if a compact C^2-continuous atlas has each member individually resolved, finitely many resolved neighborhoods give one uniform slab constant and the uniform interpolation law. This pushes the residual paper15 open problem from compact resolved families to noncompact/degenerating curvature-band families and shrinking multi-tangent regimes.
CURRENT paper15 nonobtuse one-cut addendum (local, not yet pushed): new cor:onecut-nonobtuse records the concrete specialization of the curvature-dominated one-cut theorem. For k=1, if every active contact satisfies <nu_K,n_1><=0, then the dominance margin is automatically at least c_K, so the slab lower bound and the UDC interpolation law are uniform over the whole nonobtuse-contact class with constants depending only on n and the structure constants.

proof conflates ν/m-precision m^{-1/10} with ν-precision). NEW THEOREM (parity obstruction): ν_m(D) ≡
1{G(1)G(−1)<0} mod 2 (deterministic, multiplicity-proof: conjugate pairs + sign-change identity), and
(G(1),G(−1))/√m → independent Gaussians (Cov = Σ(−1)^j = O(1)) ⇒ P(ν = k) ≤ 1/2 + o(1) ∀k ⇒ P(stable)
≤ 1/2 + o(1) for EVERY band — refutes "well-conditioned whp" for balanced bands; covers Rademacher
(needs only iid, mean 0, var 1, 3rd moment; atom-bound via Berry–Esseen). + corrected positive theorem:

clearance + Böttcher–Grudsky Thm 4.1). + open problem: LLT for ν_m(D), Var(ν_m(D)) order (conjecture
σ ≍ √m via conjugate-pair Bernoulli heuristic; even Gaussian case open). Bonus stated: disk zero-counts

t₀ ∈ {1/i}-menu (for ψ(t₀)=0), window-visits with margin 1/i (open!)}: OPEN (∃ over countable ∪ of open) +
DENSE (witnesses from Shmalo's dense exact theorem, strict margins). ∩ over (k,j,N₀,r,window-family) =
residual; limit: nested disjoint cells diam→0 ⇒ Cantor set; diagonal subsequence ⇒ every limit pair DC1
(ψ* ≡ 1 via ∀j i.o.; ψ(t₀)=0 via liminf), windowed (margin-visits stabilized per nested level), all tails.
+ h(tail_r g) = ∞ residually: ∀c∀N₀∃N≥N₀∃ε: s_{r,N}(ε) > e^{cN} — open-dense (no scale-normalization ⇒
no const-ball obstruction unlike fixed-ε). LEAVES OPEN: semifactors + sharp spectrum (state explicitly).
Supersedes paper-1's burst-only result — frame honestly as strengthening; cite paper 1 as companion.
Bookkeeping details: nested cells fix margin-levels for times ≤ N_k; one-sided strict inequalities for
limit-transfer; separation ⇒ distinctness ⇒ Cantor. Output: Downloads\Residual_DC1_Hilbert_v1.pdf.
AVOID: MP-pruning + ReLU-collapse lanes. Other deferred: Var(ν_m(D)) Gaussian; paper-2 gap; paper-4 strata.

## Problem #1 (CURRENT): Residual Hilbert-cube theorem
Source: Shmalo, "Strict cubical horseshoes and all-tail full complexity in non-autonomous dynamics"
(AIMS Math submission 2026-05-29; local text: ~\math_research\shs6_text.txt, 40pp).
Section 14 states the open problem verbatim:
  "The Hilbert-cube conclusion is dense but is not asserted to be open ... A residual Hilbert-cube
   theorem with coherent all-tail symbolic codings would require an additional Baire-fusion mechanism
   beyond the exact finite-support implantations proved here."
TARGET THEOREM: In F(Q∞)=C(Q∞,Q∞)^N with uniform-in-time metric D, for every sequence of dense open
windows W_n, the set of g with (all tails r): tail symbolic semifactor onto full variable-alphabet shift,
windowed DC1 scrambled set of cardinality c, h(tail_r g)=∞, H_{g,r}(ε) ≥ q log(1/ε) − C_{g,q} for every
finite q, hence mdim_M = ∞, IS RESIDUAL (comeager). Coherent codings: one infinite scheme gives all tails.

## VERDICT after full design exploration (2026-06-11)
FULL residual theorem (his exact conclusions on a comeager set) BLOCKED by a real quantitative wall:
depth-ℓ margin conditions have D-stability ~θ2^{-ℓ}, repair cost ~2^{-ℓ}; in any Baire/BM-game scheme the
adversary squeezes budgets below the repair cost of the band (log(θ/r_k), log(1/η_{k+1})] ⇒ unbounded-depth
margins unmaintainable. Also: finite-depth schemes cannot certify infinite-horizon orbits (dependency-slack
accumulates; no Lipschitz in C0) ⇒ entropy/DC1/semifactor (all infinite-horizon) not extractable from
finite-depth genericity. PIVOT: paper ships the three fully-proved results T1/T2/T3 below — finite-depth
residual fusion + one-step exact covering + empty-interior obstruction (squeeze map Φ_ε kills exact schemes
within any δ). This resolves the open-vs-dense half of §14 definitively and gives the maximal margin-stable
genericity. Full conclusions-residuality remains open (state honestly in the paper).

## Paper 1 contents (FINAL)
T1: U_N(W•) (all-time strict depth-N cylinder-brick schemes, margin θ, windows W) is open and dense in
    (F(Q∞),D); S := ∩_N U_N(W•) residual. Openness radius: θ·2^{-N}. Density: port Shmalo §10–12 implantation
    with overshoot ±θ in coords ≤ N (cost ≤ 3α + 2^{-K}).
T2: g ∈ S ⇒ ∀N ∀n,i and ∀ target t in next-brick ≤N-intervals: ∃x ∈ B̂_{n,i}: π_{≤N} g_n(x) = t (Poincaré–
    Miranda on the N-section). One-step exact ≤N-covering at every depth.
T3: EX (Def 12.1 exact windowed Hilbert horseshoes) has EMPTY INTERIOR: Φ_ε(x)_j := clip of x_j to
    [ε_j, 1−ε_j] with ε_j = 1/8 for j > L(ε), ρ(Φ_ε, id) ≤ 2^{-L} < ε; h := (Φ_ε∘g_n)_n misses deep-coordinate
    extremes ⇒ h_n(B) ⊉ J_{n+1} for ANY (α,K)-box (free coords need full [0,1]) ⇒ no exact scheme. Dense ∧
    empty interior. Corollary: meager-or-not unresolved, but openness impossible — confirms §14 necessity.

## Proof architecture (history of the design exploration — kept for the record)
DISCARDED: componentwise composite-Miranda chaining (signs fail off zero-set: intermediate points leave
branch boxes); exact-covering-with-margin in all coords (not open: high-coord weights 2^{-j}); whole-box
uniform crossing conditions (density pays modulus-of-continuity of f — fatal for sup_n); König extraction
from finite dyadic menus (α-pinning fails: menus must allow arbitrarily small α ⇒ infinite branching).
ADOPTED: **Banach–Mazur game fusion (Oxtoby)**:
1. Strict scheme stages: fully-restricted branch "bricks" B_{n,i}=∏_j I_j^{n,i} (ALL coords restricted;
   I_j ⊂ (0,1), default [1/4,3/4] for j>R_n; empty interior is fine — windows/gluing/diam all still work).
   Stage-k data: times n ≤ n_k, image-coords ℓ ≤ N_k, margins θ_k, width α (II picks at stage 1).
   Conditions (open!): for n < n_k, each i, each ℓ ≤ N_k:
     π_ℓ g_n(x) ≤ m⁻_ℓ(n+1) − θ_k on {x ∈ B_{n,i}: x_ℓ = min I_ℓ},
     π_ℓ g_n(x) ≥ m⁺_ℓ(n+1) + θ_k on {x ∈ B_{n,i}: x_ℓ = max I_ℓ},
   where m∓_ℓ(n+1) = min/max over next-time branch bricks of their ℓ-interval endpoints (all ⊂ (0,1) so
   overshoot room exists). Stability radius: δ < θ·2^{-N_k} (coordinate ℓ ≤ N_k moves ≤ 2^ℓ δ).
2. Extension lemma: a map realizing stage k extends to stage k+1 by perturbation
   ≤ 2^{-N_k} (new high coords cheap!) + fresh implantation at new times n_k<n≤n_{k+1} (cost ~ 3α, but α
   was fixed tiny at stage 1; new-time implant = Shmalo Lemma 11.3-style coordinatewise q_i with θ-overshoot,
   glued by Urysohn on small f-adapted neighborhoods — NO modulus problem since conditions live on bricks only).
3. Banach–Mazur game G**(A): II's strategy: in I's open V'_k pick center f, implant stage-k extension of
   II's own running scheme with double margins, play V_{k+1} = small ball inside the margin-stability radius
   ∩ V'_k, diam → 0. Limit g* realizes ALL stages. By Oxtoby/Kechris 8.33 (II wins ⇒ A comeager): A residual.
4. Limit covering: g* has strict crossing in EVERY coordinate ℓ (stage providing ℓ → ∞) on every brick ⇒
   for any y ∈ B_{n+1,i'} (all coords!): finite-dim Poincaré–Miranda per stage gives x_k ∈ B_{n,i} with
   π_{≤N_k} g_n(x_k) = π_{≤N_k} y; compactness limit x: g_n(x) = y EXACTLY. So g_n(B_{n,i}) ⊇ ∪_{i'} B_{n+1,i'}
   ⇒ Shmalo's backward-induction coding (his Lemma 13.1/8.1) runs VERBATIM on the residual set.
5. Coding/DC1/entropy: port his §13 with bricks: itinerary uniqueness via q_n-coordinate separation
   c_{α,q_n}M_n^{-1/q_n}; DC1 via C_a/D_s blocks + diam γ_n; entropy via E_{q,m} blocks: H ≥ q log(1/ε) − C;
   schedule = his §12 (Λ∞ labels, S_k/L_k → 0). Windows: bricks ⊂ W_n by basic-open-set + shrink.
NOTE Baire-Mazur subtlety: II must also handle I's arbitrary first move (α chosen then). The conclusions-set
A must be shown ⊇ {g realizing some infinite scheme}; strategy forces g* ∈ A. Cite Oxtoby 1957 / Kechris 8.33.

## (archived) Problem #3 design
Source: arXiv:2410.10288 p.7: "Note that Lemma 2 does not say F_DC1(I) is not residual. This problem stays
open." (their full text at br_text.txt). Their space: F_p(I) = convergent nads of SURJECTIVE interval maps,
ρ_F = sup_n ρ(f_n,g_n); for ρ = ρ_sup: convergent = uniformly convergent; F_p complete (limit-swap proof).
RESOLUTION (paper3/dc1_generic.tex → Downloads\DC1_Generic_Convergent_v1.pdf):
 KEY: F_DC1 not open (their Lemma 2) BUT contains the open dense subset λ^{-1}(O):
 - λ: F_p → C_s(I), system ↦ limit map; 1-Lipschitz (d(f,g) = lim d(f_n,g_n) ≤ ρ_F).
 - O := {g ∈ C_s(I): ∃ disjoint closed intervals A,B, θ>0: g(A)∩g(B) ⊇ θ-nbhd of conv(A∪B)}: OPEN in
   C_s(I) (1-D margin argument: image-interval endpoints move ≤ ρ_sup) and DENSE: zigzag implant near a
   fixed point z of g (surjective ⇒ fixed point); g(J)-hull-covering zigzag on J = [z±η]; perturbation
   ≤ 2ω_g(η)+η; surjectivity preserved (zigzag covers the lost values).
 - Chain: limit ∈ O ⇒ h(limit) ≥ log2 > 0 ⇒ limit DC1 (Schweizer–Smítal: interval PTE ⟺ DC1/2/3) ⇒
   system DC1 (Štefánková DCDS-S 2016 Thm B: uniform convergence inherits DC from limit; BR themselves
   use the chain PTE⇒DC1 via [17]+Ruette 6.27+[4] Lemma 3).
 - λ^{-1}(O) open (continuity) dense (tail-blend: g_n := f_n for n≤N, then blend/replace by g ∈ O near f).
 - ⇒ F_DC1(I) ⊇ open dense ⇒ RESIDUAL. Also h=∞ generic in F_p^{sup} (λ^{-1}(∩_m O_m), m-branch zigzags,
   + Kolyada–Snoha h(sys) ≥ h(limit)) — recovers [4] WITHOUT equicontinuity hypothesis because:
 - PROP (hypothesis collapse): uniformly convergent system ⇒ equicontinuous (ε/3 argument) — so [4]'s
   "uniformly convergent and equicontinuous" = "uniformly convergent".
 - ϱ1-metrics (ρ_int etc.): delineate: F_p not complete, genericity ill-posed without completion; state as
   the remaining open half (their Cantor-negative + Figure-1 PTE-not-DC1 examples live here).
CITATIONS: Schweizer–Smítal TAMS 344 (1994); Štefánková DCDS-S 36(6) 2016 3435-3443 (check exact thm);
Kolyada–Snoha Random Comput Dynam 4 (1996); BSŠ IJBC 28 (2018) 1850102; Kawaguchi PAMS 147 (2019);
Misiurewicz horseshoes (via Ruette Cor 6.27, AMS ULect 67).

## (archived) Problem #2 plan — paper2/udc_whp.tex
PDF #1 SHIPPED: Downloads\Residual_Hilbert_Cube_Fusion_v1.pdf (11pp, 2026-06-11 01:22).
Problem #2 = prove Shmalo's uniform doubling condition (Def 3.3 of arXiv:2210.08415v3, Ann.Math.AI) holds
w.h.p. for iid samples from density ρ∈[ρ_min,ρ_max] on convex body K. Full paper text at doubling_text.txt.
Def: ∃κ>1,δ,σ,β,ℓ: ∀ truncated slabs S (slab {|⟨x−s,u⟩|≤w/2} ∩ k half-spaces from family L): ∀ grid scales
ℓκ^i<β: μ̄(S_{ℓκ^{i+1}}) ≥ min{δ,(1+σ)μ̄(S_{ℓκ^i})}. μ̄ = empirical measure of T.
PROOF PLAN (verified mentally, all cases close):
 A. 1-D dichotomy: ν w/ density ρ·v, v log-concave, R̃=ρmax/ρmin, κ≥4: ν(I_κw)≥min{ν(R)/(4R̃),(1+σ₀)ν(I_w)},
    σ₀=(κ−1)/(2e²R̃)∧3/4. Proof: WLOG right mass M+≥(3/8)ν; mode-cases (a) m*≤c+w/2: log-drop dichotomy over
    extension (drop≥e ⇒ tail≤extension integral [log-concave tail lemma: v(t₀+Δ)≤v(t₀)/e ⇒ ∫_{t₀+Δ}^∞ v ≤
    ∫_{t₀}^{t₀+Δ} v] ⇒ ν(E+)≥M+/(2R̃)≥(3/4)ν(I_w); drop<e ⇒ slope bound ⇒ max_{I_w}v ≤ e·v(c+w/2) ⇒ gain
    ≥((κ−1)/(2e²R̃))ν(I_w)); (b) m*>c+w/2 similar. Saturation harmless (cap ≤ ν(G)).
 B. Population: profile v(t)=vol_{n−1}(G∩{⟨x,u⟩=t}) log-concave (Brunn–Mink); thickness inradius(G)≥r₀ ⇒
    uniform δ₀ := ρ_min ω_n r₀^n/(4R̃); any β (e.g. diam K); σ₀ as in A.
 C. Empirical: VC dim of (k+2)-halfspace intersections ≤ 2(n+1)(k+2)log₂(3(k+2)) [BEHW]; Vapnik relative
    deviations: w.p.≥1−ε ∀C: |μ−μ̄| ≤ 2√(μu_N)+u_N, u_N=(2d log(2eN/d)+log(4/ε))/N; τ_N:=16u_N.
    CONE LEMMA: x∈S_w∩G, G⊇B(z,r₀) ⇒ μ(S_κw∩G) ≥ ρ_min c_n (r₀/D)^{n−1}((κ−1)w/4)^n =: c_* w^n.
    ℓ_N := (16τ_N·16/c_*)^{1/n} ~ (logN/N)^{1/n}. Case analysis: μ̄(S_w)=0 trivial; else cone ⇒ μ(S_κw)≥16τ_N
    ⇒ μ̄(S_κw)≥8τ_N; if μ̄(S_w)≤8τ_N/2 done (σ'=1); else μ(S_w)≥τ_N ⇒ population dichotomy; growth case needs
    (1+σ₀)(3/16)≥2 ⇒ choose κ ≥ 1+14e²R̃ (σ₀≥10). δ' := δ₀/2.
 D. Sharpness: sliver H={x₂≤ε'} in [0,1]^n: slab dir e₂ saturates at mass ε'<δ ⇒ thickness necessary;
    ball extreme-cap gap ⇒ ℓ ≥ cN^{−2/(n+1)} necessary (direction e₁, top-spacing of x₁ order stats).
 Output: Downloads\UDC_High_Probability_v1.pdf. Then problem #3 (Balibrea–Rucká residuality or other).
